\subsection{What a constraint multiplier can and cannot accomplish}

Let \(V\in\mathbb C^{N\times r}\) and
\(W\in\mathbb C^{N\times s}\) have orthonormal columns and suppose
\(U=(V\ W)\) is unitary.  The retained subspace is \(\Ran V\), while
the constraint rows are represented by
\[
 C=W^* .                                                   \tag{10.5}
\]
Write a Hermitian ambient pencil \(M=M^*\) and a multiplier
\(Z\in\mathbb C^{N\times s}\) in these coordinates as
\[
 U^*MU=\begin{pmatrix}A&E\\E^*&D\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad Z=VZ_R+WZ_0 .                                    \tag{10.6}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact cut--cross decomposition of the multiplier]
\label{thm:newv10-multiplier}
With (10.2)--(10.3),
\[
 U^*\bigl(M+ZC+C^*Z^*\bigr)U
 =
 \begin{pmatrix}
 A&E+Z_R\\
 E^*+Z_R^*&D+Z_0+Z_0^*
 \end{pmatrix}.                                          \tag{10.7}
\]
Consequently:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item among multipliers with prescribed constraint-side component
      \(W^*Z=Z_0\), the unique Frobenius-minimum lift is
      \(Z=WZ_0\), equivalently \(Z_R=0\);
\item that minimum-norm lift cancels the cross block only when
      \(E=0\);
\item the cross block is cancelled exactly when \(Z_R=-E\), and this
      choice is unique;
\item there exists a multiplier for which the augmented ambient
      matrix is positive semidefinite if and only if \(A\succeq0\).
\end{enumerate}
Thus multiplier completion is an exact extension mechanism for an
already-positive compression.  By itself it cannot prove the
positivity of the retained block \(A=V^*MV\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Since \(W^*V=0\), \(W^*W=I_s\), and \(C=W^*\), direct block
multiplication gives
\[
 U^*ZCU=
 \begin{pmatrix}0&Z_R\\0&Z_0\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 U^*C^*Z^*U=
 \begin{pmatrix}0&0\\Z_R^*&Z_0^*\end{pmatrix},          \tag{10.8}
\]
which proves (10.4).  Orthogonality of the ranges of \(V\) and
\(W\) gives
\[
 \|Z\|_{mathrm F}^2
 =\|Z_R\|_{\mathrm F}^2+\|Z_0\|_{\mathrm F}^2.           \tag{10.9}
\]
For fixed \(Z_0=W^*Z\), (10.6) is uniquely minimized by \(Z_R=0\),
proving (i); the upper-right block in (10.4) then proves (ii)--(iii).

If the augmented matrix is positive semidefinite, compression by
\(V\) gives \(A\succeq0\).  Conversely, assume \(A\succeq0\), set
\(Z_R=-E\), and choose the Hermitian part of \(Z_0\), for example,
so that
\[
 D+Z_0+Z_0^*=I_s .                                       \tag{10.10}
\]
Then (10.4) is \(\operatorname{diag}(A,I_s)\succeq0\).
This proves (iv) and the final assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Resolution of the V1 multiplier ambiguity]
\label{rem:newv10-loop}
The SM cut/loop warning is visible in (10.4) without analogy.  A
minimum-norm lift fixes the constraint-side or ``cut'' component but
sets \(Z_R=0\), so it cannot remove a nonzero retained--constraint
cross block.  The canonical cross-cancelling value \(Z_R=-E\) does
remove that block, but it leaves \(A\) exactly unchanged.  Hence a
search for an unconstrained nineteen-column completion cannot replace
the proof of the compressed covariant pencil: the load-bearing datum
is still \(A\succeq0\).  This closes the apparent shortcut in V1 and
prevents a circular multiplier proof.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Exact four-thirds certificates on all active circles}

For \(e\in\{\mathrm W,\mathrm E\}\) and
\(\sigma\in\{-1,1\}\), put
\[
 P^{(4/3)}_{e,\sigma}(k)
 :=\frac43H_e(k)+\sigma\nabla_1H_e(k).                   \tag{10.11}
\]
As in V2, fixing \((k_2,k_3,k_4)\) makes (10.8) a Hermitian
degree-one Laurent polynomial in \(z=e^{ik_1}\).  Theorem
\ref{thm:newv2-block} can therefore remove the entire active circle
once an exact noncommutative splitting has been supplied.

\begin{theorem}[Four-thirds transverse-grid active-circle gate]
\label{thm:newv10-strong-circles}
For every transverse anchor
\[
 (k_2,k_3,k_4)\in\{0,\pi/2,\pi,3\pi/2\}^3,
\]
every \(k_1\in\mathbb R\), and both signs \(\sigma\), one has
\[
\begin{aligned}
 P^{(4/3)}_{\mathrm W,\sigma}(k)
 &\succeq
 \frac{51398848997740196963037041}
 {321428572899750000000000000000}\,I_{45},\\
 P^{(4/3)}_{\mathrm E,\sigma}(k)
 &\succeq
 \frac{5100994122399595556463102743}
 {750000002810250000000000000000}\,I_{45}.
\end{aligned}                                             \tag{10.12}
\]
The two exact floors are approximately
\(1.599075294833\times10^{-4}\) and
\(6.801325471048\times10^{-3}\).  The worst transverse anchors are
respectively the quarter-turn unit triples \((2,2,0)\) and
\((2,2,2)\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For each action and each of the \(64\) transverse anchors, form the
exact Laurent pair \(A+zB+\bar zB^*\) for the plus sign.  Starting
from the two Riccati endpoints used in V3, round their fixed interior
combination and the associated approximate eigenbasis to denominator
\(10^{10}\).  Hermitian symmetry and every Laurent adjoint pairing
are then checked exactly.  The resulting \(128\) rational
\(90\times90\) blocks are all positive definite.

Lemma~\ref{lem:newv3-congruence}, using exact rowwise square-root
ceilings, gives the uniform block floors
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \eta_{mathrm W}^{(4/3)}
 &=\frac{51398848997740196963037041}
 {642857145799500000000000000000},\\
 \eta_{mathrm E}^{(4/3)}
 &=\frac{5100994122399595556463102743}
 {1500000005620500000000000000000}.
\end{aligned}                                             \tag{10.13}
\]
The largest certified basis-orthogonality errors are
\[
 \frac{5239}{10^{12}},\qquad
 \frac{1317}{250000000000},                              \tag{10.14}
\]
both strictly below one.  Applying the vector
\(w_z=(u,zu)\) as in (3.12) doubles each block floor, producing
(10.9) uniformly for \(|z|=1\).  Momentum inversion from
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv4-inversion} conjugates the plus certificate at
the inverted anchor to the minus certificate, and the transverse
grid is closed under inversion.

The complete exact recomputation is
\[
\texttt{scripts/verify\_ym\_new\_v10\_four\_thirds\_active\_circles.py},
\]
whose canonical payload SHA--256 is
\[
\texttt{0AEE3F982F465A4D6E1489F0BE213F4DF012F298FB47C1DD668BFD9EEC422206}.
                                                               \tag{10.15}
\]
The Wilson and endpoint block-certificate ledgers have SHA--256
values
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\texttt{9ACA9EB6379AC5F37EBE7685EDFB78B2BEB37242042A92C39C86128BD93B9A80},\\
&\texttt{36A531787EA127DAC17B12DCD2F8D95BE8EEFF45D0D544E484F4FF203BA897BF}.
\end{aligned}                                             \tag{10.16}
\]
All sign decisions in the acceptance stage are rational; the

\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Explicit reserve for the original active circles]
\label{cor:newv10-original-circles}
At the same active circles, the original constant-two pencils obey

 2H_{\mathrm W}+\sigma\nabla_1H_{\mathrm W}

 2H_{\mathrm E}+\sigma\nabla_1H_{\mathrm E}

\]
The floors are approximately \(0.02629324086282\) and

Use the exact identity

 2H_e+\sigma\nabla_1H_e

\begin{assessment}[Progress in V10]
\label{ass:newv10-progress}
The stronger four-thirds pencil has advanced from \(256\) isolated

The V8 failure concerned the constant-two V7 splitting and therefore

If direct interpolation again loses positivity, go upstream to a

\section{V11: exact obstruction to the naive four-thirds recursion}
\label{sec:newv11-obstruction}

\subsection{The proposed moving splitting}

The V10 active-circle theorem provides, for every transverse
quarter-turn point
\[
 q=(q_2,q_3,q_4)\in\{0,1,2,3\}^3,
\]
an exact rational noncommutative splitting \(X_{e,q}^{\#}\).  Each is
constructed from the two Riccati endpoints with the same interior
weight \(13/25\).  The direct analogue of the V6 recursion is the
degree-one tensor field
\[
 \widetilde X_e^{(1)}(\theta_2,\theta_3,\theta_4)
 =
 \sum_{q\in\{0,1,2,3\}^3}
 \prod_{\nu=2}^4
 \ell_{q_\nu}^{(1)}(\theta_\nu)\,X_{e,q}^{\#},
 \qquad
 \ell_m^{(1)}(\theta)
 =\frac{1+2\cos(\theta-m\pi/2)}4 .                        \tag{11.1}
\]
This field is Hermitian and has Laurent degree at most one in every
transverse phase.  It would therefore permit the exact V7 recursive
elimination if its first \(90\times90\) block remained positive.
As already stressed in V6 and V8, however, the functions in (11.1)
are truncated Fourier functions, not cardinal functions.

\begin{countertheorem}[Exact Wilson failure of the V10 degree-one splitting]
\label{ctr:newv11-degree-one}
For the Wilson action and the plus four-thirds pencil, the
\(90\times90\) first block made from (11.1) is indefinite at
\[
 (k_2,k_3,k_4)=(\pi,\pi,\pi).                             \tag{11.2}
\]
More precisely, there is a Gaussian-integer vector
\(w_{\mathrm W}\in\mathbb Z[i]^{90}\), supported on \(55\)
coordinates, for which
\[
 (w_{\mathrm W})^*
 {\cal Q}^{(4/3,1)}_{\mathrm W}(\pi,\pi,\pi)
 w_{\mathrm W}
 =
 -\frac{4162569499338725470162267}{5760000000000}<0.      \tag{11.3}
\]
Consequently the uniform-\(13/25\) tensor degree-one interpolation of
the V10 splittings cannot perform even the first transverse
elimination for the four-thirds pencil.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
Reconstruct the \(64\) exact V10 Wilson splittings with denominator
\(10^{10}\).  Their ordered sample ledger has SHA--256
\[
\texttt{7C5A217595A34B1F4AC78F3C8EE4910EC8327CE608723EFC317BE071B203C683}.
                                                               \tag{11.4}
\]
At (11.2), all weights in (11.1) are rational quarter-integers.
Insert their exact tensor sum into the block (2.5), with
\(\delta=0\), for the exact Laurent coefficients of
\[
 \frac43H_{\mathrm W}+\nabla_1H_{\mathrm W}.
\]
The Laurent adjoint relation and Hermiticity of the resulting block
hold entry by entry over \(\mathbb Q[i]\).

A numerical negative eigenvector is used only to select an integer
candidate.  After rounding and deleting zeroable components, recompute
the quadratic form over \(\mathbb Q\).  The ordered ledger of the
remaining \(55\) Gaussian-integer components has SHA--256
\[
\texttt{004A99210A56854D60DA517E9A19333A4BCDFADADB0E3567571B7AA9D346B504}.
                                                               \tag{11.5}
\]
Exact substitution gives (11.3).  The executable recomputation is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v11_four_thirds_degree_one_counterexample.py}
\end{center}
and has canonical payload SHA--256
\[
\texttt{837E5A837569E8A928321DAE490AC6D88A8F6AE3F64B9EAC1F1B82A4894AC7C4}.
                                                               \tag{11.6}
\]
Thus the sign conclusion does not use the floating eigenvalue.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[The strong pencil is not refuted]
\label{fire:newv11-auxiliary}
The vector in Countertheorem~\ref{ctr:newv11-degree-one} belongs to
the doubled certificate space and is not constrained to the
structured form \((u,e^{ik_1}u)\).  It therefore does not give a
negative vector for
\(\frac43H_{\mathrm W}+\nabla_1H_{\mathrm W}\).
Indeed, Theorem~\ref{thm:newv10-strong-circles} proves that the latter
pencil is strictly positive on the entire active circle over
(11.2).  The exact witness closes only the particular interpolated
splitting (11.1).
\end{firewall}

\subsection{Upstream diagnosis}

Before forming the exact witness, a diagnostic evaluated (11.1) at
all \(64\) transverse quarter-turn points.  Only \(52\) Wilson blocks
were positive; the smallest sampled eigenvalue was approximately
\(-2.82573859823\cdot10^{-4}\) at (11.2).  All \(64\) endpoint blocks
were positive, with sampled minimum approximately
\(1.04078706360\cdot10^{-3}\) at the same point.  These latter
floating values are route-selection data only.  Their diagnostic
payload SHA--256 is
\[
\texttt{267ACB2006F63C0DBED7F28EDDC4F009A29AFFF9D8811EB2E37E054F421B9212}.
                                                               \tag{11.7}
\]
The asymmetry shows that the V10 reserve improves the endpoint
interpolation but does not cure Wilson's low-margin directions.

\begin{decision}[V12 uses local shape preservation, not global truncation]
\label{dec:newv11-next}
Abandon the globally truncated tensor field (11.1).  On each
transverse quarter-cell, retain its vertex splittings but introduce
a local Hermitian correction with zero vertex values.  Choose the
correction by the finite-dimensional max--min problem for the first
\(90\times90\) block, and certify a positive overlap margin on the
cell boundary.  Adjacent corrections may then be glued only after
their common-face restrictions agree exactly.

The first upstream test is the Wilson cell incident to
\((\pi,\pi,\pi)\).  If no affine or first-harmonic zero-vertex
correction repairs its exact witness, enrich only that cell by the
smallest second-harmonic face-compatible term.  This preserves the
V10 exact anchor splittings and avoids the global oscillation that
caused (11.3).  A successful local block must subsequently receive an
interval or sum-of-squares certificate over the whole cell; positive
sampling is not acceptance.
\end{decision}

\begin{assessment}[Progress in V11]
\label{ass:newv11-progress}
The stronger four-thirds reserve does not make the old global
degree-one recursion valid.  The failure has been upgraded from a
Riccati exception to an exact rational counterexample at a V10
anchor, so no effort need be spent on the ensuing \(180\times180\)
stage.  The surviving route is a local, face-compatible splitting
whose correction is adapted to the Wilson low-margin sector.
\end{assessment}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V11]
\label{fire:newv11-final}
No global four-thirds or constant-two Loewner
inequality, continuum response sign, interacting Yang--Mills
measure, reflection-positive continuum limit, exponential
clustering, or Hamiltonian mass gap has yet been proved.  The full
Yang--Mills mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V12: full-cardinal no-go and the independent-patch principle}
\label{sec:newv12-cardinal-patches}

\subsection{The anchor-preserving interpolation is still insufficient}

The V11 degree-one field failed partly because it did not interpolate
the V10 anchor splittings.  The unique real four-point cardinal
function used earlier in V6 is
\[
 \ell_m^{\mathrm{card}}(\theta)
 =
 \frac{1+2\cos(\theta-m\pi/2)
 +\cos(2\theta-m\pi)}4,\qquad m\in\{0,1,2,3\}.            \tag{12.1}
\]
It obeys
\(\ell_m^{\mathrm{card}}(n\pi/2)=\delta_{mn}\).
Thus the tensor field
\[
 \widetilde X_e^{\mathrm{card}}(\theta_2,\theta_3,\theta_4)
 =
 \sum_{q\in\{0,1,2,3\}^3}
 \prod_{\nu=2}^4
 \ell_{q_\nu}^{\mathrm{card}}(\theta_\nu)X_{e,q}^{\#}     \tag{12.2}
\]
preserves every exact V10 anchor splitting.  It has transverse degree
two, so even positivity would require a higher-degree elimination.
For Wilson, positivity itself fails.

\begin{countertheorem}[Exact rational-phase failure of the full-cardinal field]
\label{ctr:newv12-cardinal}
For the Wilson plus four-thirds pencil, let the transverse phases be
\[
 z_2=\frac{-4-3i}{5},\qquad
 z_3=\frac{-4+3i}{5},\qquad
 z_4=\frac{3+4i}{5}.                                     \tag{12.3}
\]
Each has unit modulus.  The \(90\times90\) certificate block formed
with (12.2) is indefinite.  In fact there is
\(w_{\mathrm W}^{\mathrm{card}}\in\mathbb Z[i]^{90}\),
supported on \(83\) coordinates, such that
\[
 (w_{\mathrm W}^{\mathrm{card}})^*
 {\cal Q}_{\mathrm W}^{(4/3,\mathrm{card})}(z_2,z_3,z_4)
 w_{\mathrm W}^{\mathrm{card}}
 =
 -\frac{7096249449853730635888957}{43945312500000}<0.     \tag{12.4}
\]
Hence exact interpolation of all \(64\) V10 splittings does not
produce a global feasible splitting.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
For a rational unit phase \(z=c+is\), the four values in (12.1) are
rational because
\[
 \cos(\theta-m\pi/2)\in\{c,s,-c,-s\},\qquad
 \cos(2\theta-m\pi)=(-1)^m(c^2-s^2).                     \tag{12.5}
\]
The three pairs \((c,s)\) in (12.3) are
\[
 (-4/5,-3/5),\qquad(-4/5,3/5),\qquad(3/5,4/5),
\]
so the exact V10 sample splittings and (12.5) make every entry of
(12.2) rational.  Evaluating the strong Laurent coefficient banks at
(12.3) keeps every entry in \(\mathbb Q[i]\).  Direct adjoint checks
prove that the splitting, central coefficient, and full block are
Hermitian.

Round a floating negative eigenvector only to choose an integer
candidate, delete dispensable components, and recompute over
\(\mathbb Q\).  The ordered \(83\)-component Gaussian-integer ledger
has SHA--256
\[
\texttt{2BD6811A56403915C9D5978AC39A0004C5111CC98AE53320C8D894C8B0A870B1},
                                                               \tag{12.6}
\]
and exact substitution yields (12.4).  The V10 splitting ledger is
the same one recorded in (11.4).  The executable recomputation is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v12_four_thirds_cardinal_counterexample.py}
\end{center}
and its canonical payload SHA--256 is
\[
\texttt{B91A4565C5D5A8377E40ED427B4368190560A48B872F1012447C361738D321E0}.
                                                               \tag{12.7}
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[A second auxiliary failure]
\label{fire:newv12-auxiliary}
The witness in (12.4) lies in the doubled certificate space and is
not required to have the structured form \((u,z_1u)\).  It refutes
only the full-cardinal splitting (12.2), not the underlying
four-thirds pencil at (12.3).  In particular it is not evidence for a
negative Yang--Mills response.
\end{firewall}

\subsection{Local splittings require no gluing}

The V11 decision imposed common-face agreement on adjacent local
splittings.  That condition would be needed to construct a single
global splitting field, but a global field is not the theorem needed
for the pencil.  The pointwise implication in V2 permits a strictly
simpler finite-cover proof.

\begin{theorem}[Independent local-splitting cover]
\label{thm:newv12-independent-cover}
Let \(T\) be compact and let
\[
 P(z;t)=A(t)+zB(t)+\bar zB(t)^*,\qquad |z|=1,\quad t\in T, \tag{12.8}
\]
where \(A(t)=A(t)^*\) and \(A,B\) are continuous.  Suppose
\(T=\bigcup_{j=1}^J U_j\) and for every \(j\) there is a fixed
Hermitian matrix \(X_j\) and a number \(\eta_j>0\) such that
\[
 {\cal Q}_j(t):=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 A(t)-X_j&B(t)\\
 B(t)^*&X_j
 \end{pmatrix}
 \succeq\eta_j I
 \qquad(t\in U_j).                                       \tag{12.9}
\]
Then
\[
 P(z;t)\succeq 2\min_{j:t\in U_j}\eta_j\,I
 \qquad(|z|=1,\ t\in T),                                 \tag{12.10}
\]
where any one index containing \(t\) may be used.  No agreement
between \(X_j\) and \(X_\ell\) on \(U_j\cap U_\ell\) is required.

More quantitatively, if at a centre \(t_j\)
\[
 {\cal Q}_j(t_j)\succeq\eta_jI                            \tag{12.11}
\]
and throughout \(U_j\)
\[
 \|A(t)-A(t_j)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
 +\|B(t)-B(t_j)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\rho_j<\eta_j,          \tag{12.12}
\]
then (12.9) holds with \(\eta_j\) replaced by
\(\eta_j-\rho_j\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For \(w_z=(u,zu)\), direct multiplication as in (2.9) gives
\[
 w_z^*{\cal Q}_j(t)w_z=u^*P(z;t)u,\qquad
 \|w_z\|^2=2\|u\|^2.                                     \tag{12.13}
\]
Choose any \(j\) with \(t\in U_j\) and apply (12.9); this proves
(12.10) with the corresponding local floor.  The calculation never
compares two splittings, so overlap compatibility is irrelevant.

For the quantitative assertion, subtract the centre block:
\[
 {\cal Q}_j(t)-{\cal Q}_j(t_j)
 =
 \begin{pmatrix}
 \Delta A&\Delta B\\
 \Delta B^*&0
 \end{pmatrix}.                                          \tag{12.14}
\]
The diagonal summand has operator norm
\(\|\Delta A\|_{\mathrm{op}}\), while the off-diagonal Hermitian
summand has operator norm exactly
\(\|\Delta B\|_{\mathrm{op}}\), by singular-value decomposition.
Thus (12.12) and Weyl's inequality give
\[
 {\cal Q}_j(t)\succeq(\eta_j-\rho_j)I,                   \tag{12.15}
\]
and (12.13) finishes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The local atlas is a finite exact target]
\label{cor:newv12-finite-atlas}
If every \(t\in T\) admits a fixed splitting \(X_t\) with a strictly
positive block at \(t\), then continuity produces an open
neighbourhood on which the same splitting remains positive.  By
compactness, finitely many such neighbourhoods prove uniform
positivity of (12.8), provided their positive floors and coverage are
certified.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:newv12-independent-cover} locally with
\(\rho_t<\eta_t\), then extract a finite subcover.  The minimum of the
finitely many reduced floors is positive.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What V5 did and did not exclude]
\label{rem:newv12-v5}
V5 proved that a coefficientwise global Frobenius stock combined with
the small V4 block floor gives an impractically tiny isotropic radius.
It did not exclude the independent-patch theorem.  The next
calculation must improve both sides of (12.12): choose a splitting
near the block analytic centre to enlarge \(\eta_j\), and bound
\(\Delta A,\Delta B\) in the local operator metric rather than by a
global coefficientwise Frobenius sum.
\end{remark}

\begin{decision}[V13 optimizes the hard Wilson patch]
\label{dec:newv12-next}
At the Wilson transverse anchor \((\pi,\pi,\pi)\), solve the convex
max--min splitting problem
\[
 \max_{X=X^*}\ 
 \lambda_{\min}
 \begin{pmatrix}A-X&B\\B^*&X\end{pmatrix}.                \tag{12.16}
\]
Rationalize the candidate and certify its floor exactly.  Then
compute local operator-norm bounds for \(\Delta A\) and \(\Delta B\)
in a congruence metric adapted to that block.  The first acceptance
target is one explicit nonzero box around the hard anchor, with an
exact coverage radius from (12.12).

If the optimized block still gives a negligible radius, subdivide and
re-optimize independently; do not impose face matching.  If
(12.16) has zero optimum despite the positive active-circle pencil,
replace the sufficient block representation by a higher-degree
spectral factor at that point and certify it directly.
\end{decision}

\begin{assessment}[Progress in V12]
\label{ass:newv12-progress}
Both global tensor interpolants of the V10 splittings are now closed
by exact Wilson counterexamples.  The proof target has nevertheless
become simpler: a finite atlas of independent fixed splittings is
enough, and its local acceptance inequality is the explicit
operator-norm estimate (12.12).  The next bottleneck is quantitative,
not topological gluing.
\end{assessment}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V12]
\label{fire:newv12-final}
No finite splitting atlas, full-transverse-torus Loewner inequality,
continuum response sign, interacting Yang--Mills measure,
reflection-positive continuum limit, exponential clustering, or
Hamiltonian mass gap has yet been proved.  The full Yang--Mills
mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V13: the first exact transverse local patch}
\label{sec:newv13-local-patch}

\subsection{Centred Taylor control for a fixed splitting}

Let \(t=(k_2,k_3,k_4)\), let
\(t_0=(\pi,\pi,\pi)\), and write the exact transverse Fourier banks
of the Wilson plus four-thirds pencil as
\[
 A(t)=\sum_{\alpha}A_\alpha e^{i\alpha\cdot t},
 \qquad
 B(t)=\sum_{\alpha}B_\alpha e^{i\alpha\cdot t},
 \qquad \alpha\in\{-1,0,1\}^3.                            \tag{13.1}
\]
For a fixed Hermitian splitting \(X\), put
\[
 {\cal Q}_X(t)=
 \begin{pmatrix}A(t)-X&B(t)\\B(t)^*&X\end{pmatrix}.       \tag{13.2}
\]
The local estimate below improves the purely coefficientwise
first-order stock of V5 by retaining cancellation in the exact first
derivatives at the centre.

\begin{lemma}[Centred first derivative plus quadratic remainder]
\label{lem:newv13-taylor}
Define
\[
\begin{aligned}
 L_1&=\sum_{\nu=2}^4
 \left(\|\partial_\nu A(t_0)\|_{\mathrm F}
       +\|\partial_\nu B(t_0)\|_{\mathrm F}\right),\\
 L_2&=\sum_\alpha \|\alpha\|_1^2
 \left(\|A_\alpha\|_{\mathrm F}+\|B_\alpha\|_{\mathrm F}\right).
\end{aligned}                                             \tag{13.3}
\]
If \(\|t-t_0\|_\infty\le h\), using the local lift of the torus
coordinates, then
\[
 \|A(t)-A(t_0)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
 +\|B(t)-B(t_0)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
 \le hL_1+\frac{h^2}{2}L_2.                              \tag{13.4}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For \(x\in\mathbb R\),
\[
 |e^{ix}-1-ix|\le\frac{x^2}{2},                           \tag{13.5}
\]
by the integral remainder for the exponential.  Apply (13.5) to
\(x=\alpha\cdot(t-t_0)\) in each Fourier term of (13.1).  The linear
parts sum to the exact centred derivatives, while
\[
 |\alpha\cdot(t-t_0)|
 \le h\|\alpha\|_1.                                      \tag{13.6}
\]
The triangle inequality and
\(\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm F}\) give
(13.4) separately for \(A\) and \(B\), and then after addition.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Exact Wilson box}

A one-dimensional max--min diagnostic along the two Riccati endpoint
splittings selected the nearby rational weight
\[
 \tau=\frac{101}{200}.                                   \tag{13.7}
\]
The sampled block-floor improvement over \(13/25\) was only about
\(0.09\%\); the choice (13.7) is therefore not presented as a global
optimum.  Its value is that it is rational and gives a reproducible
strict interior certificate.

\begin{theorem}[Exact hard-anchor local patch]
\label{thm:newv13-hard-patch}
There is an explicitly recorded rational Hermitian matrix
\(X_{\mathrm W}^{\#}\), with entry denominator \(10^{10}\), such that
at \(t_0=(\pi,\pi,\pi)\)
\[
 {\cal Q}_{X_{\mathrm W}^{\#}}(t_0)
 \succeq
 \eta_{\mathrm W}I_{90},\qquad
 \eta_{\mathrm W}
 =
 \frac{984232681826190705976066409}
 {9000000033876000000000000000000}.                      \tag{13.8}
\]
Moreover, throughout the exact coordinate box
\[
 \|t-t_0\|_\infty\le h_{\mathrm W},
 \qquad h_{\mathrm W}=\frac1{262144},                    \tag{13.9}
\]
one has
\[
 {\cal Q}_{X_{\mathrm W}^{\#}}(t)
 \succeq
 \frac{132946909115724969589965822222525629}
 {2415919113093519507456000000000000000000}\,I_{90}.
                                                               \tag{13.10}
\]
Consequently, for every active momentum \(k_1\), the Wilson plus
four-thirds pencil satisfies
\[
 \frac43H_{\mathrm W}(k)+\nabla_1H_{\mathrm W}(k)
 \succeq
 \frac{132946909115724969589965822222525629}
 {1207959556546759753728000000000000000000}\,I_{45}      \tag{13.11}
\]
whenever (13.9) holds.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Construct the two floating Riccati endpoints at \(t_0\), take their
convex combination with (13.7), round the upper triangle to
denominator \(10^{10}\), and impose Hermitian symmetry exactly.  The
ordered splitting ledger has SHA--256
\[
\texttt{10C67ABCC9B3D59CE700F8B7FCD381BEC0AC0A6344EA7CAAC8E59BC24393FCC4}.
                                                               \tag{13.12}
\]
Exact rational congruence and
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv3-congruence} give (13.8); the associated
basis-orthogonality error is at most
\[
 \frac{941}{250000000000}<1.                             \tag{13.13}
\]

For the exact coefficient banks in (13.1), rational square-root
ceilings of the Frobenius norms give
\[
 L_1=\frac{7121061293133}{500000000000},
 \qquad
 L_2=\frac{18130241884671}{500000000000}.                \tag{13.14}
\]
At the dyadic radius in (13.9), Lemma~\ref{lem:newv13-taylor}
therefore bounds the variation by
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm W}
 =
 \frac{149340204539839959}
 {2748779069440000000000}
 <\frac{\eta_{\mathrm W}}2.                              \tag{13.15}
\]
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv12-independent-cover}, or directly Weyl's
inequality applied to (13.2), gives the exact difference
\(\eta_{\mathrm W}-\rho_{\mathrm W}\), which is the fraction in
(13.10).  Finally use the structured vector
\((u,e^{ik_1}u)\), whose squared norm is \(2\|u\|^2\), to double the
block floor and obtain (13.11).

The executable recomputation is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v13_hard_wilson_local_patch.py}
\end{center}
and has canonical payload SHA--256
\[
\texttt{6630D581FF00351C33A2F6D09F97F26F31F561CD2392ACBAD227A3D1F391742B}.
                                                               \tag{13.16}
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Minus-sign inversion patch]
\label{cor:newv13-minus}
The same box and floor hold for
\(\frac43H_{\mathrm W}-\nabla_1H_{\mathrm W}\), after conjugating the
splitting and inverting momentum.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The box centred at \((\pi,\pi,\pi)\) is invariant under
\(t\mapsto-t\) modulo \(2\pi\).  Apply
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv4-inversion} to (13.11); complex conjugation
preserves the Loewner floor.
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[What the radius means]
\label{ass:newv13-radius}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv13-hard-patch} is the first exact certificate in
this compact lineage covering a nonzero three-dimensional transverse
neighbourhood together with the complete active circle.  It proves
the independent-patch mechanism rather than merely its abstract
availability.

The radius \(2^{-18}\) is nevertheless far too small for a direct
uniform subdivision of the transverse torus.  The block floor is
about \(1.09359\cdot10^{-4}\), while \(L_1\) is about \(14.2421\).
Thus the first-order term, not the quadratic remainder, controls the
present radius.  A practical finite atlas requires a much larger
interior block margin, a sharper metric-aware perturbation estimate,
or both.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V14 targets relative rather than absolute variation]
\label{dec:newv13-next}
At the same hard patch, let \(R^\#\) be the rational approximate
inverse Cholesky or eigenbasis congruence already used to certify
(13.8).  Bound the centred variation in the relative metric
\[
 \left\|(R^\#)^*
   \bigl({\cal Q}(t)-{\cal Q}(t_0)\bigr)R^\#
 \right\|_{\mathrm{op}}                                  \tag{13.17}
\]
and compare it with the exact congruence floor, rather than comparing
an ambient Frobenius stock directly with \(\eta_{\mathrm W}\).
Retain the centred Taylor remainder and compute anisotropic radii,
since the three derivative matrices need not share their worst
directions even though their scalar Frobenius ceilings coincide.

If the relative metric does not enlarge the box materially, the
small block margin is intrinsic to this Gram representation.  Then
go upstream from the splitting block and certify the direct
\(45\times45\) pencil on a local four-dimensional box using its much
larger sampled active-circle margin.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V13]
\label{fire:newv13-final}
One local box is not a finite cover of the transverse torus.  No
global covariant Loewner inequality, continuum response sign,
interacting Yang--Mills measure, reflection-positive continuum
limit, exponential clustering, or Hamiltonian mass gap has yet been
proved.  The full Yang--Mills mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V14: exact relative-metric enlargement of the hard patch}
\label{sec:newv14-relative}

\subsection{A congruence-adapted perturbation lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Relative local block control]
\label{lem:newv14-relative}
Let \(Q(t)=Q(t)^*\), let \(R\) be invertible, and suppose
\[
 R^*Q(t_0)R\succeq\beta I,\qquad
 R^*R\preceq\gamma I,                                   \tag{14.1}
\]
with \(\beta,\gamma>0\).  If on a set \(U\)
\[
 \|R^*(Q(t)-Q(t_0))R\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\rho<\beta,        \tag{14.2}
\]
then
\[
 Q(t)\succeq\frac{\beta-\rho}{\gamma}I
 \qquad(t\in U).                                         \tag{14.3}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Equations (14.1)--(14.2) and Weyl's inequality give
\(R^*Q(t)R\succeq(\beta-\rho)I\).  For \(x=Ry\),
\[
 x^*Q(t)x\ge(\beta-\rho)\|y\|^2,\qquad
 \|x\|^2\le\gamma\|y\|^2,                                \tag{14.4}
\]
which proves (14.3).  Notice that the first inequality in (14.1)
itself forces \(R\) to be invertible if this was not assumed.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The rational relative metric}

Let \(Q_0={\cal Q}_{X_{\mathrm W}^{\#}}(t_0)\) be the exact V13
centre block.  A floating inverse square root of \(Q_0\) is used only
to propose a Hermitian matrix.  Rounding its upper triangle to
denominator \(10^8\) and reflecting exactly gives \(R^\#\).  Its
ordered rational ledger has SHA--256
\[
\texttt{53B280BED9A3890E7F975F470E972A3DF5062FE83249FDA39DC638B3700A4B4E}.
                                                               \tag{14.5}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact relative hard-anchor patch]
\label{thm:newv14-relative-patch}
For the Wilson plus four-thirds pencil and the V13 splitting,
\[
 (R^\#)^*Q_0R^\#
 \succeq\beta_{\mathrm{rel}}I,\qquad
 \beta_{\mathrm{rel}}
 =
 \frac{299999799810016468500864661}
 {300000000000000000000000000}.                          \tag{14.6}
\]
Furthermore,
\[
 (R^\#)^*R^\#
 \preceq\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}I,\qquad
 \gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}
 =
 \frac{3882277999062472511}{400000000000000}.             \tag{14.7}
\]
On the exact transverse box
\[
 \|t-(\pi,\pi,\pi)\|_\infty\le\frac1{1024},               \tag{14.8}
\]
the fixed-splitting block obeys
\[
 Q(t)\succeq
 \frac{1005674410887415714332345341754301}
 {12212590605434793527083008000000000000}\,I_{90}.        \tag{14.9}
\]
Hence the Wilson plus four-thirds pencil, uniformly in \(k_1\),
has the doubled floor
\[
 \frac{1005674410887415714332345341754301}
 {6106295302717396763541504000000000000}\,I_{45}.         \tag{14.10}
\]
The same box and floor hold for the minus sign by momentum inversion.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Exact integer matrix multiplication, rowwise rational absolute-value
ceilings, and Gershgorin give (14.6).  The exact Frobenius square of
\(R^\#\) is \(\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}\), so
\(\|R^\#\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\le\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}\), proving
(14.7).

Congruence-transform the three exact centred derivative blocks and
bound their operator norms by exact Frobenius ceilings.  The
individual bounds are
\[
 \frac{7429480491163}{500000000000},\qquad
 \frac{2220262212991}{200000000000},\qquad
 \frac{3888366537931}{500000000000},                     \tag{14.11}
\]
whose sum is
\[
 L_{1,\mathrm{rel}}
 =\frac{33737005123143}{1000000000000}.                  \tag{14.12}
\]
For the quadratic remainder, use
\[
 \|(R^\#)^*ER^\#\|_{\mathrm{op}}
 \le\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}\|E\|_{\mathrm{op}}
 \le\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}\|E\|_{\mathrm F}.              \tag{14.13}
\]
Combining (14.13) with the V13 coefficient remainder stock gives
\[
 L_{2,\mathrm{rel}}
 =
 \frac{70386639186539160388901769778881}
 {200000000000000000000000000}.                          \tag{14.14}
\]
The centred Taylor proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:newv13-taylor}, now after
congruence, yields at \(h=1/1024\)
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm{rel}}
 \le hL_{1,\mathrm{rel}}+\frac{h^2}{2}L_{2,\mathrm{rel}}
 =
 \frac{84205316484978533188901769778881}
 {419430400000000000000000000000000}
 <\frac{\beta_{\mathrm{rel}}}{2}.                         \tag{14.15}
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv14-relative} gives
\((\beta_{\mathrm{rel}}-\rho_{\mathrm{rel}})
 /\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}\), which reduces to the fraction in (14.9).
The structured-vector argument doubles it to (14.10).
Momentum inversion proves the last assertion.

The exact verifier is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v14_relative_hard_wilson_patch.py}
\end{center}
and has canonical payload SHA--256
\[
\texttt{C7651B9418C6E11B6288B662AD905DB57D75F489B967A47B109D3B6408CF2BEC}.
                                                               \tag{14.16}
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[A material quantitative gain]
\label{ass:newv14-gain}
The exact radius in (14.8) is \(256\) times the V13 radius.  The
relative centre floor is approximately \(0.99999933\), the certified
relative variation at the new radius is approximately \(0.2007612\),
and the resulting pencil floor is approximately
\(1.6469469\cdot10^{-4}\).  This proves that the tiny V13 patch was
largely an artifact of comparing an ambient perturbation directly
with the smallest auxiliary block eigenvalue.

The quadratic stock (14.14) is deliberately conservative: it applies
the single global factor \(\gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}\) before summing
Fourier coefficients.  A floating coefficientwise relative
calculation predicts a half-margin radius near \(2.27\cdot10^{-2}\),
but that number is diagnostic only.  There remains substantial room
between it and the exact radius \(2^{-10}\).
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V15 audits companions and transforms the remainder coefficientwise]
\label{dec:newv14-next}
Perform the required V15 SM/NS companion audit.  Then transform each
nonzero Fourier coefficient block by \(R^\#\) exactly before taking
its Frobenius ceiling.  This replaces (14.13)--(14.14) by the smaller
coefficientwise stock
\[
 \sum_\alpha\|\alpha\|_1^2
 \|(R^\#)^*Q_\alpha R^\#\|_{\mathrm F}.                  \tag{14.17}
\]
Compute anisotropic coordinate stocks at the same time.  If the
result reaches a useful fraction of the floating radius, extend the
calculation to all symmetry-inequivalent quarter-turn anchors and
assemble a certified independent-patch cover.  Otherwise go upstream
to direct-pencil local boxes as prescribed in V13.
\end{decision}

\label{thm:newv15-coefficientwise}

The resulting exact quadratic remainder stock is
\[
 L_{2,\mathrm{cw}}
 =\sum_\alpha\|\alpha\|_1^2
 \|(R^\#)^*Q_\alpha R^\#\|_{\mathrm F}
 \le\frac{14783746644789}{31250000000}.                   \tag{15.3}

 \|t-t_0\|_\infty\le\frac1{128}.                          \tag{15.4}

Throughout this box the Wilson plus four-thirds pencil, uniformly in

\]

products defining \((R^\#)^*Q_\alpha R^\#\), square and sum all

 \le\frac{284679787629933}{1024000000000000}
 <\frac{\beta_{\mathrm{rel}}}{2}.                         \tag{15.7}
\]
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:newv14-relative} with the exact \(\beta\) and

 {2911708499296854383250000000000}.                       \tag{15.8}
\]

\section{V16: exact quarter-anchor relative atlas and coverage test}
\label{sec:newv16-atlas}

\begin{theorem}[All quarter-turn anchors have exact relative patches]
\label{thm:newv16-atlas}
For each action \(e\in\{\mathrm W,\mathrm E\}\) and every transverse
quarter-turn anchor
\[
 q\in\{0,\pi/2,\pi,3\pi/2\}^3,                           \tag{16.1}
\]
there is an explicitly hashed rational splitting, rational relative
metric, dyadic radius \(r_{e,q}>0\), and rational floor
\(\delta_{e,q}>0\) such that, for both signs and every \(k_1\),
\[
 \frac43H_e(k)\pm\nabla_1H_e(k)\succeq\delta_{e,q}I_{45}
 \quad\hbox{when}\quad
 \|(k_2,k_3,k_4)-q\|_{\infty,\mathbb T}\le r_{e,q}.        \tag{16.2}
\]
The radius ranges are
\[
\begin{array}{c|cc}
e&\min_q r_{e,q}&\max_q r_{e,q}\\ \hline
\mathrm W&1/128&1/64\\
\mathrm E&1/256&1/128,
\end{array}                                               \tag{16.3}
\]
and uniform lower floors over the respective \(64\) plus patches are
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \min_q\delta_{\mathrm W,q}
 &=
 \frac{29875787515434437682922667}
 {294654630990930529468500000000},\\
 \min_q\delta_{\mathrm E,q}
 &=
 \frac{126694753870076515419761467}
 {28410058402139894985000000000}.
\end{aligned}                                             \tag{16.4}
\]
They are approximately \(1.0139256\cdot10^{-4}\) and
\(4.4595035\cdot10^{-3}\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At each of the \(128\) action/anchor pairs, reconstruct the exact
four-thirds Laurent banks, rationalize the interior Riccati splitting
to denominator \(10^{10}\), and rationalize the inverse-square-root
metric to denominator \(10^8\).  Apply the V14 relative lemma with
the three exact transformed derivative bounds and the V15
coefficientwise transformed remainder stock.  Choose the largest
tested dyadic radius for which the exact Taylor variation is no more
than half the exact relative centre floor.  Every sign and floor
decision is then rational.  The Wilson and endpoint patch ledgers
have SHA--256 values
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\texttt{6F74F97B8B7AD39F82696B4B57936244BBA85FE954BC9C5862DEFEC4C7965ECA},\\
&\texttt{2C11C03F116EE25C1E6DE0655584941F88C67DF2FA6EC4E59079BDAB2136170E}.
\end{aligned}                                             \tag{16.5}
\]
Momentum inversion supplies the minus patches from the directly

and has canonical payload SHA--256

\begin{countertheorem}[The quarter-anchor atlas does not cover]
\label{ctr:newv16-no-cover}
The patches in Theorem~\ref{thm:newv16-atlas} do not cover the
transverse three-torus.  An exact uncovered point is
\[
 t_\diamond=(\pi/4,\pi/4,\pi/4).                          \tag{16.7}
\]
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
In each coordinate the torus distance from \(\pi/4\) to the
quarter-turn grid is \(\pi/4\).  Thus the sup distance in (16.7) from
every anchor is at least \(\pi/4\).  Since \(\pi>3\),
\[
 \frac{\pi}{4}>\frac34>\frac1{64}
 \ge\max_{e,q}r_{e,q}.                                   \tag{16.8}
\]
Hence (16.7) belongs to none of the certified boxes.
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[Progress and the correct refinement]
\label{ass:newv16}
V16 upgrades the single hard patch to exact local certificates at
every quarter-turn anchor for both actions and signs.  It also proves,
rather than guesses, that this atlas is incomplete.  Re-optimizing
the same anchors cannot bridge a gap of order \(\pi/4\) with radii of
order \(10^{-2}\); new interior centres are required.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V17 inserts an exact interior centre]
\label{dec:newv16-next}
Start at \(t_\diamond\).  Its eighth-turn phases lie in
\(\mathbb Q(\sqrt2,i)\).  Construct the active-circle coefficients
and a splitting using rigorous rational enclosures of \(\sqrt2\), or
equivalently use a nearby rational unit-circle triple whose certified
box contains \(t_\diamond\).  Apply the same relative coefficientwise
calculus and measure the new radius.  Only after an interior patch is
accepted should the centre set be refined adaptively toward an exact
cover.
\end{decision}

\section{V17: exact rational interior centres cover the first atlas gap}
\label{sec:newv17-interior}

Put
\[
 z_\circ=\frac{4059+4060i}{5741}=e^{i\theta_\circ}.
                                                               \tag{17.1}
\]
This is an exact rational point of the unit circle because
\(4059^2+4060^2=5741^2\).

\begin{lemma}[Exact eighth-turn proximity]
\label{lem:newv17-angle}
The angle in (17.1) satisfies
\[
 0<\theta_\circ-\frac{\pi}{4}
 =\arctan\frac1{8119}<\frac1{8119}.                       \tag{17.2}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The tangent subtraction identity gives
\[
 \tan(\theta_\circ-\pi/4)
 =\frac{4060-4059}{4060+4059}=\frac1{8119}.               \tag{17.3}
\]
The angle difference lies in \((0,\pi/2)\), and
\(\arctan x<x\) for \(x>0\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The V16 gap witness is covered for both signs]
\label{thm:newv17-gap-covered}
For each action, construct plus-sign relative patches at the two
rational transverse centres
\[
 (z_\circ,z_\circ,z_\circ),\qquad
 (\bar z_\circ,\bar z_\circ,\bar z_\circ).                \tag{17.4}
\]
The Wilson patches have coordinate radius \(1/64\); the endpoint
patches have coordinate radius \(1/128\).  Consequently at
\[
 t_\diamond=(\pi/4,\pi/4,\pi/4)                           \tag{17.5}
\]
and for every \(k_1\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
 \frac43H_{\mathrm W}(k)\pm\nabla_1H_{\mathrm W}(k)
 &\succeq\frac9{10000}I_{45},\\
 \frac43H_{\mathrm E}(k)\pm\nabla_1H_{\mathrm E}(k)
 &\succeq\frac1{50}I_{45}.
\end{aligned}                                             \tag{17.6}
\]
The same bounds hold at \(-t_\diamond\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At each centre in (17.4), evaluate the exact Fourier banks over
\(\mathbb Q[i]\), form the rational \(13/25\) Riccati splitting, and
apply the V14--V15 relative coefficientwise construction.  All four
relative centre floors are positive.  The resulting exact local
pencil floors are, for positive and negative orientations,
\[
\begin{array}{c|cc}
e&+\theta_\circ&-\theta_\circ\\ \hline
\mathrm W&
\dfrac{501925847219567053297938450167}
{515199963222400599007657500000000}&
\dfrac{752888674186454654092239406373}
{772799944762862541716118750000000}\\[6pt]
\mathrm E&
\dfrac{1234063558041054241165266583538773}
{61358588591691740070468265312500000}&
\dfrac{1098488901518041524347483817170867}
{54448878007989219617797682500000000}.
\end{array}                                               \tag{17.7}
\]
Direct cross multiplication shows that the first row exceeds
\(9/10000\) and the second exceeds \(1/50\).

By Lemma~\ref{lem:newv17-angle},
\[
 |\theta_\circ-\pi/4|<1/8119<1/128\le1/64,               \tag{17.8}
\]
so the positive-orientation patches contain (17.5).  The plus pencil
therefore has the stated bounds.  Applying momentum inversion to the
negative-orientation plus patch gives the minus pencil at (17.5).
The opposite assignment proves the statement at \(-t_\diamond\).

The executable verifier is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v17_rational_interior_patch.py}
\end{center}
and has canonical payload SHA--256
\[
\texttt{480D6DD24DFABA7A1BA6780EB20CE5D24817DDD479B5ED835335C3AF272EE92C}.
                                                               \tag{17.9}
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[A genuine adaptive-cover step]
\label{ass:newv17}
The exact point used in V16 to disprove quarter-anchor coverage is no
longer a gap: it now has independent positive patches for both signs
and both actions.  This demonstrates that rational unit-circle
centres can replace algebraic eighth-turn arithmetic without losing
coverage.  It does not imply that the rest of the quarter cells are
covered; the certified radii remain much smaller than the cell size.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V18 populates the symmetry orbit of interior centres]
\label{dec:newv17-next}
Generate the signed-coordinate and quarter-turn translates of

against the quarter-cell decomposition.  When a box gap remains,
insert a rational unit phase obtained from the tangent

\[
 z(r)=\frac{1-r^2+2ri}{1+r^2},\qquad r\in\mathbb Q,       \tag{17.10}
\]

\section{V19: direct-pencil relative boxes at the quarter-grid bottlenecks}
\label{sec:newv19-direct}

The V18 decision proposed another layer of independently split
active-circle patches.  That construction is exact, but its certified
radii remain at most \(1/64\), and
Countertheorem~\ref{ctr:newv18-no-cover} shows that two such layers
still leave macroscopic gaps.  Continuing to enumerate local
\(90\times90\) splittings would also preserve a separate gluing
problem.  We therefore follow the upstream alternative already
recorded after V13: certify the direct \(45\times45\) pencil itself
on four-dimensional boxes.  The first test is at the two
quarter-grid bottlenecks of Theorem~\ref{thm:newv9-four-thirds}.

\subsection{Exact direct Laurent bank}

Write the exact Hessian bank as
\[
 H_e(k)=\sum_{\alpha\in{\cal S}_e}
 G_{e,\alpha}e^{i\alpha\cdot k},\qquad
 G_{e,-\alpha}=G_{e,\alpha}^*.                           \tag{19.1}
\]
If \(s_{j,1}\) is the first parity label of retained coordinate
\(j\), then the plus four-thirds pencil has the exact bank
\[
 P_e^+(k):=\frac43H_e(k)+\nabla_1H_e(k)
 =\sum_{\alpha\in{\cal S}_e}C_{e,\alpha}e^{i\alpha\cdot k},
                                                                  \tag{19.2}
\]
where, entrywise,
\[
 (C_{e,\alpha})_{ij}
 =
 \left\{\frac43+
 i\left(\alpha_1-\frac{s_{i,1}-s_{j,1}}2\right)\right\}
 (G_{e,\alpha})_{ij}.                                   \tag{19.3}
\]
Thus \(C_{e,-\alpha}=C_{e,\alpha}^*\) exactly.  Formula
(19.3), rather than a sampled matrix oracle, is used in every bound
below.

\begin{lemma}[Coefficientwise direct-pencil variation]
\label{lem:newv19-direct-variation}
Fix \(a\in\mathbb T^4\), an invertible matrix \(R\), and \(0<h<\pi\).
Put
\[
\begin{aligned}
 L_1&=\sum_{j=1}^4
 \left\|R^*(\partial_jP_e^+)(a)R\right\|_{\mathrm F},\\
 L_2&=\sum_{\alpha\in{\cal S}_e}
 |\alpha|_1^2\left\|R^*C_{e,\alpha}R\right\|_{\mathrm F}.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{19.4}
\]
Then, whenever
\(d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k,a)\le h\),
\[
 \left\|R^*\{P_e^+(k)-P_e^+(a)\}R\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
 \le hL_1+\frac{h^2}{2}L_2.                             \tag{19.5}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Choose the unique coordinate displacement \(u\in[-h,h]^4\) from
\(a\) to \(k\).  Apply the one-variable Taylor formula with integral
remainder to \(t\mapsto P_e^+(a+tu)\).  The linear term is bounded by
\(hL_1\).  For a Fourier monomial,
\[
 |(\alpha\cdot u)^2|\le h^2|\alpha|_1^2.
\]
The triangle inequality, coefficientwise congruence, and
\(\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm F}\) give the
remainder in (19.5).  No numerical differentiation or mesh
interpolation occurs.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The two exact bottleneck boxes}

Let
\[
 a_{\mathrm W}=(0,\pi,\pi,0),\qquad
 a_{\mathrm E}=(3\pi/2,\pi,\pi,\pi).                    \tag{19.6}
\]
These are respectively the Wilson bottleneck and the endpoint
plus-sign bottleneck in Theorem~\ref{thm:newv9-four-thirds}.

\begin{theorem}[Exact direct boxes at both quarter-grid bottlenecks]
\label{thm:newv19-direct-boxes}
For
\[
 h=\frac1{64},                                           \tag{19.7}
\]
the direct pencils satisfy
\[
\begin{aligned}
 P_{\mathrm W}^+(k)&\succeq
 \frac{784341753625211643}
 {245494101600257419520}\,I_{45}
 &&\text{if }d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k,a_{\mathrm W})\le h,\\
 P_{\mathrm E}^+(k)&\succeq
 \frac{1598228652728405643}
 {17905613826669929984}\,I_{45}
 &&\text{if }d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k,a_{\mathrm E})\le h.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{19.8}
\]
In particular, the two displayed floors exceed \(3/1000\) and
\(2/25\), respectively.  On the inverted boxes,
\[
 P_e^-(k)\succeq\delta_e I_{45}
 \quad\text{if}\quad
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k,-a_e)\le h,                    \tag{19.9}
\]
with the same exact floor \(\delta_e\) as in (19.8).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
All phases at (19.6) belong to \(\{1,i,-1,-i\}\), so (19.1)--(19.3)
give exact Gaussian-rational centre matrices and derivatives.  For
each action, a floating inverse square root is used only to propose
a matrix; rounding its real and imaginary parts to denominator
\(10^8\) produces an exact rational \(R_e\).  Direct rational
congruence and Gershgorin give
\[
\begin{array}{c|cc}
e&\beta_e:\ R_e^*P_e^+(a_e)R_e\succeq\beta_eI&
\gamma_e:\ R_e^*R_e\preceq\gamma_eI\\ \hline
\mathrm W&
\dfrac{4999997004051503}{5000000000000000}&
\dfrac{191792266875201109}{1000000000000000}\\[5pt]
\mathrm E&
\dfrac{2499993847015907}{2500000000000000}&
\dfrac{34971902005214707}{5000000000000000}.
\end{array}                                              \tag{19.10}
\]
The four exact transformed first-derivative ceilings sum to
\[
 L_{1,\mathrm W}=\frac{5698304565281}{250000000000},
 \qquad
 L_{1,\mathrm E}=\frac{21334417730781}{1000000000000},
                                                               \tag{19.11}
\]
and the coefficientwise stocks in Lemma
\ref{lem:newv19-direct-variation} are
\[
 L_{2,\mathrm W}=\frac{63668982703189}{250000000000},
 \qquad
 L_{2,\mathrm E}=\frac{173421339815429}{500000000000}.
                                                               \tag{19.12}
\]
At \(h=1/64\), the exact variations
\(\rho_e=hL_{1,e}+h^2L_{2,e}/2\) are
\[
 \rho_{\mathrm W}=
 \frac{793051967059157}{2048000000000000},\qquad
 \rho_{\mathrm E}=
 \frac{1538824074585413}{4096000000000000}.              \tag{19.13}
\]
Exact subtraction gives
\[
\frac{\beta_{\mathrm W}}2-\rho_{\mathrm W}
=\frac{144342137106619259}{1280000000000000000}>0,
\quad
\frac{\beta_{\mathrm E}}2-\rho_{\mathrm E}
=\frac{318231803056261259}{2560000000000000000}>0.       \tag{19.14}
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv19-direct-variation} and the relative control
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv14-relative} therefore yield
\((\beta_e-\rho_e)/\gamma_e\).  Reducing these two rational numbers
gives exactly (19.8); direct cross multiplication gives the simpler
strict lower bounds following it.

Finally Lemma~\ref{lem:newv4-inversion} states
\(P_e^-(-k)=\overline{P_e^+(k)}\).  Complex conjugation preserves
Loewner order and torus inversion is an isometry, which proves
(19.9).
\end{proof}

\begin{record}[Exact certificate provenance]
\label{rec:newv19-provenance}
The rational relative metrics have SHA--256 values
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm W:\;&
\texttt{259BBD75C0EA7EB630C88810402524AF2C6C64FE66474B7F618C357F92BBC2C3},\\
\mathrm E:\;&
\texttt{2377F37EF4F51DD9CF3F343B090ABA540D985AB6FE9C372ECC872A71AB37D308}.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{19.15}
\]
The ordered coefficient-bound ledgers have SHA--256 values
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm W:\;&
\texttt{FFBADD74859FB2DDA0986C23C2AA03FF36891F838CEF30A113267BD0045D1FD5},\\
\mathrm E:\;&
\texttt{29395B1B005701C1F51DB86A70CE2D29B22D2DE372CB0F8C55A3E59B59D5EFFF}.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{19.16}
\]
The executable verifier is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v19_direct_pencil_relative_patches.py}
\end{center}
and its canonical payload SHA--256 is
\[
\texttt{70FCFAB56329314B4D9A30D615EC883E4FE26C9611302D0AC3245680E6E8F398}.
                                                               \tag{19.17}
\]
\end{record}

\begin{assessment}[What the upstream pivot establishes]
\label{ass:newv19}
The auxiliary active-circle splitting is not needed to obtain a
useful neighbourhood of the two weakest quarter-grid matrices.  The
direct \(45\times45\) relative calculation reaches radius \(1/64\)
in all four momentum coordinates while retaining explicit positive
floors.  This removes splitting-gluing from these two boxes and
improves the endpoint radius beyond the entire \(1/256\)--\(1/128\)
range of the V18 split patches.  It does not yet show that direct
boxes cover even one quarter cell, nor that the radius \(1/64\) is
uniform over all \(256\) quarter-turn centres.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V20 audits companions and populates the direct grid]
\label{dec:newv19-next}
At the required fifth-version checkpoint, inspect the current SM and
NS notes before fixing the next direction.  Then run the exact
direct-pencil relative construction at every point of the
four-dimensional quarter-turn grid for both actions and the plus
sign, using inversion for the minus sign.  Record the minimum radius
and floor, exploit exact quarter-turn symmetries, and perform an exact
box-cover test.  If the direct radii remain far below the quarter-cell
inradius, stop uniform dyadic refinement and go upstream to an
analytic interval, Schur-complement, or sum-of-squares certificate
for the whole cell.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V19]
\label{fire:newv19-final}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv19-direct-boxes} proves two local
finite-dimensional boxes.  It does not prove a full-torus covariant
Loewner inequality or a cofinal regulator-uniform response sign.
It gives no
interacting four-dimensional Yang--Mills measure,
reflection-positive continuum limit, exponential clustering, or
Hamiltonian mass gap.  The full Yang--Mills mass-gap goal remains
open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V20: companion audit and the complete direct quarter-grid atlas}
\label{sec:newv20-direct-grid}

\subsection{Required fifth-version companion audit}

\begin{audit}[V20 companion provenance]
\label{audit:newv20-companions}
The audit was performed read-only against the current companion
sources available at the checkpoint.  The Standard--Model--Einstein
source was
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{Renewal_SM_Einstein_Continuum_Research_Notes_V54.tex}
\end{center}
with
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
\text{bytes}&702323\\
\text{lines}&21559\\
\text{SHA--256}&
\texttt{FD5F10AFD5EFCCBF89AFB7DE30DAA3BA3C90A0158A5CE960EF701D8EE7C34259}.
\end{array}                                               \tag{20.1}
\]
The Navier--Stokes source was
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{Renewal_NS_Stability_Research_Notes_V26.tex}
\end{center}
with
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
\text{bytes}&278283\\
\text{lines}&5319\\
\text{SHA--256}&
\texttt{82C887F8C2C69E4F28FAD7C3DC8DE5B9099CB5A4BF431EBA1FFF3E91935978AD}.
\end{array}                                               \tag{20.2}
\]

SM V54 proves the sharp lower edge for a two-block Hermitian matrix
from two diagonal floors and a cross-block norm, and records both a
fixed-vector and a semidefinite-dual obstruction.  The transferable
point is structural: a whole-cell Yang--Mills certificate should
retain diagonal/cross information instead of replacing the entire
matrix variation by one Frobenius stock.  Its gravity--matter
matrices and constants do not act on the present \(45\)-dimensional
fibre and are not imported.

NS V26 is unchanged.  Its exact rank-one calculation finds no gain
for one derivative and a localized gain of at most about eleven
percent for another.  This supports testing structured norms, but
also shows that structure is not automatically decisive.  No Oseen
constant is inserted into a Yang--Mills bound.  The audit therefore
changes the next proof architecture, not any coefficient in the
theorem below.
\end{audit}

\subsection{Exhaustive exact direct boxes}

For \(m\in\{0,1,2,3\}^4\), put
\[
 q_m=\frac{\pi}{2}m\in\mathbb T^4.                       \tag{20.3}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Complete direct quarter-grid atlas for both signs]
\label{thm:newv20-direct-grid}
For every \(m\), both actions
\(e\in\{\mathrm W,\mathrm E\}\), and both signs
\(\sigma\in\{-1,1\}\),
\[
 \frac43H_e(k)+\sigma\nabla_1H_e(k)
 \succeq\delta_e^{\mathrm{dir}}I_{45}
 \quad\text{if}\quad
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k,q_m)\le\frac1{64},             \tag{20.4}
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
\delta_{\mathrm W}^{\mathrm{dir}}
&=\frac{784341753625211643}{245494101600257419520},\\
\delta_{\mathrm E}^{\mathrm{dir}}
&=\frac{1598228652728405643}{17905613826669929984}.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{20.5}
\]
The first floor is attained in the verified plus ledger at
quarter-turn units \((0,2,2,0)\), and the second at
\((3,2,2,2)\).  Thus the two V19 bottlenecks are also the exact
minimum-floor records of the complete plus ledger.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At all \(512\) action/centre pairs, construct the exact Laurent bank
(19.2)--(19.3), the exact Gaussian-rational centre matrix, and the
four exact centre derivatives.  Rationalize the proposed inverse
square root to denominator \(10^8\), then compute the congruence
Gershgorin floor \(\beta_{e,m}\), Frobenius metric ceiling
\(\gamma_{e,m}\), first stock \(L_{1,e,m}\), and coefficientwise
second stock \(L_{2,e,m}\) exactly as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv19-direct-variation}.  In every record,
\[
 \frac1{64}L_{1,e,m}
 +\frac1{2\cdot64^2}L_{2,e,m}
 \le\frac{\beta_{e,m}}2.                                 \tag{20.6}
\]
Consequently Lemma~\ref{lem:newv14-relative} gives the exact local
floor
\[
 \frac{\beta_{e,m}-
 64^{-1}L_{1,e,m}-(2\cdot64^2)^{-1}L_{2,e,m}}
 {\gamma_{e,m}}>0.                                       \tag{20.7}
\]
The radius distribution is particularly rigid:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
e&\text{plus centres}&\text{radius }1/64&
\min\text{ of (20.7)}\\ \hline
\mathrm W&256&256&\delta_{\mathrm W}^{\mathrm{dir}}\\
\mathrm E&256&256&\delta_{\mathrm E}^{\mathrm{dir}}.
\end{array}                                              \tag{20.8}
\]
Thus the minimum over each action is (20.5).  Torus inversion
permutes the quarter grid, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv4-inversion} transfers every plus box to a minus
box of the same radius and floor.  This proves (20.4).
\end{proof}

\begin{record}[V20 exact ledger]
\label{rec:newv20-ledger}
The ordered \(512\)-record ledger has SHA--256
\[
\texttt{E0C19B96EF5E6E72925BEE7628A4D5AADEE1EE4826C68231F7842A78816E4B0B}.
                                                               \tag{20.9}
\]
The executable recomputation is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v20_direct_quarter_grid_atlas.py}
\end{center}
and its compact canonical payload SHA--256 is
\[
\texttt{83F55B9265AC9DE9E8E914FB8B93C4524472074935EBC631FBB8343D82E7EA82}.
                                                               \tag{20.10}
\]
\end{record}

\begin{countertheorem}[The complete direct grid does not cover]
\label{ctr:newv20-no-cover}
For either action or sign, the boxes in
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv20-direct-grid} do not cover
\(\mathbb T^4\).  The point
\[
 k_\square=(\pi/4,\pi/4,\pi/4,\pi/4)                    \tag{20.11}
\]
is outside every box.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
In each coordinate, the circular distance from \(\pi/4\) to any
quarter-turn phase is at least \(\pi/4\).  Hence
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k_\square,q_m)
 \ge\frac{\pi}{4}>\frac34>\frac1{64}                    \tag{20.12}
\]
for every \(m\), where only \(\pi>3\) is used.  Equation (20.8)
shows that no direct box has a larger radius.
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[A complete diagnostic, not a scalable cover]
\label{ass:newv20}
V19 has been promoted from two bottleneck tests to all \(256\)
quarter-turn centres for each action.  The uniform radius does not
deteriorate, and the exact uniform floors remain controlled by the
known quarter-grid bottlenecks.  Nevertheless, \(1/64\) is smaller
than the quarter-cell inradius by a factor exceeding \(48\), and
Countertheorem~\ref{ctr:newv20-no-cover} rules out the direct
quarter-grid atlas.  Further uniform centre insertion would repeat
the same local estimate without addressing its coefficientwise
overestimate.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V21 replaces centre insertion by a structured cell test]
\label{dec:newv20-next}
Do not add another uniform layer of rational centres.  On the
Wilson bottleneck cell, split the retained fibre by the first parity
label,
\[
 \mathbb C^{45}=\mathbb C^{14}\oplus\mathbb C^{31},      \tag{20.13}
\]
and write the direct pencil as
\(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&X\\X^*&D\end{smallmatrix}\right)\).
Construct independent rational relative metrics for \(A\) and \(D\),
retain an exact cross-block norm, and test the sharp condition
\(\chi^2<\alpha\delta\) after outward whole-cell variation debits.
This is the V54 structural lesson proved afresh on the actual
Yang--Mills bank.

If the scalar cross test fails, retain the exact Schur complement
and seek a trigonometric interval or sum-of-squares certificate on
that whole quarter cell; do not infer nonpositivity from failure of
a sufficient bound.  If an actual negative direction appears,
record its exact rational or algebraic witness and move upstream
again.  Only a successful whole-cell mechanism should then be
transported across the finite symmetry orbit.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V20]
\label{fire:newv20-final}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv20-direct-grid} is an exhaustive local theorem,
and Countertheorem~\ref{ctr:newv20-no-cover} is an exact obstruction
to that atlas.  Neither is a full-torus covariant Loewner inequality.
No cofinal regulator-uniform response sign, interacting
four-dimensional Yang--Mills measure, reflection-positive continuum
limit, exponential clustering, or Hamiltonian mass gap has been
proved.  The full Yang--Mills mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V21: exact spectral test of the first-parity block route}
\label{sec:newv21-parity-block}

V20 identified a natural \(14\oplus31\) split, but the companion
lesson was explicitly conditional on a sharp cross-block norm.  This
section computes that norm rather than silently replacing it by a
Frobenius estimate.

\subsection{Block normalization and the cross operator}

Let \(I_0\) and \(I_1\) be the retained coordinates whose first
parity labels are respectively zero and one.  The exact retained
ledger gives
\[
 |I_0|=14,\qquad |I_1|=31.                               \tag{21.1}
\]
At either bottleneck \(a_e\) in (19.6), write
\[
 P_e^+(a_e)=
 \begin{pmatrix}A_e&X_e\\X_e^*&D_e\end{pmatrix}
 \quad\text{on}\quad
 \mathbb C^{I_0}\oplus\mathbb C^{I_1}.                   \tag{21.2}
\]
Rational inverse-square-root proposals \(R_{A,e},R_{D,e}\), rounded
to denominator \(10^8\), give exact centre floors
\[
\begin{array}{c|cc}
e&\beta_{A,e}&\beta_{D,e}\\ \hline
\mathrm W&
\dfrac{703124894829679}{703125000000000}&
\dfrac{3749999589355439}{3750000000000000}\\[5pt]
\mathrm E&
\dfrac{44999970678728203}{45000000000000000}&
\dfrac{1874999311917883}{1875000000000000}.
\end{array}                                              \tag{21.3}
\]
Thus
\[
 R_{A,e}^*A_eR_{A,e}\succeq\beta_{A,e}I,\qquad
 R_{D,e}^*D_eR_{D,e}\succeq\beta_{D,e}I.                 \tag{21.4}
\]
Put
\[
 Y_e=R_{A,e}^*X_eR_{D,e},\qquad
 \theta_e=\beta_{A,e}\beta_{D,e}.                        \tag{21.5}
\]

\begin{countertheorem}[Frobenius cross control fails at the centres]
\label{ctr:newv21-frobenius}
For the rational metrics in (21.3),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|Y_{\mathrm W}\|_{\mathrm F}^2
&=\frac{16934139920425661060028053389573249}
{4050000000000000000000000000000000}>4,\\
\|Y_{\mathrm E}\|_{\mathrm F}^2
&=\frac{93023579647392954713744598763113083}
{16200000000000000000000000000000000}>5.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{21.6}
\]
Since \(\theta_e<1\), the sufficient test
\(\|Y_e\|_{\mathrm F}^2<\theta_e\) fails for both actions even at
the exact centre.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
The transformed cross blocks have rational real and imaginary
parts.  Exact summation of their entrywise squared moduli gives
(21.6); the two displayed inequalities follow by cross
multiplication.  Every fraction in (21.3) is strictly smaller than
one, so \(\theta_e<1\).
\end{proof}

This is a failure of a proposed sufficient bound, not a negative
direction of \(P_e^+(a_e)\).  The operator norm is much smaller.

\begin{theorem}[Exact spectral cross certificate]
\label{thm:newv21-cross}
There are exact rational numbers \(\eta_e>0\) such that
\[
 \theta_eI_{31}-Y_e^*Y_e\succeq\eta_eI_{31}.             \tag{21.7}
\]
Consequently the following rational operator-norm ceilings are
valid:
\[
 \|Y_{\mathrm W}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
 \le\frac{3968788643}{4000000000},\qquad
 \|Y_{\mathrm E}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
 \le\frac{992343241371}{1000000000000}.                  \tag{21.8}
\]
Both ceilings have square strictly below \(\theta_e\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Form \(Y_e^*Y_e\) by exact integer matrix multiplication.  An
approximate eigenbasis of the complement in (21.7) is rounded to
denominator \(10^8\); exact congruence, rational rowwise
absolute-value ceilings, and the Gram-residual debit give
\[
\begin{aligned}
\eta_{\mathrm W}
&=\frac{
2518215107882458987558861850113685589483549667487}
{162000026926182000000000000000000000000000000000000},\\
\eta_{\mathrm E}
&=\frac{
197690221450599557009085965646345057843172208341}
{12960002010873600000000000000000000000000000000000}.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{21.9}
\]
They are positive integers divided by positive integers, proving
(21.7).  Thus
\(\|Y_e\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\le\theta_e-\eta_e\).
Exact rational square-root ceilings give (21.8), and direct
cross multiplication verifies that their squares remain below
\(\theta_e\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{The structured local radii}

For \(B\in\{A,D,X\}\), let \(L_{1,B,e}\) and \(L_{2,B,e}\) be the
exact transformed derivative and coefficientwise remainder stocks
defined blockwise as in (19.4), using the two metrics in (21.3).
For a coordinate radius \(r\), put
\[
\begin{aligned}
\rho_{B,e}(r)&=rL_{1,B,e}+\frac{r^2}{2}L_{2,B,e},\\
\alpha_e(r)&=\beta_{A,e}-\rho_{A,e}(r),\\
\delta_e(r)&=\beta_{D,e}-\rho_{D,e}(r),\\
\chi_e(r)&=\bar\chi_e+\rho_{X,e}(r),
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{21.10}
\]
where \(\bar\chi_e\) is the corresponding rational ceiling in
(21.8).

\begin{theorem}[Exact first-parity structured patches]
\label{thm:newv21-patches}
The block test
\[
 \alpha_e(r)>0,\qquad \delta_e(r)>0,\qquad
 \Delta_e(r):=\alpha_e(r)\delta_e(r)-\chi_e(r)^2>0        \tag{21.11}
\]
holds at
\[
 r_{\mathrm W}=\frac1{1024},\qquad
 r_{\mathrm E}=\frac1{2048}.                             \tag{21.12}
\]
It gives the ambient direct-pencil floors
\[
\begin{aligned}
\varepsilon_{\mathrm W}^{\mathrm{blk}}
&=\frac{
8328719916309382394450769637859767253549}
{787203671843608859956532949588308142334083072},\\
\varepsilon_{\mathrm E}^{\mathrm{blk}}
&=\frac{
254540470539060806384759803567978336748237}
{127818027550844902811784816214464198676316160}.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{21.13}
\]
The immediately larger dyadic radii fail this particular sufficient
test, since
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Delta_{\mathrm W}(1/512)
&=-\frac{
10249101506437875464328675152159182483411}
{724775731200000000000000000000000000000000}<0,\\
\Delta_{\mathrm E}(1/1024)
&=-\frac{
12460985507263452906361851862085525416243}
{2899102924800000000000000000000000000000000}<0.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{21.14}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Taylor's formula applied separately to the three transformed block
banks gives (21.10) as outward bounds.  For vectors \(u,v\), the
quadratic form of the transformed pencil is bounded below by the
scalar matrix
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}\alpha_e(r)&-\chi_e(r)\\
 -\chi_e(r)&\delta_e(r)\end{pmatrix}.                    \tag{21.15}
\]
All stocks and all values in (21.10) are rational.  Exact evaluation
at (21.12) gives positive diagonal entries and determinant margins
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Delta_{\mathrm W}(1/1024)
&=\frac{
8328719916309382394450769637859767253549}
{11596411699200000000000000000000000000000000},\\
\Delta_{\mathrm E}(1/2048)
&=\frac{
254540470539060806384759803567978336748237}
{46385646796800000000000000000000000000000000}.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{21.16}
\]
The smaller eigenvalue of (21.15) is at least
\(\Delta_e(r)/(\alpha_e(r)+\delta_e(r))\), because its larger
eigenvalue is at most the trace.  Dividing this relative floor by
the exact Frobenius-square ceiling for
\(\operatorname{diag}(R_{A,e},R_{D,e})\) yields (21.13).
The same exact evaluation at the next larger dyadic radii gives
(21.14).
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[The first-parity scalar block route is noncompetitive]
\label{ass:newv21}
The operator certificate (21.7) proves that the large Frobenius
values in (21.6) are caused by harmless singular directions, not by
centre indefiniteness.  Nevertheless the true normalized cross norm
is within eight thousandths of one.  Its small determinant slack
makes the structured radius sixteen times smaller than the direct
Wilson radius and thirty-two times smaller than the direct endpoint
radius in V20.  The first-parity two-block scalar test therefore
amplifies, rather than resolves, the whole-cell bottleneck.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V22 keeps the full near-null cancellation]
\label{dec:newv21-next}
Close the \(14\oplus31\) scalar block route; do not populate it over
the quarter grid.  Return to the full rational metric of V19--V20,
which adapts to the coupled near-null eigenvector.  On the Wilson
bottleneck quarter cell, express the degree-one Laurent bank in
cell-centred sine and cosine variables and construct an exact
matrix-valued interval or Bernstein enclosure that keeps
coefficient signs and cancellations.  First test faces and edges to
locate the true lower-edge geometry.  If a full-cell enclosure is
too coarse, subdivide only along a certified offending direction,
not uniformly in four dimensions.
\end{decision}

\section{V22: a rational active-phase soft-mode patch}
\label{sec:newv22-soft-mode}

The first-parity split in V21 cut across the weak eigenvector and
therefore left a normalized cross norm near one.  We now align the
split with the actual soft eigendirection.  A numerical line search
is used only to propose the following exceptionally simple rational
phase; every accepted statement is then checked exactly.

\subsection{An exact centre inside the bottleneck cell}

Put
\[
 z_\circ=\frac{55-48i}{73}=e^{i\vartheta_\circ},
 \qquad
 a_\circ=(\vartheta_\circ,\pi,\pi,0).                    \tag{22.1}
\]

\begin{lemma}[Exact phase and cell membership]
\label{lem:newv22-phase}
The point \(z_\circ\) lies on the unit circle,
\[
 \tan\frac{\vartheta_\circ}{2}=-\frac38,                 \tag{22.2}
\]
and \(-\pi/4<\vartheta_\circ<0\).  Hence \(a_\circ\) lies
inside the Wilson bottleneck quarter cell centred at
\((0,\pi,\pi,0)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The identity \(55^2+48^2=73^2\) proves unit modulus.  The
half-angle identity gives
\[
 \tan\frac{\vartheta_\circ}{2}
 =\frac{-48/73}{1+55/73}=-\frac38.
\]
Also \(\tan(\pi/8)=\sqrt2-1>3/8\), since
\(\sqrt2>11/8\).  Monotonicity of tangent on
\((-\pi/2,\pi/2)\) proves the angle bound.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A rational soft/hard whitening basis}

Evaluate the exact direct Wilson bank (19.2) at \(a_\circ\).
A floating eigendecomposition is used only to propose the matrix
\[
 T_\circ=U\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_j^{-1/2}).
                                                               \tag{22.3}
\]
Round every real and imaginary entry to denominator \(10^8\), with
the first column corresponding to the proposed lowest mode.  All
subsequent operations use this rational matrix.  On
\(\mathbb C\oplus\mathbb C^{44}\), write
\[
 T_\circ^*P_{\mathrm W}^+(a_\circ)T_\circ
 =
 \begin{pmatrix}A_\circ&X_\circ\\
 X_\circ^*&D_\circ\end{pmatrix}.                         \tag{22.4}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact soft/hard centre separation]
\label{thm:newv22-centre}
The rational congruence in (22.4) obeys
\[
\begin{aligned}
 A_\circ&\succeq
 \frac{22500000020087887}{22500000000000000},\\
 D_\circ&\succeq
 \frac{9999995652975553}{10000000000000000}I_{44},\\
 \|X_\circ\|_{\mathrm{op}}
 &=\|X_\circ\|_{\mathrm F}
 \le\frac{40403}{1000000000000}.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{22.5}
\]
For the scalar soft block, the four exact transformed
first-derivative ceilings are
\[
 \frac{20263}{500000000000},\quad
 \frac{471}{31250000000},\quad
 \frac{1973}{250000000000},\quad
 \frac{2297}{1000000000000},                             \tag{22.6}
\]
and their sum is
\[
 L_{1,A}=\frac{65787}{1000000000000}.                    \tag{22.7}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At (22.1), every active phase is in
\(\mathbb Q(i)\) and every transverse phase is a fourth root of
unity.  Thus the centre, its derivatives, \(T_\circ\), and the
congruence are rational complex matrices.  The first line of (22.5)
is the exact scalar entry.  Exact Gershgorin bounds the hard block.
Because \(X_\circ\) is a \(1\times44\) row, its operator norm equals
its Frobenius norm; exact squared-modulus summation gives
\[
 \|X_\circ\|_{\mathrm F}^2
 =
 \frac{26444163219241159447}
 {16200000000000000000000000000000000},                 \tag{22.8}
\]
whose rational square-root ceiling is the last line of (22.5).
The same exact matrix multiplication applied to the four derivative
banks gives (22.6), and addition gives (22.7).
\end{proof}

The extremely small number in (22.7) is not assumed to vanish; its
full rational debit is retained.

\subsection{The enlarged exact direct box}

Define the soft, hard, and cross variation stocks exactly as in
(21.10), now for the \(1\oplus44\) blocks of
\(T_\circ^*P_{\mathrm W}^+(k)T_\circ\).  Their coefficientwise
second stocks are
\[
\begin{aligned}
 L_{2,A}&=\frac{4371508183219}{31250000000},\\
 L_{2,D}&=\frac{34502716039487}{500000000000},\\
 L_{2,X}&=\frac{4155170500191}{50000000000},
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{22.9}
\]
while the hard and cross first stocks are
\[
 L_{1,D}=\frac{6207946854329}{500000000000},\qquad
 L_{1,X}=\frac{6398582104517}{500000000000}.             \tag{22.10}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact radius-\(1/32\) soft-mode patch]
\label{thm:newv22-patch}
If
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k,a_\circ)\le\frac1{32},         \tag{22.11}
\]
then
\[
 P_{\mathrm W}^+(k)\succeq
 \frac{
317746264500752482094900600747135931}
{282781631694743907632663152068097499200}\,I_{45}.
                                                               \tag{22.12}
\]
The floor is approximately \(0.001123645346398\).
The immediately larger dyadic radius \(1/16\) fails this particular
soft/hard sufficient test by the exact determinant margin
\[
 -\frac{
49585046323542268077015630163608809}
{57600000000000000000000000000000000}<0.                \tag{22.13}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For \(r=1/32\), use (22.5)--(22.10) and the blockwise version of
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv19-direct-variation}.  After all outward debits,
the scalar comparison matrix has entries
\[
\begin{aligned}
\alpha&=\frac{335410266050695567}{360000000000000000},\\
\delta&=\frac{370117650092596017}{640000000000000000},\\
\chi&=\frac{45106100106367}{102400000000000}.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{22.14}
\]
Its exact determinant is
\[
 \Delta=
 \frac{
105915421500250827364966866915711977}
{307200000000000000000000000000000000}>0.               \tag{22.15}
\]
Therefore the transformed pencil has floor at least
\(\Delta/(\alpha+\delta)\).  The rational transform obeys
\[
 T_\circ^*T_\circ\preceq
 \frac{508008100666825753}{2500000000000000}I,           \tag{22.16}
\]
by its exact Frobenius-square ceiling.  Dividing the relative floor
by (22.16) and reducing gives (22.12).  Repeating the same rational
substitution at \(r=1/16\) gives (22.13).
\end{proof}

\[

                                                               \tag{22.17}
\]

&\texttt{5EE01FD83D58B8EB232CADA3EE0785E7727F0E4D83F54A3EF0CE9FFF33CDEE7B},\\
&\texttt{4F17F60F26988CAC0B458E4398D13C5E60D29F95DFFF5BB2ABE9877B37ACFD59},\\
&\texttt{80D99A5CAA472FC65745BAD160D90F52D888928F6AD60355FB59FD7C7956375E}.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{22.18}
\]
The executable verifier is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v22_soft_mode_rational_phase_patch.py}
\end{center}
and has canonical payload SHA--256
\[
\texttt{A474617126731474FB883EC3BC9485259D68FD7AE3B27DE9891DB6DEE7D9B56B}.
                                                               \tag{22.19}
\]

\begin{assessment}[The useful structure is spectral, not combinatorial]
\label{ass:newv22}
Aligning one block with the near-null mode reduces the centre cross
norm from approximately \(0.992\) in V21 to less than
\(4.041\times10^{-8}\).  The resulting certified radius is twice
the full-metric V20 radius and thirty-two times the V21 Wilson
parity-block radius.  The gain is exact and preserves the full
coupled soft direction.  It still covers only a small part of one
quarter cell; (22.13) identifies cross/hard variation, rather than
the soft first derivative, as the next debit.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V23 makes the soft-mode patch anisotropic]
\label{dec:newv22-next}
Retain the rational centre and whitening transform of V22.  Replace
the summed isotropic stocks in (22.9)--(22.10) by four separate
coordinate ledgers and solve the exact box condition for
\((r_1,r_2,r_3,r_4)\).  In particular, distinguish motion along the
already-controlled active circle from the three transverse
directions.  Test whether the patch can span the active-coordinate
width of the quarter cell while maintaining a materially larger
transverse radius.

If the hard/cross debit still prevents this, use the exact
degree-one active-circle block certificate from V10 as the base
operator and apply the soft/hard Schur argument only to transverse
variation.  This reuses the proved circle theorem and does not
reintroduce the V11--V12 moving-splitting assumptions.
\end{decision}

The rational phase (22.1) was proposed by numerical reconnaissance;

No cofinal regulator-uniform response sign, interacting
