\section{V23: exact anisotropic profiles of the soft-mode patch}
\label{sec:newv23-anisotropic}

V22 used one common radius in all four momentum coordinates.  The
same exact ledgers permit a genuinely anisotropic test without
recomputing or weakening the centre congruence.

\subsection{An anisotropic coefficientwise lemma}

For one of the soft, hard, or cross blocks \(B\), let
\(M_{B,\alpha}\) be its transformed Laurent coefficient and let
\(L_{1,B,j}\) be the exact Frobenius ceiling for its \(j\)-th centre
derivative.  Given radii \(r=(r_1,r_2,r_3,r_4)\), define
\[
 \rho_B(r)
 =
 \sum_{j=1}^4r_jL_{1,B,j}
 +\frac12\sum_\alpha
 \left(\sum_{j=1}^4|\alpha_j|r_j\right)^2
 \|M_{B,\alpha}\|_{\mathrm F}.                           \tag{23.1}
\]

\begin{lemma}[Exact anisotropic block variation]
\label{lem:newv23-anisotropic}
On the torus box
\[
 |k_j-(a_\circ)_j|_{\mathbb T}\le r_j
 \qquad(1\le j\le4),                                    \tag{23.2}
\]
the soft and hard diagonal blocks lose at most
\(\rho_A(r)\) and \(\rho_D(r)\) in Loewner order, while the
cross-block operator norm increases by at most \(\rho_X(r)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Use the straight path from \(a_\circ\) to \(k\).  The linear term is
bounded coordinate by coordinate.  For exponent \(\alpha\), the
second directional derivative carries
\((\alpha\cdot u)^2\), and
\[
 |\alpha\cdot u|
 \le\sum_j|\alpha_j|r_j.                                 \tag{23.3}
\]
Taylor's integral remainder, the triangle inequality, and
\(\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm F}\) give (23.1).
All quantities are rational because the centre phase and transform
are rational.
\end{proof}

Retain the centre quantities from (22.5), and put
\[
\begin{aligned}
\alpha(r)&=\beta_A-\rho_A(r),\\
\delta(r)&=\beta_D-\rho_D(r),\\
\chi(r)&=\bar\chi_\circ+\rho_X(r),\\
\Delta(r)&=\alpha(r)\delta(r)-\chi(r)^2,
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{23.4}
\]
where \(\bar\chi_\circ=40403/10^{12}\).

\subsection{Exact profile table}

\begin{theorem}[Active-versus-transverse dyadic profile]
\label{thm:newv23-profiles}
Fix an active radius \(r_1\) and set
\(r_2=r_3=r_4=r_\perp\).  The largest dyadic
\(r_\perp\) accepted by (23.4), when candidates are tested from one
downward, is
\[
\begin{array}{c|cccccc}
r_1&0&1/256&1/128&1/64&1/32&1/16\\ \hline
r_\perp&1/32&1/32&1/32&1/32&1/32&1/64.
\end{array}                                              \tag{23.5}
\]
For every column, the immediately larger dyadic transverse radius
is rejected by an exact nonpositive diagonal or determinant test.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Substitute the three exact coefficient ledgers of (22.18) into
(23.1)--(23.4).  For example, at \(r_1=0\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Delta(0,1/32,1/32,1/32)
&=\frac{
2970561274886279168299116568782874757}
{4915200000000000000000000000000000000}>0,\\
\Delta(0,1/16,1/16,1/16)
&=-\frac{
10656379028214220872127970504291569}
{921600000000000000000000000000000000}<0.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{23.6}
\]
At the widest accepted active radius,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Delta(1/16,1/64,1/64,1/64)
&=\frac{
62526492147003002819185737176961427711}
{235929600000000000000000000000000000000}>0,\\
\Delta(1/16,1/32,1/32,1/32)
&=-\frac{
74928951004256430657600634547135081}
{1638400000000000000000000000000000000}<0.
\end{aligned}                                            \tag{23.7}
\]
The other four columns are identical exact rational substitutions.
In each accepted case the diagonal quantities in (23.4) are also
positive.  The scalar \(2\times2\) comparison from (21.15) therefore
proves the corresponding box.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[One-coordinate dyadic profile]
\label{thm:newv23-axes}
If only one coordinate moves, the largest accepted dyadic radii for
the four zero-based axes \(0,1,2,3\) are respectively
\[
 \frac1{16},\qquad\frac18,\qquad\frac18,\qquad\frac18.    \tag{23.8}
\]
The immediately larger dyadic radius is rejected in every case.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Set the other three radii to zero in (23.1).  Exact substitution
gives positive diagonal entries and determinant at the four radii
in (23.8).  At \(1/8\) in the active direction the determinant is
\[
 -\frac{
9583925117820798107874542385074603}
{19200000000000000000000000000000000}<0.                \tag{23.9}
\]
For each transverse direction, the candidate \(1/4\) has a negative
diagonal or determinant.  These rational sign checks are included

\end{proof}

Each transverse axis separately reaches \(1/8\), four times the

\section{V24: a matrix-level transverse vertex hull}
\label{sec:newv24-vertex-hull}

The V23 loss came from bounding mixed transverse motion term by
term.  Replacing Fourier terms by separate entry intervals is still
too destructive: that test certifies only radius \(1/64\).  The
correct finite object is the whole transformed matrix polynomial.

\subsection{The grouped multi-affine polynomial}

Keep the exact active phase and rational transform of V22.  For
transverse displacements \(u=(u_2,u_3,u_4)\), put
\[
 c_j=\cos u_j,\qquad s_j=\sin u_j.                       \tag{24.1}
\]
Because every transverse Laurent exponent is in
\(\{-1,0,1\}\), exact expansion of
\[
 e^{i\alpha_ju_j}=
 \begin{cases}
 1,&\alpha_j=0,\\
 c_j+i\alpha_js_j,&\alpha_j=\pm1
 \end{cases}                                             \tag{24.2}
\]
and collection of equal real monomials gives
\[
 {\cal P}(c_2,s_2,c_3,s_3,c_4,s_4)
 =T_\circ^*P_{\mathrm W}^+
  (\vartheta_\circ,\pi+u_2,\pi+u_3,u_4)T_\circ.          \tag{24.3}
\]
There are \(19\) nonzero grouped monomials.  Each matrix coefficient
is exactly Hermitian, and \({\cal P}\) is affine separately in each
of its six real arguments.

\begin{lemma}[Multi-affine matrix vertex hull]
\label{lem:newv24-hull}
Let \(F\) be Hermitian and affine separately in \(n\) real variables
on a rectangle \(I_1\times\cdots\times I_n\).  If
\[
 F(v)\succeq\eta I
 \quad\text{at every one of the }2^n\text{ vertices }v,  \tag{24.4}
\]
then the same inequality holds throughout the rectangle.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In one variable, affine interpolation writes an interior value as a
convex combination of the two endpoint values.  Iterate this
identity over the \(n\) variables.  The resulting \(2^n\)
coefficients are nonnegative and sum to one.  The Loewner cone is
convex, proving the claim.
\end{proof}

For \(0<r\le1/2\) and \(|u_j|\le r\),
\[
 1-\frac{r^2}{2}\le c_j\le1,\qquad -r\le s_j\le r,       \tag{24.5}
\]
using \(\cos x\ge1-x^2/2\) and \(|\sin x|\le|x|\).
Thus the actual transverse trigonometric image is contained in the
rational rectangle
\[
 {\cal R}_r=
 [1-r^2/2,1]^3\times[-r,r]^3.                           \tag{24.6}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact radius-\(1/8\) transverse hull]
\label{thm:newv24-hull}
At the rational active phase \(e^{i\vartheta_\circ}=(55-48i)/73\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W}^+
 (\vartheta_\circ,\pi+u_2,\pi+u_3,u_4)
 \succeq
 \frac{
318377895044572507764781089152663}
{182882963719022687826228374163120000}\,I_{45}           \tag{24.7}
\]
whenever
\[
 |u_2|,|u_3|,|u_4|\le\frac18.                           \tag{24.8}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At \(r=1/8\), the rectangle (24.6) has \(64\) rational vertices.
Evaluate the complete matrix (24.3) at each vertex.  A rationally
rounded approximate eigenbasis, exact congruence, exact rowwise
absolute-value ceilings, and the Gram-residual debit certify all
\(64\) matrices as positive definite.  Their common relative floor
is
\[
 \eta_{1/8}=
 \frac{
318377895044572507764781089152663}
{900000233652600000000000000000000}.                    \tag{24.9}
\]
The weakest relaxation vertex has
\[
 (c_2,s_2,c_3,s_3,c_4,s_4)
 =(1,1/8,1,-1/8,1,-1/8).                                \tag{24.10}
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv24-hull} proves the same relative floor on all of
\({\cal R}_{1/8}\), hence on the trigonometric subset by (24.5).
Finally use the exact metric ceiling (22.16) to convert (24.9) to
the ambient floor (24.7).
\end{proof}

\begin{countertheorem}[The radius-\(1/4\) outer hull is indefinite]
\label{ctr:newv24-quarter-relaxation}
The same rational outer-rectangle certificate cannot work at
\(r=1/4\).  Its vertex
\[
 (c_2,s_2,c_3,s_3,c_4,s_4)
 =(1,-1/4,1,-1/4,1,-1/4)                               \tag{24.11}
\]
has a Gaussian-integer vector \(w\ne0\) for which
\[
 w^*{\cal P}(1,-1/4,1,-1/4,1,-1/4)w
 =
 -\frac{41485168722284022969241500905167}
 {15000000000000000}<0.                                 \tag{24.12}
\]
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
Round a numerical negative eigenvector only to propose \(w\), then
evaluate the quadratic form using exact rational arithmetic.
Equation (24.12) proves the sign without floating-point input.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Relaxation boundary]
\label{rem:newv24-relaxation}
The vertex (24.11) is not a physical trigonometric point:

radius-\(1/4\) outer rectangle, not positivity of the Yang--Mills
pencil on the true transverse box.
\end{remark}

\begin{assessment}[What the audit changes]
\label{ass:newv25-audit}
SM--Einstein V76 preserves its V75 audit of Yang--Mills V24 and
imports only the multi-affine vertex-hull method.  It constructs a
conditional finite reflection-factorized adjacent law from a
positive kinetic kernel, while explicitly separating a finite graph
connectivity gap from a continuum mass gap.  Its earlier
soft-mode/Mosco analysis also shows why pointwise or merely strong
resolvent convergence cannot prevent low-energy modes from escaping:
a compact physical graph-screen, norm-resolvent control near the
isolated ground sector, and cofinal tightness remain separate future
obligations.  None of its numerical constants acts on the present
Yang--Mills fibre.

Navier--Stokes V26 is unchanged and supplies no new fibre or
reflection-positive input.  Thus the correct immediate adjustment
is to complete the finite whole-pencil coverage problem, preserving
mixed cancellation, while adding the compact-screen and cofinal
transfer rows explicitly to the later continuum ledger.  No
companion theorem is imported as if it proved either row.
\end{assessment}

\subsection{The eight-variable exact polynomial}

Let
\[
 a_\circ=(\vartheta_\circ,\pi,\pi,0),\qquad
 e^{i\vartheta_\circ}=\frac{55-48i}{73},                 \tag{25.3}
\]
as in (22.1), and write
\[
 a_\circ+u
 =(\vartheta_\circ+u_1,\pi+u_2,\pi+u_3,u_4),\qquad
 c_j=\cos u_j,\quad s_j=\sin u_j.                        \tag{25.4}
\]
The active phase is represented exactly by
\[
 e^{i(\vartheta_\circ+u_1)}
 =\frac{55-48i}{73}(c_1+is_1),                           \tag{25.5}
\]
and the other three phases are expanded as in (24.2), including
their two minus signs at the transverse \(\pi\)-coordinates.
Collecting equal real monomials in the complete direct Wilson bank
defines
\[
 {\cal P}_4(c_1,s_1,\ldots,c_4,s_4)
 :=T_\circ^*P_{\mathrm W}^+(a_\circ+u)T_\circ.           \tag{25.6}
\]
Every Laurent exponent of this bank belongs to
\(\{-1,0,1\}^4\).  Consequently (25.6) has \(33\) nonzero grouped
Hermitian coefficient matrices and is affine separately in each of
its eight real arguments.  This is an identity of matrix
polynomials, not a Taylor approximation.

For radii \({\bf r}=(r_1,r_2,r_3,r_4)\), \(0<r_j\le1/2\), set
\[
 {\cal R}_{\bf r}:=
 \prod_{j=1}^4
 \left([1-r_j^2/2,1]\times[-r_j,r_j]\right).             \tag{25.7}
\]
The elementary inequalities used in (24.5) give
\[
 |u_j|\le r_j\ (1\le j\le4)
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 (c_1,s_1,\ldots,c_4,s_4)\in{\cal R}_{\bf r}.            \tag{25.8}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact four-dimensional radius-\(1/8\) Wilson patch]
\label{thm:newv25-four-hull}
If
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k,a_\circ)\le\frac18,            \tag{25.9}
\]
then
\[
 P_{\mathrm W}^+(k)\succeq
 \frac{
1386273667756962907147116839395543}
{5852254866449941826427421384231680000}\,I_{45}.        \tag{25.10}
\]
The same floor holds for \(P_{\mathrm W}^-(k)\) whenever
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k,-a_\circ)\le\frac18.           \tag{25.11}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Take \(r_1=r_2=r_3=r_4=1/8\) in (25.7).  The resulting rational
rectangle has \(2^8=256\) vertices.  Evaluate the full matrix
polynomial (25.6) at every vertex.  At each vertex, a numerical
eigenbasis is used only to propose a rational congruence; the
congruenced entries, rowwise absolute-value ceilings, and
Gram-residual debit are then evaluated in exact rational arithmetic.
All \(256\) matrices pass.  Their common floor in the
\(T_\circ\)-coordinates is
\[
 \eta_4=
 \frac{
1386273667756962907147116839395543}
{28800007611926400000000000000000000}.                  \tag{25.12}
\]
The weakest relaxation vertex has
\[
 (c_1,s_1,c_2,s_2,c_3,s_3,c_4,s_4)
 =(1,-1/8,1,1/8,1,-1/8,1,-1/8).                        \tag{25.13}
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv24-hull}, now with \(n=8\), writes every
interior matrix as a convex combination of these \(256\) matrices.
Equations (25.8) and (25.12) therefore give
\[
 T_\circ^*P_{\mathrm W}^+(k)T_\circ\succeq\eta_4I_{45}
\]
throughout the physical box (25.9).

The exact metric ceiling remains
\[
 T_\circ^*T_\circ\preceq
 \gamma_\circ I_{45},\qquad
 \gamma_\circ=
 \frac{508008100666825753}{2500000000000000},           \tag{25.14}
\]
by (22.16).  For \(x=T_\circ y\),
\(\|x\|^2\le\gamma_\circ\|y\|^2\); hence the ambient floor is
\(\eta_4/\gamma_\circ\), which reduces exactly to (25.10).
Finally (4.4) gives
\(P_{\mathrm W}^-(k)=\overline{P_{\mathrm W}^+(-k)}\).
Complex conjugation preserves the Loewner floor, proving (25.11).
\end{proof}

\begin{countertheorem}[The next active outer hull is indefinite]
\label{ctr:newv25-active-quarter}
Keep \(r_2=r_3=r_4=1/8\) but increase only the active radius to
\(r_1=1/4\).  The outer rectangle (25.7) has the vertex
\[
 (1,-1/4,1,1/8,1,1/8,1,1/8),                           \tag{25.15}
\]
at which a nonzero Gaussian-integer vector \(w\) satisfies
\[
 w^*{\cal P}_4(1,-1/4,1,1/8,1,1/8,1,1/8)w
 =
 -\frac{18333215957636917781923929482338187}
 {2880000000000000000}<0.                               \tag{25.16}
\]
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
An approximate lowest eigenvector proposes \(w\).  Clearing
denominators gives a Gaussian-integer vector, after which direct
evaluation of (25.6) gives the exact rational number (25.16).
Thus the sign conclusion contains no floating-point premise.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Outer-hull failure is not physical negativity]
\label{rem:newv25-relaxation}
The first pair in (25.15) obeys
\(1^2+(1/4)^2>1\), and the other nonzero sine pairs likewise lie off
their unit circles.  Countertheorem~\ref{ctr:newv25-active-quarter}
therefore rejects this anisotropic rational relaxation only.  It
does not exhibit a physical momentum at which the Wilson pencil is
negative.
\end{remark}

\section{V26: an independently certified transverse-permutation atlas}
\label{sec:newv26-permutation-atlas}

The first transport test should not assume that the spanning-tree
reduction makes coordinate symmetry manifest.  We therefore rebuild
the rational metric and the complete vertex certificate separately
at each transverse permutation of \((\pi,\pi,0)\).

Put
\[
 {\cal B}:=\{(2,2,0),(2,0,2),(0,2,2)\}\subset(\mathbb Z/4)^3
                                                               \tag{26.1}
\]
and, for \(b=(b_2,b_3,b_4)\in{\cal B}\), define
\[
 a_b:=
 \left(\vartheta_\circ,\frac{\pi b_2}{2},
       \frac{\pi b_3}{2},\frac{\pi b_4}{2}\right).       \tag{26.2}
\]
Let
\[
 {\cal C}_+:=\{a_b:b\in{\cal B}\},\qquad
 {\cal C}_-:=-{\cal C}_+.                               \tag{26.3}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact three-centre radius-\(1/8\) atlas]
\label{thm:newv26-atlas}
For every \(k\in\mathbb T^4\),
\[
 \min_{a\in{\cal C}_+}d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k,a)
 \le\frac18
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 P_{\mathrm W}^+(k)\succeq\kappa_{26}I_{45},            \tag{26.4}
\]
and
\[
 \min_{a\in{\cal C}_-}d_{\infty,\mathbb T^4}(k,a)
 \le\frac18
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 P_{\mathrm W}^-(k)\succeq\kappa_{26}I_{45},            \tag{26.5}
\]
where the common exact floor is
\[
 \kappa_{26}:=
 \frac{
1386273667756962907147116839395543}
{5852254866449941826427421384231680000}.                \tag{26.6}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix \(b\in{\cal B}\).  The four centre phases consist of
\((55-48i)/73\) and three fourth roots of unity, so evaluating the
direct Wilson bank at \(a_b\) is exact over \(\mathbb Q(i)\).
Construct from that centre matrix its own denominator-\(10^8\)
rational whitening transform \(T_b\).  Expanding
\[
 T_b^*P_{\mathrm W}^+(a_b+u)T_b                         \tag{26.7}
\]
in the eight variables
\((\cos u_1,\sin u_1,\ldots,\cos u_4,\sin u_4)\) gives
\(33\) grouped Hermitian monomials and is separately affine in all
eight variables.

For each \(b\), apply the outer rectangle (25.7) with all radii
\(1/8\).  All \(256\) rational vertices pass the exact congruence,
Gershgorin, and Gram-residual certificate.  The resulting ambient
floors are
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
b&\text{ambient floor}\\ \hline
(2,2,0)&
\displaystyle
\frac{1386273667756962907147116839395543}
{5852254866449941826427421384231680000}\\[6pt]
(2,0,2)&
\displaystyle
\frac{363114106940663297669438573785959}
{84325586281455592887485070419200000}\\[6pt]
(0,2,2)&
\displaystyle
\frac{10686925870126287652341451305449527}
{1526609173117580866185879818707200000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{26.8}
\]
Exact cross multiplication shows that the first entry is the
smallest, giving (26.4)--(26.6).  This accounts for
\(3\cdot256=768\) independent vertex matrices; no coordinate
permutation identity is assumed.  Equation (4.4) then transports the
plus certificate at \(-k\) to the minus pencil at \(k\), proving
(26.5).
\end{proof}

\begin{countertheorem}[The three-centre atlas does not cover]
\label{ctr:newv26-no-cover}
Neither sign-specific atlas in Theorem~\ref{thm:newv26-atlas}
covers its whole momentum torus.  In particular,
\[
 q_+:=(\vartheta_\circ,0,0,0)                            \tag{26.9}
\]
belongs to none of the plus boxes, and
\(q_-:=-q_+\) belongs to none of the minus boxes.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
Every element of \({\cal B}\) has exactly two entries equal to \(2\).
Thus \(q_+\) differs from each \(a_b\) by the torus distance \(\pi\)
in two transverse coordinates.  Since \(\pi>3>1/8\), its
sup-distance from every plus centre is greater than \(1/8\).
Negation is an isometry of \(\mathbb T^4\), giving the minus
statement.
\end{proof}

\]
The combined record ledger has SHA--256

\label{ass:newv26}
The three centres have different centre spectra, rational metrics,

For each transverse anchor, retain the active Laurent variable

The uniform active-circle step must be a whole-matrix certificate.

\section{V27: a full-active-circle transverse tube}
\label{sec:newv27-circle-tube}

V26 enlarged a local four-dimensional atlas but left long active
arcs uncovered.  We now reverse the order of elimination.  The V10
noncommutative splitting removes the entire active circle first, and
the V24 matrix-convex hull is applied only to the three transverse
displacements.

Let
\[
 b_\circ=(\pi,\pi,0)\in\mathbb T^3.                      \tag{27.1}
\]
For the four-thirds Wilson pencil, write at transverse displacement
\(u=(u_2,u_3,u_4)\)
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(z;u)
 =A(u)+zB(u)+\bar zB(u)^*,\qquad |z|=1.                 \tag{27.2}
\]
Fix the exact V10 Hermitian splitting \(X_\circ^\#\) at \(u=0\)
and define
\[
 {\cal Q}(u):=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 A(u)-X_\circ^\#&B(u)\\
 B(u)^*&X_\circ^\#
 \end{pmatrix}.                                         \tag{27.3}
\]
After expanding the three transverse phases about (27.1),
\({\cal Q}\) is affine separately in
\[
 (c_2,s_2,c_3,s_3,c_4,s_4)
 =(\cos u_2,\sin u_2,\ldots,\cos u_4,\sin u_4).          \tag{27.4}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact radius-\(1/16\) tube over the active circle]
\label{thm:newv27-circle-tube}
For either \(\sigma\in\{-1,1\}\), every \(k_1\in\mathbb R\), and
every transverse momentum satisfying
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^3}
 \bigl((k_2,k_3,k_4),b_\circ\bigr)\le\frac1{16},         \tag{27.5}
\]
one has
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq
 \frac{50157588039687396532062769}
 {1500000007765500000000000000000}\,I_{45}.             \tag{27.6}
\]
Consequently the original constant-two pencil obeys
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}(k)\succeq
 \frac{39250157790978087396532062769}
 {1500000007765500000000000000000}\,I_{45}.             \tag{27.7}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At radius \(r=1/16\), place each pair \((c_j,s_j)\) in the rational
outer rectangle
\[
 [1-r^2/2,1]\times[-r,r].                               \tag{27.8}
\]
The product has \(2^6=64\) vertices.  Evaluate the complete
\(90\times90\) block (27.3) at each vertex, retaining the fixed
rational splitting \(X_\circ^\#\).  Every vertex passes an exact
rational congruence/Gershgorin certificate with the common block
floor
\[
 \beta_{27}=
 \frac{50157588039687396532062769}
 {3000000015531000000000000000000}.                     \tag{27.9}
\]
The weakest vertex has
\[
 (c_2,s_2,c_3,s_3,c_4,s_4)
 =(1,-1/16,1,-1/16,1,-1/16).                           \tag{27.10}
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv24-hull} applies to the six real variables and
proves \({\cal Q}(u)\succeq\beta_{27}I_{90}\) throughout the outer
rectangle, hence throughout the trigonometric box.

For \(w_z=(v,zv)\), the identity (2.9) with \(\delta=0\) gives
\[
 v^*P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(z;u)v
 =w_z^*{\cal Q}(u)w_z
 \ge2\beta_{27}\|v\|^2.                                 \tag{27.11}
\]
This proves (27.6) for the plus sign and every active phase at once.
The transverse tube is invariant under momentum inversion, so (4.4)
gives the minus sign.  Finally use (10.18) and the global Wilson
Hessian floor \(H_{\mathrm W}\succeq(49/1250)I\):
\[
 2\beta_{27}+\frac23\frac{49}{1250}
 =
 \frac{39250157790978087396532062769}
 {1500000007765500000000000000000},                     \tag{27.12}
\]
which is (27.7).
\end{proof}

\begin{countertheorem}[The radius-\(1/8\) fixed-splitting rectangle fails]
\label{ctr:newv27-eighth-relaxation}
At \(r=1/8\), the outer rectangle for (27.3) contains the vertex
\[
 (1,-1/8,1,1/8,1,1/8),                                 \tag{27.13}
\]
where a nonzero Gaussian-integer vector \(w\in\mathbb Z[i]^{90}\)
satisfies
\[
 w^*{\cal Q}w
 =-\frac{105006388004410181020151}{5625000000}<0.       \tag{27.14}
\]
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
Round an approximate negative eigenvector only to propose \(w\), then
evaluate (27.3) at (27.13) in exact rational arithmetic.  The
result is (27.14).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The obstruction is again an outer-corner artefact]
\label{rem:newv27-physical-arc}
Every pair \((1,\pm1/8)\) in (27.13) lies strictly outside the unit
circle.  Thus Countertheorem~\ref{ctr:newv27-eighth-relaxation}
rejects the rectangular outer hull with the fixed V10 splitting; it
does not prove that the splitting fails on the physical radius-
\(1/8\) trigonometric tube.
\end{remark}

\begin{assessment}[One torus coordinate is now eliminated on an open tube]
\label{ass:newv27}
Unlike V25--V26, Theorem~\ref{thm:newv27-circle-tube} imposes no
restriction on the active momentum.  It is a continuous
four-dimensional theorem on
\(\mathbb T\times[-1/16,1/16]^3\) about the transverse anchor.
The proof keeps the whole \(90\times90\) block, so no active-phase or
mixed-transverse cancellation is lost.

The transverse width remains far below the quarter-grid covering
radius: the centre of a quarter-grid cell has distance
\(\pi/4>3/4>1/16\) from every grid anchor.  Merely repeating the
same radius at all \(64\) anchors cannot produce a full-torus proof.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V28 replaces rectangles by rational arc hulls]
\label{dec:newv27-next}
Do not spend the next iteration certifying \(64\) tubes whose union
is already known not to cover.  The exact failure (27.13) occurs
off the trigonometric locus.  Replace each rectangle (27.8) by a
rational polygon containing the circular arc
\[
 \{(\cos u,\sin u):|u|\le r\}.
\]
Because (27.3) is affine in each coordinate pair, positivity on the
vertices of the product of three such polygons proves positivity on
the physical tube by iterated convexity.

First test a rational outer polygon at \(r=1/8\), where the
rectangle is known to fail.  If successful, refine the polygon and
increase toward the quarter-cell radius.  Any rejection must include
an exact negative polygon-vertex witness and must remain explicitly
separate from physical trigonometric negativity.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V27]
\label{fire:newv27-final}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv27-circle-tube} eliminates the active coordinate
only within one transverse tube.  The union of analogous
radius-\(1/16\) quarter-grid tubes would not cover the transverse
torus.  The radius-\(1/8\) negative vector belongs to an outer
relaxation.  No full-torus covariant inequality, cofinal
regulator-uniform response sign, compact graph-screen, interacting
continuum measure, reflection-positive limit, exponential
clustering, or Hamiltonian mass gap has been proved.  The full
Yang--Mills mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V28: a rational arc polygon doubles the circle-tube radius}
\label{sec:newv28-arc-polygon}

The radius-\(1/8\) failure in V27 occurs at impossible pairs
\((1,\pm1/8)\).  A rational outer polygon can respect the curvature
of the unit circle while preserving a finite matrix-convex
certificate.

\begin{lemma}[Exact two-tangent arc hull]
\label{lem:newv28-arc-hull}
Let
\[
 {\cal A}_{1/8}:=
 \{(\cos u,\sin u):|u|\le1/8\}.                          \tag{28.1}
\]
Then \({\cal A}_{1/8}\) is contained in the rational polygon
\({\cal G}_{1/8}\) defined by
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \frac{127}{128}\le x\le1,\qquad |y|\le\frac18,\\
 1023x+64y\le1025,\quad 1023x-64y\le1025,\\
 255x+32y\le257,\quad 255x-32y\le257.                   \tag{28.2}
\end{gathered}
\]
The distinct vertices of \({\cal G}_{1/8}\) are
\[
\begin{split}
 &(127/128,\ \pm511/4096),\\
 &(511/513,\ \pm16/171),\\
 &(1,\ \pm1/32).
\end{split}                                             \tag{28.3}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The first line of (28.2) follows from
\(\cos u\ge1-u^2/2\), \(\cos u\le1\), and
\(|\sin u|\le|u|\).  For a rational half-angle parameter \(q\), put
\[
 C_q=\frac{1-q^2}{1+q^2},\qquad
 S_q=\frac{2q}{1+q^2}.                                  \tag{28.4}
\]
Then \(C_q^2+S_q^2=1\), and for either sign
\[
 C_q\cos u\pm S_q\sin u\le1                             \tag{28.5}
\]
by Cauchy--Schwarz, equivalently by a cosine difference identity.
Taking \(q=1/32\) and \(q=1/16\) gives respectively the last four
inequalities of (28.2).  Hence the physical arc is contained in the
polygon.  Intersect every pair of boundary lines in (28.2), discard
points violating any half-plane, and reduce the remaining rational
pairs.  This exact enumeration gives precisely (28.3).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Product-polygon matrix hull]
\label{lem:newv28-product-hull}
Suppose a Hermitian matrix function \(F(p_2,p_3,p_4)\) is affine in
each vector argument \(p_j\in\mathbb R^2\) separately.  If
\[
 F(v_2,v_3,v_4)\succeq\beta I
 \quad\text{for all }v_j\in\operatorname{Vert}({\cal G}),\tag{28.6}
\]
then the same inequality holds for all
\((p_2,p_3,p_4)\in{\cal G}^3\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write each \(p_j\) as a convex combination of vertices of
\({\cal G}\).  Substitute the first combination into the affine
first argument, then the second and third.  The result is a convex
combination of the matrices in (28.6).  Convexity of the Loewner
cone proves the assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact radius-\(1/8\) tube over the full active circle]
\label{thm:newv28-arc-tube}
For either \(\sigma\in\{-1,1\}\), every \(k_1\in\mathbb R\), and
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^3}
 \bigl((k_2,k_3,k_4),(\pi,\pi,0)\bigr)\le\frac18,        \tag{28.7}
\]
the four-thirds and original Wilson pencils satisfy
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq
 \frac{18854964476581952344378912051}
 {170666667552256000000000000000000}\,I_{45},           \tag{28.8}
\]
and
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}(k)\succeq
 \frac{13436831629526616257033136736153}
 {512000002656768000000000000000000}\,I_{45}.           \tag{28.9}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the same fixed splitting \(X_\circ^\#\) and block
\({\cal Q}\) as in (27.3).  This block is affine separately in the
three pairs \(p_j=(c_j,s_j)\).  The polygon in (28.3) has six
vertices, so its triple product has \(6^3=216\) product vertices.
At every one of them, exact rational congruence, rowwise
square-root ceilings, and the Gram-residual debit give a positive
block.  Their common block floor is
\[
 \beta_{28}=
 \frac{18854964476581952344378912051}
 {341333335104512000000000000000000}.                   \tag{28.10}
\]
The weakest product vertex is
\[
 (127/128,511/4096,\,
   127/128,-511/4096,\,
   127/128,-511/4096).                                  \tag{28.11}
\]
Lemmas~\ref{lem:newv28-arc-hull} and
\ref{lem:newv28-product-hull} prove
\({\cal Q}(u)\succeq\beta_{28}I_{90}\) on the complete physical
transverse box (28.7).  Applying \(w_z=(v,zv)\) as in (27.11)
doubles the block floor and proves (28.8) for every active phase.
Momentum inversion gives the other sign.  Adding the exact global
Hessian reserve \((2/3)(49/1250)\) through (10.18) and reducing the
fraction gives (28.9).
\end{proof}

\begin{record}[V28 exact provenance]

The six polygon vertices (28.3) have ledger SHA--256
\[
\texttt{A2B5E6D80BEA213B33A1254A6357CD818FD68B8AAE86CEEF1F395ABF83E8CE61}.
                                                               \tag{28.12}
\]
The \(216\)-product-vertex block certificate has ledger SHA--256
\[
\texttt{59AFC05BE4ED91938D10779659BD0BFE73EE7E6F4EADB4334883D3A8008D0F6C}.
                                                               \tag{28.13}
\]
The fixed splitting and grouped active-coefficient bank retain the
V27 hashes (27.15)--(27.16).  The executable verifier is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v28_rational_arc_polygon_hull.py}.
\end{center}
Its source and canonical-payload SHA--256 values are
\[
\begin{gathered}
\texttt{1FD40257A76E43D7FF0A3FBD8E500CE0944966B23E3F6E23329BD6795BB3551E},\\
\texttt{071219E2E8D2F0129B30214AB9C239795127EEF3653E961C497F661CF71C3D80}.
\end{gathered}                                          \tag{28.14}
\]

\begin{assessment}[Curvature, not a smaller radius, was the missing datum]
\label{ass:newv28}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv28-arc-tube} doubles V27's physical transverse
radius while using the same noncommutative splitting.  The gain
comes entirely from imposing rational tangent inequalities valid on
the circle.  Thus the radius-\(1/8\) rectangular failure was not a
failure of the active-circle splitting on physical phases.

The new radius is still less than the quarter-grid covering radius
\(\pi/4\).  It therefore advances the local theorem but does not
yet justify running the construction over all \(64\) anchors.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V29 scales the rational polygon to radius \(1/4\)]
\label{dec:newv28-next}
At physical angular radius \(r=1/4\), use the rational base bounds
\[
 31/32\le\cos u\le1,\qquad |\sin u|\le1/4,
\]
and tangent pairs with half-angle parameters \(1/16\) and \(1/8\).
Enumerate the exact polygon vertices and test their triple product
with the fixed V10 splitting.  If it fails only at outer vertices,
insert intermediate rational tangents before changing the splitting.
If a certified physical phase fails, then and only then construct a
shape-preserving moving splitting upstream.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V28]
\label{fire:newv28-final}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv28-arc-tube} covers the complete active circle
over one transverse radius-\(1/8\) box.  It does not cover the
transverse torus or prove a global covariant Loewner inequality.
No cofinal response sign, compact graph-screen, interacting
four-dimensional measure, reflection-positive continuum limit,
exponential clustering, or Hamiltonian Yang--Mills mass gap has
been proved.  The full mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V29: a physical obstruction to the fixed active-circle splitting}
\label{sec:newv29-fixed-splitting-obstruction}

V28 showed that rational tangent polygons can distinguish curvature
loss from genuine failure.  Scaling the same construction to radius
\(1/4\) first produces a very close outer-polygon obstruction and
then, more decisively, an exact obstruction on the physical
trigonometric locus.

\subsection{The rational polygon at radius one-quarter}

Using half-angle parameters \(1/16\) and \(1/8\), define
\({\cal G}_{1/4}\) by
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \frac{31}{32}\le x\le1,\qquad |y|\le\frac14,\\
 255x\pm32y\le257,\qquad 63x\pm16y\le65.                \tag{29.1}
\end{gathered}
\]
The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:newv28-arc-hull} applies verbatim and
shows
\[
 \{(\cos u,\sin u):|u|\le1/4\}\subset{\cal G}_{1/4}.     \tag{29.2}
\]
Exact half-plane intersection gives the six vertices
\[
\begin{split}
 &(31/32,\ \pm127/512),\\
 &(127/129,\ \pm8/43),\\
 &(1,\ \pm1/16).
\end{split}                                             \tag{29.3}
\]

\begin{countertheorem}[The radius-\(1/4\) two-tangent hull is indefinite]
\label{ctr:newv29-polygon}
The product polygon \({\cal G}_{1/4}^3\) contains the vertex
\[
 (31/32,-127/512,\,
   31/32,127/512,\,
   31/32,127/512),                                      \tag{29.4}
\]
where a nonzero \(w_{\rm out}\in\mathbb Z[i]^{90}\) satisfies
\[
 w_{\rm out}^*{\cal Q}w_{\rm out}
 =-\frac{37992117028310188030970739}{320000000000}<0.   \tag{29.5}
\]
Each of the three coordinate pairs in (29.4) has unit-circle
residual
\[
 (31/32)^2+(127/512)^2-1=\frac1{262144}>0.              \tag{29.6}
\]
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
Substitute the rational vertex (29.4) into the multi-affine block
(27.3).  Rounding a numerical negative eigenvector proposes
\(w_{\rm out}\); exact Gaussian-rational evaluation gives (29.5).
Equation (29.6) is direct integer arithmetic and proves that this
first witness is not physical.
\end{proof}

\subsection{An exact point on all three physical arcs}

Let
\[
 C=\frac{63}{65},\qquad S=\frac{16}{65},\qquad
 t=2\arctan\frac18.                                     \tag{29.7}
\]
Then
\[
 C^2+S^2=1,\qquad (C,S)=(\cos t,\sin t),\qquad
 0<t<\frac14,                                           \tag{29.8}
\]
because \(\arctan x<x\) for \(x>0\).

\begin{countertheorem}[The fixed V10 splitting fails at a physical phase]
\label{ctr:newv29-physical}
At the physical transverse displacement
\[
 (u_2,u_3,u_4)=(-t,t,t),                                \tag{29.9}
\]
equivalently at the six exact variables
\[
 (C,-S,C,S,C,S)
 =(63/65,-16/65,63/65,16/65,63/65,16/65),              \tag{29.10}
\]
there is a nonzero \(w_{\rm phys}\in\mathbb Z[i]^{90}\)
for which the fixed-splitting block obeys
\[
 w_{\rm phys}^*{\cal Q}w_{\rm phys}
 =-\frac{879493546613436239273167271}
 {7605000000000}<0.                                     \tag{29.11}
\]
Consequently no outer polygon, however finely tangent-refined, can
prove a full physical radius-\(1/4\) tube using the single fixed
splitting \(X_\circ^\#\).
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
The identities (29.7)--(29.10) put the point strictly inside all
three angular radius-\(1/4\) arcs.  Evaluate the exact active Laurent
coefficients \(A(u),B(u)\) there and insert the rational
\(X_\circ^\#\) into (27.3).  A floating eigenvector is used only to
choose an integer vector.  Direct rational evaluation then gives
(29.11).  Since every valid outer set for the physical tube must
contain (29.10), positivity of the fixed block on such an outer set
is impossible.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[The direct pencil has not been refuted]
\label{fire:newv29-block-only}
Negative definiteness of one certificate block
\({\cal Q}_{0,X_\circ^\#}\) does not imply negativity of
\(P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(z;u)\).  Theorem
\ref{thm:newv2-block} is a one-way implication: a positive block is
sufficient for the active-circle pencil, but a chosen block may fail
even when another splitting or a spectral factor exists.  Thus
(29.11) closes only the constant-\(X_\circ^\#\) extension route.
\end{firewall}

The polygon vertex list (29.3) has SHA--256
\[
\texttt{86346B57D350B7C676E1CA2020518B94638C05F8AC43616865C7BD509146CD03}.
                                                               \tag{29.12}
\]
The witnesses in (29.5) and (29.11) have respective SHA--256 values

\texttt{7CB8B4B23BC8CA70BB2DE0200D401C9135EF508645790C24BF2AC749D18F037F},\\
\texttt{5C46F7899CEFB998079F1EE17847729F9E1FC2E6D4A6CD91B056B722B066D48C}.
\end{gathered}                                          \tag{29.13}
\]
The executable verifier is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v29_quarter_radius_arc_polygon.py}.
\end{center}

\texttt{88C48B92EB924344586F8D2F1AC3166AC7AC7FA1FD2DB071F55370BC7D2CDB1E}.

\begin{assessment}[The bottleneck has moved from geometry to splitting transport]
\label{ass:newv29}
V28 removed a false rectangular obstruction.  V29 now proves that
the same splitting really does cease to be feasible at a physical
rational phase before radius \(1/4\).  Further tangent refinement is
therefore circular.  The surviving question is whether the direct
active-circle pencil at (29.10) admits a different exact
noncommutative splitting and whether neighbouring feasible
splittings can be joined without reproducing the failed V11--V12
global interpolation.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V30 audits companions and constructs the next splitting]
\label{dec:newv29-next}
Perform the compulsory SM/NS audit.  Then solve the two active
Riccati endpoint equations at the physical rational point (29.10),
round an interior Hermitian splitting independently, and certify its
\(90\times90\) block exactly.  If successful, compute a rational
arc-polygon neighbourhood for that new splitting and an exact
overlap with the V28 tube.

Do not reuse the V11 degree-one or V12 cardinal global splitting:
their failures are already proved.  The new construction is a local
finite atlas of constant feasible splittings, with overlap checked
at the pencil level.  If the block at (29.10) itself has no feasible
splitting, return upstream to a direct active-circle spectral
factorization.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V29]
\label{fire:newv29-final}
The exact physical witness (29.11) concerns only the fixed V10
certificate block.  It is not a negative Yang--Mills pencil
direction.  The V28 radius-\(1/8\) full-active-circle tube remains
proved, but no radius-\(1/4\) tube, transverse-torus cover, global
covariant inequality, cofinal continuum response, interacting
measure, reflection-positive limit, exponential clustering, or
Hamiltonian mass gap has been proved.  The full Yang--Mills
mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V30: a second splitting and an overlapping active-circle tube}
\label{sec:newv30-second-tube}

The physical point which defeated the fixed V10 splitting in V29
need not defeat the Wilson pencil.  This iteration first performs
the compulsory cross-program audit, then constructs a new rational
splitting at that point and certifies a full-active-circle
neighbourhood which overlaps the V28 tube.

\subsection{Compulsory companion audit}

\begin{record}[Frozen V30 companion checkpoint]
\label{rec:newv30-companions}
The co-current Standard Model--Einstein checkpoint is
\begin{center}
\path{Renewal_SM_Einstein_Continuum_Research_Notes_V94.tex},
\end{center}
with \(1114934\) bytes, \(33394\) lines, and SHA--256
\[
\texttt{F8350B77D7F4A4981FD0FB4A5D20791D0736326DE3D37CEA67067DDDE02D6E0D}.
                                                               \tag{30.1}
\]
The Navier--Stokes checkpoint is
\begin{center}
\path{Renewal_NS_Stability_Research_Notes_V26.tex},
\end{center}
with \(278283\) bytes, \(5319\) lines, and SHA--256
\[
\texttt{82C887F8C2C69E4F28FAD7C3DC8DE5B9099CB5A4BF431EBA1FFF3E91935978AD}.
                                                               \tag{30.2}
\]
\end{record}

SM V90 read YM V28 and independently extracted exactly the
constraint-locus lesson now used here: prove that the physical
curved locus lies in a rational outer polytope before invoking
whole-matrix vertex convexity.  SM V91 applies the same rule to
admissible metric loci.  These are method checks, not imports of an
SM coefficient or physical theorem.

SM V93--V94 also gives a downstream warning which is relevant to the
mass-gap endpoint.  A global stationary refresh adds an exact scalar
shift only on the centred space, can lift disconnected-sector zero
modes, and cancels from an honest comparison with a refresh-free
local Hamiltonian.  The present Wilson-symbol certificate contains
no such refresh.  Later measure and OS steps must likewise derive
their gap from local dynamics rather than from a nonlocal reset.

NS V26 concerns rank-one contractions of first and second
derivatives of the Oseen kernel.  It has no common matrix,
configuration space, or implication with the present
noncommutative trigonometric splitting.  No NS constant or theorem
is imported.

\begin{audit}[V30 audit verdict]
\label{aud:newv30-verdict}
The companion audit changes no coefficient in the Wilson
calculation and closes no Yang--Mills dependency.  It confirms two
proof disciplines:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item use only an outer hull whose containment of the physical
      trigonometric locus is proved exactly; and
\item do not convert an auxiliary nonlocal mixing floor into a
      physical Hamiltonian mass gap.
\end{enumerate}
The first discipline is implemented below; the second is retained
for the later measure, reconstruction, and spectral rows.
\end{audit}

\subsection{The new centre and its rational block}

Retain
\[
 C=\frac{63}{65},\qquad S=\frac{16}{65},\qquad
 t=2\arctan\frac18
\]
from (29.7), and put, modulo \(2\pi\),
\[
 b_+=(\pi-t,\pi+t,t),\qquad b_-=-b_+.                  \tag{30.3}
\]
The three absolute phase pairs at \(b_+\) are
\[
 (-C,S),\qquad(-C,-S),\qquad(C,S).                     \tag{30.4}
\]
They are rational points of the unit circle.

For a local displacement \(v=(v_2,v_3,v_4)\) from \(b_+\), compose
the rational base phases (30.4) with
\[
 p_j=(x_j,y_j)=(\cos v_j,\sin v_j).                    \tag{30.5}
\]
Regrouping the complete direct Wilson coefficient bank gives
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(z;b_++v)
 =A_1(v)+zB_1(v)+\bar zB_1(v)^*,\qquad |z|=1.          \tag{30.6}
\]
Both \(A_1\) and \(B_1\) are affine separately in the three pairs
\(p_j\).

At \(v=0\), a floating Riccati computation is used only to propose a
matrix.  Entrywise rationalization and Hermitian symmetrization
produce \(X_1^\#=(X_1^\#)^*\).  All subsequent assertions use exact
rational arithmetic on
\[
 {\cal Q}_1(v):=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 A_1(v)-X_1^\#&B_1(v)\\
 B_1(v)^*&X_1^\#
 \end{pmatrix}.                                        \tag{30.7}
\]
In particular, at the centre the exact rounded-basis certificate is
\[
 {\cal Q}_1(0)\succeq
 \frac{32778352053372488774759108371}
 {380250001950682500000000000000000}\,I_{90}.          \tag{30.8}
\]
Thus V29's physical witness disproved only \(X_\circ^\#\), exactly as
the claim firewall there required.

\subsection{A rational hull at the new centre}

Set
\[
 r_1=\frac{99}{800},\qquad
 {\cal R}=\left\{\frac{99}{3200},\frac{99}{1600},
                         \frac{31}{500}\right\}.        \tag{30.9}
\]
For \(q\in{\cal R}\), let \(C_q,S_q\) be as in (28.4), and define
the rational polygon
\[
\begin{split}
 {\cal G}_{30}:=\{(x,y):\;&1-r_1^2/2\le x\le1,\quad
                  |y|\le r_1,\\
                &C_qx+S_qy\le1,\quad
                  C_qx-S_qy\le1\quad(q\in{\cal R})\}.
\end{split}                                                    \tag{30.10}
\]

\begin{lemma}[Exact eight-vertex local arc hull]
\label{lem:newv30-arc-hull}
The physical arc
\[
 {\cal A}_{r_1}=\{(\cos u,\sin u):|u|\le r_1\}
\]
is contained in \({\cal G}_{30}\).  The distinct vertices of
\({\cal G}_{30}\) are
\[
\begin{split}
 &\left(\frac{1270199}{1280000},
          \ \pm\frac{4900991239}{39680000000}\right),\\
 &\left(\frac{796931}{803069},
          \ \pm\frac{99100}{803069}\right),\\
 &\left(\frac{5110199}{5129801},
          \ \pm\frac{475200}{5129801}\right),\\
 &\left(1,\ \pm\frac{99}{3200}\right).
\end{split}                                                    \tag{30.11}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For \(|u|\le r_1\),
\[
 1-r_1^2/2\le\cos u\le1,\qquad |\sin u|\le r_1.
\]
Moreover \(C_q^2+S_q^2=1\), so Cauchy--Schwarz gives
\[
 C_q\cos u\pm S_q\sin u\le1
\]
for every \(q\in{\cal R}\).  This proves physical-locus
containment, independently of any matrix test.  Intersecting every
pair among the ten rational boundary lines in (30.10), retaining
precisely those intersections satisfying all ten weak
inequalities, and reducing the coordinates gives the eight points
in (30.11).  Hence those points are exactly the vertex set.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Second exact tube over the full active circle]
\label{thm:newv30-second-tube}
Let \(\sigma\in\{-1,1\}\), and use \(b_\sigma=b_+\) for
\(\sigma=1\) and \(b_\sigma=b_-\) for \(\sigma=-1\).
For every \(k_1\in\mathbb R\) and every transverse momentum with
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^3}
 \bigl((k_2,k_3,k_4),b_\sigma\bigr)\le\frac{99}{800},   \tag{30.12}
\]
the four-thirds Wilson pencil satisfies
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq
 \frac{2340853314404247793837525020475729673713}
 {187095168973269063936000000000000000000000000}\,
 I_{45}.                                                \tag{30.13}
\]
The original constant-two pencil satisfies on the same tube
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}(k)\succeq
 \frac{4891761269149169118654637525020475729673713}
 {187095168973269063936000000000000000000000000}\,
 I_{45}.                                                \tag{30.14}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The block (30.7) is affine separately in \(p_2,p_3,p_4\).
The triple product of the eight vertices in (30.11) has
\(8^3=512\) elements.  At every product vertex, exact rational
congruence, rowwise square-root ceilings, and the Gram-residual
debit certify strict positivity.  Their common block floor is
\[
 \beta_{30}=
 \frac{2340853314404247793837525020475729673713}
 {374190337946538127872000000000000000000000000}.       \tag{30.15}
\]
The weakest certified vertex is
\[
\begin{split}
\biggl(&\frac{1270199}{1280000},
        \frac{4900991239}{39680000000},\
        \frac{1270199}{1280000},
       -\frac{4900991239}{39680000000},\\
      &\frac{1270199}{1280000},
       -\frac{4900991239}{39680000000}\biggr).
\end{split}                                             \tag{30.16}
\]
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv30-arc-hull} and the product-hull
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv28-product-hull} imply
\({\cal Q}_1(v)\succeq\beta_{30}I_{90}\) throughout the
physical box \(|v_j|\le r_1\).

For \(w_z=(v,zv)\), the exact block identity gives
\[
 v^*P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(z;b_++v)v
 =w_z^*{\cal Q}_1(v)w_z
 \ge2\beta_{30}\|v\|^2.
\]
This is (30.13) for the plus tube and every active phase.
Momentum inversion carries it to the minus tube about \(b_-\).
Finally, (10.18) and
\(H_{\mathrm W}\succeq(49/1250)I\) add
\((2/3)(49/1250)\) to (30.13).  Exact fraction reduction gives
(30.14).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Exact overlap with the first tube}

\begin{proposition}[The two certified charts overlap]
\label{prop:newv30-overlap}
For each sign, the V28 radius-\(1/8\) tube about
\(b_\circ=(\pi,\pi,0)\) has nonempty interior intersection with the
corresponding V30 radius-\(99/800\) tube about \(b_\sigma\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The transverse \(L^\infty\) distance from \(b_\circ\) to either
\(b_+\) or \(b_-\), modulo \(2\pi\), is \(t\).  The alternating
series for \(\arctan x\), truncated after its positive \(x^5/5\)
term, gives the strict rational upper bound
\[
\begin{split}
 t=2\arctan\frac18
 &<2\left(\frac18-\frac1{3\cdot8^3}
                    +\frac1{5\cdot8^5}\right)\\
 &=\frac{61123}{245760}
 <\frac{199}{800}
 =\frac18+\frac{99}{800}.
\end{split}                                             \tag{30.17}
\]
The gap between the sum of the radii and the displayed upper bound
is
\[
 \frac{199}{800}-\frac{61123}{245760}
 =\frac{49}{1228800}>0.                                \tag{30.18}
\]
Thus the corresponding coordinate intervals overlap with positive
length in each of the three transverse coordinates.  Their product,
and hence the two open transverse tubes, has nonempty intersection.
\end{proof}

\end{gathered}                                          \tag{30.19}
\]
The ordered polygon-vertex list and the \(512\)-product-vertex

\texttt{432B90013CA3694E1CB84607686B433D77DA67EBD8A7E25AB688DAC5E995E5A0},\\
\texttt{056489A9E4E4B745D9FFDF1293DDD8E3CD27743D60EED37FBAD241E13293395E}.
\end{gathered}                                          \tag{30.20}
\]
The executable verifier is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v30_second_active_circle_tube.py}.
\end{center}

\texttt{50964E6772BB2B0461820D27CBFCF61098DC2F5631D94ECD6E14B25208357702},\\
\texttt{35BCB81C42D4DB6781919AD1F7E686E432C0160FB9715E62F4D0E4266ADBB248}.

\begin{assessment}[What the second chart proves]
\label{ass:newv30}
The exact Wilson pencil remains feasible at the physical point where
the old fixed splitting fails.  The new splitting supplies a
positive neighbourhood, and Proposition~\ref{prop:newv30-overlap}
joins it to the V28 chart.  This is the first rigorously connected
two-chart atlas across a physical fixed-splitting obstruction in
the present active-circle line.

The conclusion is local in the three transverse directions.  Two
overlapping cubes along the diagonal displacement
\((-t,t,t)\) do not cover a quarter cell, much less
\(\mathbb T^3\).  Overlap of domains also does not assert equality or
continuous interpolation of the two splittings; none is needed,
because either positive block suffices on the overlap.  What is
proved is the union of two certified pencil domains.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V31 constructs a quantitative chart-transition rule]
\label{dec:newv30-next}
Do not repeat an unstructured point-by-point march.  Use the positive
block floor at a rational centre to bound the operator variation of
\({\cal Q}_{X^\#}\) under a transverse displacement.  This should
produce a computable certified radius from one exact splitting before
any vertex enumeration.  Then compare that radius with the distance
to a rational next centre and rebuild the splitting only when the
bound forces it.

The target is a finite adaptive cover of a quarter transverse cell,
with every new chart carrying: a physical rational centre, an exact
splitting, an exact local floor, a physical-locus polygon, and an
overlap edge.  If the variation bound degenerates faster than the
available centre floors, return upstream to a direct parameterized
active-circle factor rather than assuming a global interpolating
splitting.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V30]
\label{fire:newv30-final}
V30 proves a second full-active-circle Wilson tube and its exact
overlap with the V28 tube.  It does not prove a finite cover of the
transverse torus, a global covariant Loewner inequality, a
regulator-uniform response sign, a compact graph-screen, an
interacting continuum measure, reflection-positive reconstruction,
exponential clustering, or a Hamiltonian Yang--Mills mass gap.
The full Yang--Mills mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V31: the third chart reaches the twice-shifted phase}
\label{sec:newv31-third-tube}

V30 proposed a generic norm-variation transition estimate.  That
method is not repeated here: V5 already proved that the global
Frobenius derivative stock gives unusably small transport radii, and
V13 recorded the corresponding local Taylor-stock loss.  The exact
physical-locus polygon is materially sharper.  We therefore use it
at the next rational point of the same one-parameter subgroup.

\subsection{The squared rational phase}

Let \(t=2\arctan(1/8)\) as before.  Squaring the unit phase
\((63+16i)/65\) gives
\[
 D=\cos(2t)=\frac{3713}{4225},\qquad
 E=\sin(2t)=\frac{2016}{4225},\qquad D^2+E^2=1.         \tag{31.1}
\]
Set, modulo \(2\pi\),
\[
 c_+=(\pi-2t,\pi+2t,2t),\qquad c_-=-c_+.               \tag{31.2}
\]
The absolute transverse phases at \(c_+\) are
\[
 (-D,E),\qquad(-D,-E),\qquad(D,E).                     \tag{31.3}
\]
They are exact rational points of the physical three-torus.

Regroup the direct Wilson bank about (31.3), write local displacement
as \(v\), and denote the resulting active-circle coefficients by
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(z;c_++v)
 =A_2(v)+zB_2(v)+\bar zB_2(v)^*.                       \tag{31.4}
\]
An independently proposed and rationalized Hermitian splitting
\(X_2^\#\) defines
\[
 {\cal Q}_2(v)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 A_2(v)-X_2^\#&B_2(v)\\
 B_2(v)^*&X_2^\#
 \end{pmatrix}.                                        \tag{31.5}
\]
Its exact centre certificate is
\[
 {\cal Q}_2(0)\succeq
 \frac{9131629368726908758837174153789}
 {85683000440239254000000000000000000}\,I_{90}.        \tag{31.6}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Third exact tube over the full active circle]
\label{thm:newv31-third-tube}
For \(\sigma\in\{-1,1\}\), let \(c_\sigma=c_+\) when
\(\sigma=1\) and \(c_\sigma=c_-\) when \(\sigma=-1\).
For every active momentum \(k_1\) and
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^3}
 \bigl((k_2,k_3,k_4),c_\sigma\bigr)\le\frac18,          \tag{31.7}
\]
one has
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq
 \frac{1732625801408472987159905002742791}
 {263218177363470151680000000000000000000}\,I_{45},    \tag{31.8}
\]
and
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}(k)\succeq
 \frac{6880500994233428436891159905002742791}
 {263218177363470151680000000000000000000}\,I_{45}.    \tag{31.9}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The block (31.5) is affine separately in the three local pairs
\((\cos v_j,\sin v_j)\).  Use the already proved physical arc hull
\({\cal G}_{1/8}\) of Lemma~\ref{lem:newv28-arc-hull}.  Adding the
tangent with half-angle parameter \(63/1000\) changes none of its
six vertices, so the product polygon still has \(6^3=216\) product
vertices.

Every one of the \(216\) matrices is certified in exact rational
arithmetic.  The common block floor is
\[
 \beta_{31}=
 \frac{1732625801408472987159905002742791}
 {526436354726940303360000000000000000000}.             \tag{31.10}
\]
The weakest vertex is
\[
 (127/128,511/4096,\,
  127/128,-511/4096,\,
  127/128,-511/4096).                                  \tag{31.11}
\]
The product-hull lemma therefore yields
\({\cal Q}_2(v)\succeq\beta_{31}I_{90}\) on the complete
physical radius-\(1/8\) box.  Evaluation on
\((w,zw)\) doubles the block floor and proves (31.8) for the plus
sign.  Momentum inversion proves the statement about \(c_-\).
Adding the global Wilson Hessian reserve
\((2/3)(49/1250)\) and reducing exactly gives (31.9).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Second exact atlas edge]
\label{prop:newv31-overlap}
For each sign, the V31 tube about \(c_\sigma\) has nonempty open
intersection with the V30 tube about \(b_\sigma\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The two centres differ by \((-t,t,t)\), up to sign, and hence have
transverse \(L^\infty\) distance \(t\).  Equations (30.17)--(30.18)
give
\[
 t<\frac{61123}{245760}
 <\frac18+\frac{99}{800},
\]
with strict rational margin \(49/1228800\).  The three pairs of
coordinate intervals therefore overlap with positive length.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[A connected three-chart diagonal corridor]
\label{cor:newv31-corridor}
For the plus pencil, the three exact charts of V28, V30, and V31
form a connected open union containing the complete diagonal path
\[
 s\longmapsto(\pi-s,\pi+s,s),\qquad
 -\frac18\le s\le2t+\frac18.                            \tag{31.12}
\]
The four-thirds pencil has throughout this union the uniform lower
bound (31.8).  Momentum inversion gives the corresponding minus
corridor.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Along the path, the three chart conditions reduce to intervals of
radii \(1/8,99/800,1/8\) centred at \(0,t,2t\).
Propositions~\ref{prop:newv30-overlap} and
\ref{prop:newv31-overlap} show consecutive intervals overlap.
Their union is therefore the interval in (31.12).  The least of the
three chart floors is (31.8), by exact fraction comparison, so it is
a common lower bound on the union.
\end{proof}

The splitting and grouped active-coefficient bank have SHA--256

\end{gathered}                                          \tag{31.13}
\]
The polygon retains the V28 vertex hash
\[
\texttt{A2B5E6D80BEA213B33A1254A6357CD818FD68B8AAE86CEEF1F395ABF83E8CE61},
                                                               \tag{31.14}
\]
and the \(216\)-vertex certificate ledger has SHA--256
\[
\texttt{F13FEEE061EC5E775FFB5DBFA5C69E9749B76DE8A8CAFF8A6BD342F89F7020E6}.
                                                               \tag{31.15}
\]
The verifier is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v31_third_active_circle_tube.py}.
\end{center}
Its source and canonical-payload SHA--256 values are
\[
\begin{gathered}
\texttt{B9D65A063E09159C00F9DB278C133B74B982E1002255FC48D5EDDDD17CA1E459},\\
\texttt{1300FB5C88FBC2091FB6B236611E6CBA27E75A4038B881EACDFADD4AAA2E1547}.
\end{gathered}                                          \tag{31.16}
\]

\begin{assessment}[The diagonal is no longer the immediate obstruction]
\label{ass:newv31}
The new splitting remains feasible at displacement \(2t\), and the
three domains form an exact connected corridor.  This demonstrates
that the V29 fixed-splitting failure was a chart failure, not a
pencil failure, at two successive rational centres.

The corridor is only one-dimensional evidence inside a
three-dimensional transverse cell.  A positive tube around a
diagonal does not cover off-diagonal faces or mixed-sign corners.
No conclusion about those regions follows from symmetry presently
proved in these notes.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V32 reaches the quarter-cell diagonal and then turns off it]
\label{dec:newv31-next}
Test the third phase power, centred at
\((\pi-3t,\pi+3t,3t)\).  Since \(t<1/4\),
\[
 3t<\frac34<\frac{\pi}{4},
\]
so this is still inside the diagonal of the quarter cell.  If a
radius-\(1/8\) chart succeeds, it overlaps V31 and crosses the
diagonal face; the diagonal continuation should then stop.

The next acquisition must vary at least one transverse coordinate
independently.  Use the established chart centres as seeds for a
two-dimensional rational phase grid and require exact overlap edges.
If the centre block or physical arc fails in an off-diagonal
direction, isolate a direct-pencil witness before modifying the
splitting.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V31]
\label{fire:newv31-final}
V31 proves a third full-active-circle tube and a connected diagonal
corridor through three local splittings.  It does not cover a
transverse quarter cell or the transverse torus.  No global
covariant inequality, cofinal response sign, compact graph-screen,
interacting continuum measure, reflection-positive limit,
exponential clustering, or Hamiltonian Yang--Mills mass gap has
been proved.  The full Yang--Mills mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V32: the certified corridor crosses the quarter-cell diagonal}
\label{sec:newv32-diagonal-crossing}

The final diagonal test uses the third power of the same rational
unit phase.  Its success distinguishes the diagonal direction from
the remaining off-diagonal covering problem.

\subsection{The cubed rational phase}

Direct exact multiplication gives
\[
 \cos(3t)=\frac{201663}{274625},\qquad
 \sin(3t)=\frac{186416}{274625},                        \tag{32.1}
\]
and the sum of the squares of the two numerators is
\(274625^2\).  Define
\[
 d_+=(\pi-3t,\pi+3t,3t),\qquad d_-=-d_+                \tag{32.2}
\]
on the transverse torus.  The absolute phases at \(d_+\) are
\[
 \left(-\frac{201663}{274625},\frac{186416}{274625}\right),
 \quad
 \left(-\frac{201663}{274625},-\frac{186416}{274625}\right),
 \quad
 \left(\frac{201663}{274625},\frac{186416}{274625}\right).
                                                               \tag{32.3}
\]

Regrouping the direct Wilson bank about (32.3) gives coefficients
\(A_3(v),B_3(v)\).  An independently rationalized Hermitian
splitting \(X_3^\#\) defines
\[
 {\cal Q}_3(v)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 A_3(v)-X_3^\#&B_3(v)\\
 B_3(v)^*&X_3^\#
 \end{pmatrix}.                                        \tag{32.4}
\]
At the centre,
\[
 {\cal Q}_3(0)\succeq
 \frac{96202470170796751572080170772641}
 {658201230653248776000000000000000000}\,I_{90}.       \tag{32.5}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Fourth exact tube over the full active circle]
\label{thm:newv32-fourth-tube}
For \(\sigma\in\{-1,1\}\), use \(d_\sigma=d_+\) for the plus
sign and \(d_\sigma=d_-\) for the minus sign.  If
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^3}
 \bigl((k_2,k_3,k_4),d_\sigma\bigr)\le\frac18,          \tag{32.6}
\]
then, for every active momentum \(k_1\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq
 \frac{2072154946123058680064300246889869183}
 {44483871975760971786240000000000000000000}\,I_{45},  \tag{32.7}
\]
and
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}(k)\succeq
 \frac{1164584009246009788027136300246889869183}
 {44483871975760971786240000000000000000000}\,I_{45}.  \tag{32.8}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the six-vertex rational polygon
\({\cal G}_{1/8}\) from (28.2)--(28.3).  The block (32.4) is affine
separately in its three local phase pairs.  Exact rational
certification of all \(6^3=216\) product vertices gives the common
block floor
\[
 \beta_{32}=
 \frac{2072154946123058680064300246889869183}
 {88967743951521943572480000000000000000000}.           \tag{32.9}
\]
The weakest vertex is again
\[
 (127/128,511/4096,\,
  127/128,-511/4096,\,
  127/128,-511/4096).                                  \tag{32.10}
\]
Physical-arc containment and multi-affine convexity yield
\({\cal Q}_3(v)\succeq\beta_{32}I_{90}\) for
\(|v_j|\le1/8\).  Evaluation on \((w,zw)\), momentum inversion, and
the exact Hessian reserve \((2/3)(49/1250)\) prove
(32.7)--(32.8).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Third overlap edge and diagonal-face crossing]
\label{prop:newv32-crossing}
For each sign, the V32 tube overlaps the V31 tube with nonempty
interior.  For the plus sign, the resulting four-chart corridor
contains
\[
 s\longmapsto(\pi-s,\pi+s,s)
 \quad\text{for}\quad
 -\frac18\le s\le3t+\frac18,                            \tag{32.11}
\]
and this interval crosses the quarter-cell face \(s=\pi/4\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The V31 and V32 centres are at distance \(t\).  The rational upper
bound (30.17) gives
\[
 t<\frac{61123}{245760}<\frac14=\frac18+\frac18,
\]
and the displayed upper bound lies below the radius sum by
\[
 \frac14-\frac{61123}{245760}
 =\frac{317}{245760}>0.                                \tag{32.12}
\]
Thus the consecutive tubes overlap.  Together with the two earlier
overlap edges, their diagonal intervals form (32.11).

The alternating series truncated after its negative cubic term
gives
\[
 t=2\arctan\frac18
 >2\left(\frac18-\frac1{3\cdot8^3}\right)
 =\frac{191}{768}.                                     \tag{32.13}
\]
Hence
\[
 3t+\frac18>\frac{223}{256}>
 \frac{11}{14}>\frac{\pi}{4},                          \tag{32.14}
\]
where the middle comparison is integer arithmetic and the last is
the classical bound \(\pi<22/7\).  Conversely
\(3t<3/4<\pi/4\), using \(t<1/4\) and \(\pi>3\).
Therefore the centre remains inside the quarter cell while its
certified tube crosses the diagonal face.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Uniform floor on the four-chart corridor]
\label{cor:newv32-corridor-floor}
The plus four-thirds pencil has the uniform lower bound (31.8) on
the complete corridor (32.11); the minus pencil has the same bound
on its inverted corridor.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The V28, V30, V31, and V32 floors are positive.  Exact fraction
comparison shows that (31.8) is their minimum.  Apply the appropriate
chart theorem at every point of the connected union.
\end{proof}

\begin{record}[V32 exact provenance]
\label{rec:newv32-provenance}
The new rational splitting and grouped active bank have SHA--256
values
\[
\begin{gathered}
\texttt{1C2F0F24F04AF461C82C09FA6D86CF5490E7D7B0D80412427B2F43B36E441508},\\
\texttt{BBE512C85027F5ACF96E58A62A5A8E3CBBB3102E1555B9EA15FC3105C633FC16}.
\end{gathered}                                          \tag{32.15}
\]
The polygon retains the V28 vertex hash, and the new
\(216\)-vertex certificate ledger has SHA--256
\[
\texttt{1E1C40CA95D742BA8895EB5B2FA3FB1FE2DB0C5F382F6AEDCF41571F6CAAD7BC}.
                                                               \tag{32.16}

The verifier is

\begin{assessment}[The first uncovered geometry is off-diagonal]
\label{ass:newv32}
The active-circle pencil is now controlled along a connected
diagonal corridor from the V28 anchor through the quarter-cell
face.  Continuing farther on the same ray would not address the
uncovered parts of the three-dimensional transverse cell.

This is not a cell cover: a tube around one diagonal misses points
with independently varying transverse coordinates.  The certified
face crossing therefore ends the diagonal march and identifies the
next honest geometry as a two- and then three-dimensional atlas.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V33 branches into independent transverse directions]
\label{dec:newv32-next}
From \(b_\circ=(\pi,\pi,0)\), construct fresh exact splittings at
the three one-axis rational centres obtained by displacing exactly
one of \(k_2,k_3,k_4\) by \(t\).  Test all three independently before
using any permutation symmetry.  A radius-\(1/8\) tube at each such
centre would overlap V28 because \(t<1/4\).

If all three pass, use their overlap graph to seed a two-dimensional
rational phase grid.  If one fails, distinguish failure of the
proposed splitting, failure of an outer polygon, and a physical
negative direct-pencil direction in that order.  Do not infer a
full cell from the diagonal corridor.
\end{decision}

\label{fire:newv32-final}
V32 proves a fourth full-active-circle tube and an exact corridor

\section{V33: three independently certified one-axis branches}
\label{sec:newv33-one-axis}

V32 ended the diagonal march.  We now return to the V28 anchor and
move in each transverse coordinate separately.  The three
calculations below share only their physical arc hull; their
splittings and exact matrix ledgers are constructed independently.

\subsection{The three physical branch centres}

Let \(b_\circ=(\pi,\pi,0)\), \(t=2\arctan(1/8)\), and define
\[
\begin{split}
 a_2&=(\pi-t,\pi,0),\\
 a_3&=(\pi,\pi+t,0),\\
 a_4&=(\pi,\pi,t).
\end{split}                                             \tag{33.1}
\]
Their displacement vectors from \(b_\circ\) are respectively
\[
 (-t,0,0),\qquad(0,t,0),\qquad(0,0,t).                 \tag{33.2}
\]
Writing \(C=63/65\) and \(S=16/65\), their exact absolute phase
triples are
\[
\begin{array}{c|ccc}
 &e^{ik_2}&e^{ik_3}&e^{ik_4}\\ \hline
 a_2&(-C,S)&(-1,0)&(1,0)\\
 a_3&(-1,0)&(-C,-S)&(1,0)\\
 a_4&(-1,0)&(-1,0)&(C,S).
\end{array}                                             \tag{33.3}
\]
Every entry in (33.3) lies exactly on the unit circle.

For \(j=2,3,4\), regroup the direct Wilson bank about \(a_j\), and
write its active-circle form as
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(z;a_j+v)
 =A_j(v)+zB_j(v)+\bar zB_j(v)^*.                       \tag{33.4}
\]
Independently rationalize a proposed Hermitian splitting \(X_j^\#\)
and form
\[
 {\cal Q}_j(v)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 A_j(v)-X_j^\#&B_j(v)\\
 B_j(v)^*&X_j^\#
 \end{pmatrix}.                                        \tag{33.5}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Three exact one-axis branch tubes]
\label{thm:newv33-branches}
For each \(j\in\{2,3,4\}\), every active momentum \(k_1\), and
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^3}
 \bigl((k_2,k_3,k_4),a_j\bigr)\le\frac18,               \tag{33.6}
\]
the plus four-thirds Wilson pencil obeys
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq\gamma_j I_{45},     \tag{33.7}
\]
where
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
j&\gamma_j\\ \hline
2&
\displaystyle
\frac{4694682483335493985116536816009}
{119808000614016000000000000000000000}\\[6pt]
3&
\displaystyle
\frac{1073134217485099151212444156991}
{14976000078114816000000000000000000}\\[6pt]
4&
\displaystyle
\frac{5213211766619225025243926895533}
{59904000307786752000000000000000000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{33.8}
\]
The minus pencil satisfies the same bound on the radius-\(1/8\)
tube about \(-a_j\).  On every one of these tubes the original
constant-two pencil obeys
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}(k)\succeq
 \left(\gamma_j+\frac{49}{1875}\right)I_{45}.           \tag{33.9}
\]
In particular, all six signed tubes share the four-thirds floor
\[
 \gamma_{\rm branch}:=\gamma_2
 =
\frac{4694682483335493985116536816009}
{119808000614016000000000000000000000}>0.              \tag{33.10}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At each centre, exact Laurent pairing is first checked and the
rational matrix \(X_j^\#=(X_j^\#)^*\) is certified by a positive
centre block.  The respective centre block floors are
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
2&\displaystyle
\frac{1086339557857074169286589331}
{13000000063362000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
3&\displaystyle
\frac{684844492956276844937911327}
{8357142896914500000000000000000}\\[5pt]
4&\displaystyle
\frac{1176237780786628814433937669}
{14625000074031750000000000000000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{33.11}
\]

For the local variables use the six-vertex radius-\(1/8\) polygon
of Lemma~\ref{lem:newv28-arc-hull}.  Each block (33.5) is affine
separately in the three local phase pairs.  For each \(j\), all
\(6^3=216\) product-vertex blocks pass independent exact rational
congruence and Gram-residual certification.  Their common block
floor is \(\gamma_j/2\); in all three ledgers the weakest local
vertex is
\[
 (127/128,511/4096,\,
  127/128,-511/4096,\,
  127/128,-511/4096).                                  \tag{33.12}
\]
Physical-arc containment and iterated matrix convexity prove
(33.7).  Momentum inversion gives the signed partner.  Relation
(10.18) adds the exact reserve
\((2/3)(49/1250)=49/1875\), proving (33.9).
Direct exact comparison of the three fractions in (33.8) gives
(33.10).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Three new overlap edges]
\label{prop:newv33-overlaps}
Each plus tube in Theorem~\ref{thm:newv33-branches} has
nonempty open intersection with the V28 plus tube about
\(b_\circ\); the same holds after momentum inversion.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Every centre in (33.1) is at transverse \(L^\infty\) distance \(t\)

\[

\]
and the rational margin below the sum of radii is
\[
 \frac14-\frac{61123}{245760}
 =\frac{317}{245760}>0.                                \tag{33.13}
\]
Coordinatewise interval intersection proves the assertion.
\end{proof}

\section{V34: an exact finite atlas covers the first phase cube}
\label{sec:newv34-first-cube}

The V28, V30, and V33 charts occupy five vertices of a rational
phase cube.  This iteration constructs the remaining three
two-axis vertices and then proves that the eight chart domains cover
the entire cube, including the corner carrying V30's slightly
smaller radius.

\subsection{The three two-axis centres}

Retain \(b_\circ=(\pi,\pi,0)\), \(t=2\arctan(1/8)\), and
\(C=63/65\), \(S=16/65\).  Define
\[
\begin{split}
 a_{23}&=b_\circ+(-t,t,0),\\
 a_{24}&=b_\circ+(-t,0,t),\\
 a_{34}&=b_\circ+(0,t,t).
\end{split}                                             \tag{34.1}
\]
Their absolute phase triples are respectively
\[
\begin{split}
 &((-C,S),(-C,-S),(1,0)),\\
 &((-C,S),(-1,0),(C,S)),\\
 &((-1,0),(-C,-S),(C,S)).
\end{split}                                             \tag{34.2}
\]
Each entry is an exact rational unit phase.

For \(J\in\{23,24,34\}\), independently regroup the complete Wilson
bank about \(a_J\), rationalize a Hermitian splitting \(X_J^\#\),
and form the local block \({\cal Q}_J(v)\) by the construction
(33.4)--(33.5).

\begin{theorem}[Three exact two-axis tubes]
\label{thm:newv34-two-axis}
For each \(J\in\{23,24,34\}\), every active momentum \(k_1\), and
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^3}
 \bigl((k_2,k_3,k_4),a_J\bigr)\le\frac18,               \tag{34.3}
\]
the plus four-thirds Wilson pencil satisfies
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq\eta_JI_{45},        \tag{34.4}
\]
where
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
J&\eta_J\\ \hline
23&
\displaystyle
\frac{3323190119284031031100923176317}
{311500801619804160000000000000000000}\\[6pt]
24&
\displaystyle
\frac{25824825214799235761105173358923}
{1557504008072543232000000000000000000}\\[6pt]
34&
\displaystyle
\frac{123121862765924849720614914763}
{2496000012966720000000000000000000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{34.5}
\]
The minus pencil has the same bounds on the tubes about \(-a_J\).
The original constant-two pencil has lower bound
\((\eta_J+49/1875)I_{45}\) on each corresponding signed tube.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The independently certified centre block floors are
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
23&\displaystyle
\frac{130337454920988759281544298507}
{1521000007378371000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
24&\displaystyle
\frac{3551687086210436630889656507}
{42250000218305750000000000000000}\\[5pt]
34&\displaystyle
\frac{642966027437250401240006917}
{7800000039811200000000000000000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{34.6}
\]
For each block use the six-vertex physical arc hull
\({\cal G}_{1/8}\).  All \(6^3=216\) product vertices pass exact
rational congruence and Gram-residual certification.  The common
block floor is \(\eta_J/2\), and the weakest local variables are
\[
 (127/128,511/4096,\,
  127/128,-511/4096,\,
  127/128,-511/4096)                                   \tag{34.7}
\]
in every ledger.  Physical-arc containment, the product-hull lemma,
evaluation on \((w,zw)\), momentum inversion, and the exact Hessian
reserve prove the theorem.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The whole-cube covering argument}

Use positive coordinates \(s=(s_2,s_3,s_4)\) for the signed
transverse displacement
\[
 b_\circ+(-s_2,s_3,s_4),
\]
and define the closed physical phase cube
\[
 {\cal K}_t:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,s_3,s_4):0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le t\}.
                                                               \tag{34.8}
\]
For \(\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}^3\), let
\[
 a_\varepsilon
 =b_\circ+(-\varepsilon_2t,\varepsilon_3t,\varepsilon_4t).
                                                               \tag{34.9}
\]
The centre \(a_{000}\) is V28; the three Hamming-weight-one
centres are V33; the three Hamming-weight-two centres are
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv34-two-axis}; and \(a_{111}=b_+\) is V30.
Every chart except \(a_{111}\) has radius \(r=1/8\); its radius is
\(r_*=99/800\).

\begin{theorem}[Exact cover of the first physical phase cube]
\label{thm:newv34-cube-cover}
For every active momentum \(k_1\) and every
\((k_2,k_3,k_4)\in{\cal K}_t\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq
 \eta_{\rm cube} I_{45},                               \tag{34.10}
\]
where
\[
 \eta_{\rm cube}:=
\frac{3323190119284031031100923176317}
{311500801619804160000000000000000000}>0.              \tag{34.11}
\]
On the inverted cube \(-{\cal K}_t\), the same bound holds for the
minus pencil.  On both cubes the original pencil satisfies
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}(k)\succeq
 \left(\eta_{\rm cube}+\frac{49}{1875}\right)I_{45}.    \tag{34.12}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Take \(s\in[0,t]^3\).  If
\[
 \max_i(t-s_i)\le r_*,
\]
then \(s\) lies in the V30 chart about \((t,t,t)\).
Otherwise choose an index \(j\) with \(t-s_j>r_*\).  The exact V30
overlap estimate gives
\[
 t<r+r_*=\frac{199}{800},
\]
and hence
\[
 s_j<t-r_*<r.                                          \tag{34.13}
\]
Set \(\varepsilon_j=0\).  For each \(i\ne j\), choose
\(\varepsilon_i\in\{0,1\}\) so that \(s_i\) is nearest to
\(\varepsilon_it\).  Because \(t<2r=1/4\),
\[
 |s_i-\varepsilon_it|\le\frac t2<r.                    \tag{34.14}
\]
The selected vertex is not \(111\), so its chart radius is \(r\).
Equations (34.13)--(34.14) show
\[
 \|s-\varepsilon t\|_\infty<r.
\]
Thus the eight certified tubes cover \({\cal K}_t\).

The four families of chart floors entering this cover are those of
V28, V30, V33, and (34.5).  Exact fraction comparison shows their
minimum is \(\eta_{23}=\eta_{\rm cube}\).  Apply the relevant local
theorem at each point.  Momentum inversion and the Hessian reserve
give the remaining statements.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The phase-cube overlap graph is connected]
\label{prop:newv34-graph}
Every V34 two-axis chart overlaps the two incident V33 one-axis
charts and the V30 all-shifted chart with nonempty interior.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Adjacent Boolean vertices are at \(L^\infty\) distance \(t\).

\]
For the V30 corner, (30.17)--(30.18) give

\]
with rational margin \(49/1228800\).  Coordinatewise interval
intersection proves all stated edges.
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[First phase cube covered]
\label{ass:newv34}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv34-cube-cover} is stronger than a path or a
collection of overlapping tubes.  It proves positivity on every
point of a three-dimensional phase cube by an exact finite atlas of
eight constant noncommutative splittings.  The proof explicitly
repairs the only unequal chart radius.

The cube side \(t<1/4\) is still much smaller than the transverse
quarter-cell half-side \(\pi/4\).  Translating this first cube
without rebuilding the rational centre banks is not justified.
The remaining finite-dimensional problem is to extend the phase
lattice through powers two and three in each coordinate and then
control all sign octants or prove a conjugating symmetry.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V35 audits companions and designs the cofinal phase lattice]
\label{dec:newv34-next}
Perform the compulsory SM/NS audit.  Then avoid a blind
\(4^3\)-centre enumeration.  Test exact hypercubic conjugacies of the
direct active-circle coefficient bank under transverse coordinate
permutations and sign changes.  Any accepted symmetry must include
an explicit rational unitary or signed permutation and must
transport both the pencil and the splitting block.

Use the proved symmetry group, if nontrivial, to select orbit
representatives among rational phase-power centres
\[
 b_\circ+(-n_2t,n_3t,n_4t),\qquad n_j\in\{0,1,2,3\}.
                                                               \tag{34.19}
\]
The ultimate finite target is a certified cover through the
quarter-cell faces, followed by the exact quarter-grid translation
to the transverse torus.  If no useful conjugacy survives, batch the
centres with independent ledgers rather than claiming symmetry from
similar floors.
\end{decision}

\section{V35: companion audit and an exact symmetry obstruction}

\subsection{Compulsory companion audit}
