SM V10 audited YM V33 and imported only its atlas discipline:
independent coordinate directions and pairwise overlaps do not by
themselves cover a higher-dimensional cell.  V34 has since supplied
the missing pointwise interior argument for the first phase cube.
SM V11 proves an abstract refresh-free cluster-gap compiler for
bounded motifs, and SM V12 proves a finite coloring and fractional
coverage bound for the bare bounded head.  Its determinant/control
modulus and Wilsonian shell tail still lack a uniform weighted
boundary-influence norm.

\begin{audit}[V35 audit verdict]
\label{aud:newv35-verdict}
No SM coefficient, conditional kernel, cluster clock, or spectral
constant acts on the Wilson active-circle pencil, and none is
imported.  The useful logical separation is retained:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item a graph of overlaps is weaker than pointwise coverage of the
      region between chart centres;
\item a finite bare cover is weaker than a cofinal local gap when
      a quasilocal tail lacks a summable influence norm; and
\item a refresh-free local gap is the relevant downstream object,
      not an auxiliary nonlocal mixing floor.
\end{enumerate}
NS V26 is unchanged and contains no common matrix, measure, or
implication with the present phase-lattice problem.
\end{audit}

\subsection{Exact invariants of the one-axis centre pencils}

Let \(P_j(z)\) be the plus four-thirds Wilson pencil at the exact
one-axis centre \(a_j\) of (33.1), for \(j=2,3,4\).  For
\[
 z\in\{1,i,-1,-i\},                                    \tag{35.3}
\]
compute in rational arithmetic
\[
 \tau_j(z)=\Tr P_j(z),\qquad
 \nu_j(z)=\Tr(P_j(z)^2)=\|P_j(z)\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2.     \tag{35.4}
\]
Hermiticity is checked before evaluating (35.4).

\begin{theorem}[Exact transverse symmetry screen]
\label{thm:newv35-screen}
For every phase in (35.3),
\[
 \tau_2(z)=\tau_3(z)=\tau_4(z)=120.                     \tag{35.5}
\]
The second invariants for axes \(2\) and \(3\) agree exactly:
\[
\begin{array}{c|cccc}
z&1&i&-1&-i\\ \hline
\nu_2(z)=\nu_3(z)&
\frac{260878}{585}&
\frac{2254172}{5265}&
\frac{2247802}{5265}&
\frac{2341532}{5265}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{35.6}
\]
For axis \(4\),
\[
\begin{array}{c|cccc}
z&1&i&-1&-i\\ \hline
\nu_4(z)&
\frac{2344318}{5265}&
\frac{750196}{1755}&
\frac{2244218}{5265}&
\frac{259772}{585}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{35.7}
\]
At every listed active phase,
\[
 \nu_2(z)-\nu_4(z)=\nu_3(z)-\nu_4(z)
 =\frac{3584}{5265}>0.                                 \tag{35.8}
\]
Consequently no unitary conjugacy, and in particular no signed
permutation conjugacy, can carry the axis-\(2\) or axis-\(3\)
centre pencil to the axis-\(4\) centre pencil while fixing the
active phase.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Regroup the complete direct Wilson coefficient bank at each of the
three exact rational phase triples (33.3).  Evaluate
\[
 P_j(z)=A_j+zB_j+\bar zB_j^*
\]
at each phase in (35.3).  Exact Laurent pairing makes every matrix
Hermitian.  Direct summation of its diagonal and squared moduli of
all entries gives (35.5)--(35.7).  Converting the entries of (35.7)
to denominator \(5265\) and subtracting gives (35.8).

If \(P_4(z)=U^*P_2(z)U\) for a unitary \(U\), cyclicity of trace
would give
\[
 \Tr(P_4(z)^2)=\Tr(P_2(z)^2),
\]
contradicting (35.8).  A signed permutation is unitary, so it is
excluded as well.  The same argument applies to axis \(3\).
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[What the invariant match does not prove]
\label{fire:newv35-match}
Equality of \(\tau_2,\tau_3\) and \(\nu_2,\nu_3\) at four active
phases is necessary but not sufficient for a conjugacy of the
complete Laurent pencils.  It neither constructs an intertwiner nor
proves equality of characteristic polynomials.  Thus the possible
axis-\(2\)/axis-\(3\) reduction remains open.

The obstruction involving axis \(4\) is only for conjugacies which
fix the active phase and the present reduced inner product.  A
general nonunitary congruence could preserve Loewner positivity, but
it would require an explicit invertible matrix and quantitative
condition-number control before transporting a floor.
\end{firewall}

\begin{corollary}[No full transverse permutation reduction]
\label{cor:newv35-no-s3}
The \(4^3\) phase-power lattice in (34.19) cannot be reduced by
assuming a unitary action of the full transverse permutation group
on the present active-coordinate pencil.  At minimum, centres which
move the fourth transverse coordinate must be treated in a separate
orbit from centres which move only coordinates two and three.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
A full transverse permutation action would contain a permutation
interchanging coordinate \(4\) with coordinate \(2\) or \(3\) while
fixing the active coordinate.  Theorem~\ref{thm:newv35-screen}
excludes the required unitary similarity already at a one-axis
centre.
\end{proof}

\begin{record}[V35 exact provenance]
\label{rec:newv35-provenance}
The executable invariant screen is
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v35_transverse_symmetry_invariants.py}.
\end{center}
Its source and canonical-payload SHA--256 values are
\[
\begin{gathered}
\texttt{AF04810FBD7E196849B5C5172B718957ED39C5321A71272F9DFA914E643370D8},\\
\texttt{76A476CB4D821BB04453605739BF9018BD4E31F41009A691308EE576A36379C6}.
\end{gathered}                                          \tag{35.9}
\]
The payload binds the companion hashes (35.1)--(35.2), the exact

\end{record}

\begin{assessment}[The symmetry shortcut is sharply limited]
\label{ass:newv35}
The most optimistic \(S_3\) orbit reduction is false in the fixed
active-coordinate representation.  The fourth transverse direction
must be certified separately.  This agrees with the unequal V33 and
V34 ledgers, but V35 upgrades that observation to a direct-pencil
unitary-conjugacy obstruction independent of any chosen splitting.

There may still be a useful exchange of axes \(2\) and \(3\), or a
nonunitary gauge-restoring congruence inherited from the unreduced
hypercube edge space.  Neither may be used until its exact
intertwining identity and floor transport are proved.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V36 resolves the surviving exchange and starts the next slab]
\label{dec:newv35-next}
Lift the reduced \(45\)-coordinate pencil to the \(64\)-edge
hypercube incidence representation.  Apply the coordinate exchange
\(2\leftrightarrow3\), restore the declared tree gauge, and derive
the induced rational \(45\times45\) map \(T_{23}\).  Test the exact
Laurent identity
\[
 P_3(z;k_3,k_2,k_4)
 =T_{23}^*P_2(z;k_2,k_3,k_4)T_{23}.                    \tag{35.10}
\]
If \(T_{23}\) is unitary in the certified metric, use it to reduce
orbits.  If it is merely invertible, record exact singular-value
bounds before transporting Loewner floors.  If (35.10) fails,
close the symmetry route and batch all centres independently.

In parallel with that algebraic test, begin the next phase slab with
centres whose power triple lies in
\(\{0,1,2\}\times\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}\), certifying any new
power-two face before inferring a larger cell cover.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V35]
\label{fire:newv35-final}
V35 performs the required companion audit and excludes a full
unitary transverse-permutation reduction.  It adds no new phase
cube beyond V34.  The quarter cell and transverse torus remain
uncovered.  No global covariant inequality, cofinal response sign,
compact graph-screen, interacting continuum measure,
reflection-positive limit, exponential clustering, or Hamiltonian
Yang--Mills mass gap has been proved.  The full mass-gap goal
remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V36: the surviving symmetry fails and the second phase slab}
\label{sec:newv36-obstruction-slab}

V35 left one possible unitary identification unresolved because the
first two spectral moments of the \(a_2\) and \(a_3\) centre pencils
agree.  We first compute the next moment and then go upstream to the
\(64\)-edge representation to test the actual gauge-restored
coordinate exchange.  Both tests close the proposed symmetry
shortcut.  We therefore follow the independent-chart branch of
Decision~\ref{dec:newv35-next} and certify the four new vertices of
the next phase slab.

\subsection{The cubic trace separates the remaining branches}

Retain the V33 centre pencils \(P_j(z):=P_j(z;0)\), \(j=2,3\), and
the four exact active phases
\[
 {\cal Z}_4:=\{1,i,-1,-i\}.                            \tag{36.1}
\]
Define the third spectral moment
\[
 \rho_j(z):=\Tr(P_j(z)^3).                             \tag{36.2}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact closure of the unitary symmetry screen]
\label{thm:newv36-cubic-screen}
For every \(z\in{\cal Z}_4\), the first and second moments of
\(P_2(z)\) and \(P_3(z)\) agree as in V35, but
\[
 \rho_2(z)-\rho_3(z)=-\frac{1864}{15795}\ne0.          \tag{36.3}
\]
More explicitly,
\[
\begin{array}{c|cc}
z&\rho_2(z)&\rho_3(z)\\ \hline
1&\dfrac{29127308}{15795}&\dfrac{9709724}{5265}\\[4pt]
i&\dfrac{26502868}{15795}&\dfrac{26504732}{15795}\\[4pt]
-1&\dfrac{8774836}{5265}&\dfrac{26326372}{15795}\\[4pt]
-i&\dfrac{28948948}{15795}&\dfrac{28950812}{15795}.
\end{array}                                            \tag{36.4}
\]
Consequently, at every phase in (36.1), \(P_2(z)\) and \(P_3(z)\)
are not similar.  In particular there is no unitary or signed
permutation conjugacy between these two centre pencils at fixed
active phase.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Regroup the complete direct Wilson coefficient bank at the two exact
rational phase triples in (33.3).  Matrix multiplication in
\(\mathbb Q(i)\) gives the entries of (36.4); every imaginary trace
vanishes exactly.  Converting the two entries in each row to a common
denominator gives (36.3).  If
\(P_3(z)=S^{-1}P_2(z)S\), cyclicity of trace would imply
\(\Tr(P_3(z)^3)=\Tr(P_2(z)^3)\), a contradiction.  A unitary
congruence is a similarity because \(U^*=U^{-1}\), and a signed
permutation is unitary.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[All three one-axis branches are distinct under the
fixed-phase unitary screen]
\label{cor:newv36-three-orbits}
No two of the three V33 centre pencils can be identified by a
fixed-active-phase unitary coordinate permutation.  The pair
\((2,3)\) is separated by Theorem~\ref{thm:newv36-cubic-screen};
the pairs involving axis \(4\) are separated already by the second
moment in (35.8).
\end{corollary}

\begin{firewall}[Scope of the cubic obstruction]
\label{fire:newv36-cubic}
Equation (36.3) excludes ordinary similarity and hence unitary
congruence at the four stated active phases.  It does not exclude an
arbitrary nonunitary congruence \(P_3=T^*P_2T\).  Pointwise positive
definite matrices are always related by some such congruence; a
useful floor transport would require a geometrically specified,
uniformly controlled family.  Phase-changing reflections are also
not identified with fixed-phase similarities here.
\end{firewall}

\subsection{The exact re-tree map and its missing response connection}

Let
\[
 {\cal E}=\{(s,\mu):s\in\{0,1\}^4,\ 0\le\mu\le3\}
                                                               \tag{36.5}
\]
be the \(64\) fine-edge types.  Use the same filling tree as the
incidence construction underlying the Wilson Hessian: its
\(15\) tree edges are eliminated, \(45\) reduced edges are retained,
and four second-constituent boundary edges are kept only while
constructing the gauge restoration.  Let
\[
 \tau_{23}=(0,2,1,3)                                  \tag{36.6}
\]
in zero-based coordinates.

For a momentum phase \(q\), first permute all edge starts and
directions by \(\tau_{23}\).  If \(a_T\) denotes the values induced
on the declared tree and \(d_T(\tau_{23}q)\) is the nonroot
tree-gradient matrix, use the unique tree-restoring parameter
\[
 \phi(q)=-d_T(\tau_{23}q)^{-1}a_T.                    \tag{36.7}
\]
Projecting the permuted field plus \(d(\tau_{23}q)\phi(q)\) back to
the \(45\) reduced coordinates defines the exact map
\({\cal T}_{23}(q)\).

\begin{theorem}[The geometric swap preserves the Hessian but not the
naive response congruence]
\label{thm:newv36-retree}
At each of the two V33 transverse triples \(a_2,a_3\) and each
\(z\in{\cal Z}_4\), the map \({\cal T}_{23}(q)\) is exactly
invertible over \(\mathbb Q(i)\).  At all eight points the Wilson
Hessian satisfies
\[
 H_{\mathrm W}(q)
 ={\cal T}_{23}(q)^*
  H_{\mathrm W}(\tau_{23}q){\cal T}_{23}(q).           \tag{36.8}
\]
The analogous identity for the direct plus four-thirds pencil fails
at all eight points.  For example, at
\[
 q=(1;\,-63/65+16i/65,\,-1,\,1),                      \tag{36.9}
\]
put
\[
 R(q):=P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(q)
 -{\cal T}_{23}(q)^*
  P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(\tau_{23}q)
  {\cal T}_{23}(q).                                   \tag{36.10}
\]
Then \(R(q)\) has twelve nonzero entries and, in one-based reduced
coordinates,
\[
 R(q)_{4,22}=\frac{8-i}{195}\ne0.                     \tag{36.11}
\]
Thus the re-tree map cannot transport the certified pencil floor by
the naive congruence proposed in (35.10).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The tree edges in (36.7) do not cross a coarse-cell boundary, so
\(d_T\) is an integer \(15\times15\) matrix.  Exact Gaussian
elimination constructs (36.7).  Apply the self-inverse permutation a
second time and restore the original tree.  Direct multiplication
gives the \(45\times45\) identity at all eight points, proving
invertibility.

Build the \(96\times64\) plaquette incidence, form its Wilson Gram
matrix, and reduce it to the declared \(45\) coordinates.  Exact
complex-rational multiplication gives zero residual in (36.8) at
all eight points.  Build the direct response coefficient bank with
the active exponent and parity-rate correction used since V19.
The eight congruence residuals are nonzero; direct enumeration at
(36.9) gives twelve nonzero entries and (36.11).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Why a moving-tree connection is unavoidable]
\label{prop:newv36-connection}
Suppose on an active arc that a differentiable moving-frame identity
has the form
\[
 H(q)={\cal T}(q)^*\widetilde H(q){\cal T}(q).          \tag{36.12}
\]
Then ordinary differentiation gives
\[
\begin{split}
 \partial_1H={}&
 {\cal T}^*(\partial_1\widetilde H){\cal T}\\
 &+(\partial_1{\cal T}^*)\widetilde H{\cal T}
   +{\cal T}^*\widetilde H(\partial_1{\cal T}).
\end{split}                                            \tag{36.13}
\]
Consequently Hessian covariance alone never justifies deleting the
two moving-frame terms in a response-pencil transport.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Equation (36.13) is the product rule applied entrywise to (36.12).
The exact residual (36.11) confirms that the zero-connection
specialization is false for the present fixed-tree direct pencil.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[What is and is not identified upstream]
\label{fire:newv36-retree}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv36-retree} proves failure of the specified
geometric re-tree congruence, not failure of covariance of the
underlying physical theory.  A correctly connection-completed
response may have a transformation law, but it must be derived
before it can move a Loewner floor.  V36 neither invents such a
completion nor uses the Hessian identity as a pencil certificate.
\end{firewall}

\subsection{Four new charts and a two-by-one-by-one slab}

Let \(t=2\arctan(1/8)\), \(b_\circ=(\pi,\pi,0)\), and for
\(n=(n_2,n_3,n_4)\) define
\[
 a_n=b_\circ+(-n_2t,n_3t,n_4t).                       \tag{36.14}
\]
The four new power triples are
\[
 {\cal N}_2=\{(2,0,0),(2,1,0),(2,0,1),(2,1,1)\}.       \tag{36.15}
\]
Writing \(C=63/65\), \(S=16/65\), the common first phase at power two
is
\[
 -\,(C-iS)^2=-\frac{3713}{4225}
                 +i\frac{2016}{4225}.                 \tag{36.16}
\]
The other two phases are independently selected from
\((-1,0),(-C,-S)\) and \((1,0),(C,S)\), respectively, according to
\((n_3,n_4)\).

\begin{theorem}[Four exact second-slab vertex tubes]
\label{thm:newv36-four-charts}
For each \(n\in{\cal N}_2\), every active momentum \(k_1\), and
\[
 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^3}
 \bigl((k_2,k_3,k_4),a_n\bigr)\le\frac18,              \tag{36.17}
\]
the plus four-thirds Wilson pencil satisfies
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq\gamma_n I_{45},    \tag{36.18}
\]
where
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
n&\gamma_n\\ \hline
200&
\dfrac{12901409161901593393106798474651}
{259584001349836800000000000000000000}\\[6pt]
210&
\dfrac{511152788711067723119418639710129}
{20247552105975687168000000000000000000}\\[6pt]
201&
\dfrac{534449256719047568020380278474243}
{20247552104882319360000000000000000000}\\[6pt]
211&
\dfrac{53543524052354523326188125464407}
{20247552105550488576000000000000000000}.
\end{array}                                            \tag{36.19}
\]
The minus pencil has the same bounds about the inverted centres.
The original constant-two pencil gains the reserve \(49/1875\) at
each corresponding point.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At every centre in (36.15), regroup the complete direct coefficient
bank, independently construct a rational Hermitian splitting, and
certify its centre block.  Use the six-vertex physical arc hull of
V28 in each of the three transverse variables.  All
\[
 4\cdot6^3=864                                         \tag{36.20}
\]
product-vertex blocks pass exact rational rounded-basis congruence
and Gram-residual certification.  Their doubled minimum block
floors are precisely (36.19).  Iterated matrix convexity proves
(36.18), momentum inversion gives the signed partner, and (10.18)
adds the stated Hessian reserve.
\end{proof}

Define the second cube and the extended slab by
\[
\begin{split}
 {\cal K}^{(2)}_t
  &:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,s_3,s_4):
       t\le s_2\le2t,\ 0\le s_3,s_4\le t\},\\
 {\cal S}_{2,1,1}
  &:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,s_3,s_4):
       0\le s_2\le2t,\ 0\le s_3,s_4\le t\}.
\end{split}                                            \tag{36.21}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact pointwise cover of the extended phase slab]
\label{thm:newv36-slab-cover}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal S}_{2,1,1}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\gamma_{2,1,1}I_{45},                         \tag{36.22}
\]
with
\[
 \gamma_{2,1,1}:=
\frac{53543524052354523326188125464407}
{20247552105550488576000000000000000000}>0.           \tag{36.23}
\]
The inverted slab obeys the same bound for the minus pencil, and the
original pencil has floor \(\gamma_{2,1,1}+49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
V34 covers the part of (36.21) with \(0\le s_2\le t\).
For the second cube use local coordinates
\[
 x=(s_2-t,s_3,s_4)\in[0,t]^3.                         \tag{36.24}
\]
Its four \(x_2=0\) vertices are the already certified power triples
with \(n_2=1\), and its four \(x_2=t\) vertices are
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv36-four-charts}.  The only radius smaller than
\(r=1/8\) is the V30 chart at power triple \(111\), located at
\((0,t,t)\) in (36.24), with radius \(r_*=99/800\).

Reflect \(x_2\mapsto t-x_2\).  This sends the exceptional corner to
\((t,t,t)\) without changing any \(L^\infty\) distances.  The exact
V34 covering proof applies because
\[
 t<2r,\qquad t<r+r_*                                  \tag{36.25}
\]
by (33.13) and (30.17).  Hence the eight second-cube tubes cover
\({\cal K}^{(2)}_t\) pointwise.  Union with the V34 cube covers the
whole slab.  Exact comparison of the old and new chart floors gives
the minimum (36.23).
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[The atlas expands after the symmetry route closes]
\label{ass:newv36}
The V35 equality of two low spectral moments was accidental at the
level needed for unitary conjugacy: the cubic moment separates the
two remaining branches.  The upstream incidence calculation then
pinpoints why even the natural nonunitary re-tree map cannot be used
naively on the response pencil.  No symmetry reduction is used in
the new atlas.

Independently, the pointwise covered region has doubled in the
\(s_2\) direction.  This is a genuine three-dimensional slab, not
only an overlap graph.  Its side lengths are \(2t,t,t\); it still
does not cover a transverse quarter cell.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V37 reaches the first quarter-cell face]
\label{dec:newv36-next}
Certify independently the four power-three centres
\[
 (3,0,0),\quad(3,1,0),\quad(3,0,1),\quad(3,1,1),       \tag{36.30}
\]
and use their tubes to cover the third \(s_2\)-slab.  In addition to
the three-cube cover, prove with explicit rational bounds for
\(\pi\) and \(\arctan(1/8)\) that the outward faces of those charts
reach \(s_2=\pi/4\).  This will close the first
\([0,\pi/4]\times[0,t]^2\) plate.

Then repeat the independent power-two and power-three expansion in
\(s_3\) and \(s_4\).  Do not resurrect a transverse orbit reduction
unless a connection-completed pencil identity is proved.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V36]
\label{fire:newv36-final}
V36 closes the fixed-phase unitary symmetry shortcut, exhibits the
failure of the naive re-tree pencil congruence, and proves a
pointwise positive two-by-one-by-one phase slab.  It does not cover
a quarter cell or the transverse torus.  No global covariant
inequality, cofinal response sign, compact graph-screen, interacting
continuum measure, reflection-positive limit, exponential
clustering, or Hamiltonian Yang--Mills mass gap has been proved.
The full Yang--Mills mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V37: four power-three charts reach the first quarter face}
\label{sec:newv37-first-face}

V36 covered the two-by-one-by-one slab
\({\cal S}_{2,1,1}\).  This iteration constructs the next four
vertices without symmetry identifications, covers the third
\(s_2\)-cube, and then closes the short residual strip between the
power-three centres and the geometric face \(s_2=\pi/4\).

\subsection{Exact angular inequalities}

We first isolate every transcendental comparison used in the cover.

\begin{lemma}[Rational brackets for the step and the quarter face]
\label{lem:newv37-angle}
For \(t=2\arctan(1/8)\),
\[
 \frac{191}{768}<t<\frac14,                            \tag{37.1}
\]
and
\[
 0<\frac{\pi}{4}-3t
 <\frac{71}{1792}<\frac18.                            \tag{37.2}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For \(0<x\le1/8\),
\[
 1-u^2<\frac1{1+u^2}<1\qquad(0<u\le x),
\]
because the difference between the middle term and \(1-u^2\) is
\(u^4/(1+u^2)>0\).  Integration gives
\[
 x-\frac{x^3}{3}<\arctan x<x.
\]
At \(x=1/8\), multiplication by two proves (37.1).

For completeness, the elementary rational bounds
\[
 3<\pi<\frac{22}{7}                                   \tag{37.3}
\]
can be obtained directly from integrals.  Indeed, on \(0\le x\le1\),
\[
 \frac1{1+x^2}>
 \sum_{j=0}^{7}(-1)^j x^{2j},
\]
and therefore
\[
 \pi=4\int_0^1\frac{dx}{1+x^2}
 >
 4\sum_{j=0}^{7}\frac{(-1)^j}{2j+1}
 =\frac{135904}{45045}>3.                             \tag{37.4}
\]
Also
\[
 \frac{22}{7}-\pi
 =\int_0^1\frac{x^4(1-x)^4}{1+x^2}\,dx>0,             \tag{37.5}
\]
where polynomial division verifies the equality.

The upper half of (37.1) and the lower half of (37.3) give
\[
 3t<\frac34<\frac{\pi}{4}.
\]
The lower half of (37.1) and the upper half of (37.3) give
\[
 \frac{\pi}{4}-3t
 <\frac{11}{14}-\frac{191}{256}
 =\frac{71}{1792}<\frac18,
\]
which proves (37.2).
\end{proof}

\subsection{The four power-three centres}

Retain
\[
 a_n=b_\circ+(-n_2t,n_3t,n_4t),
 \qquad b_\circ=(\pi,\pi,0).                           \tag{37.6}
\]
The new power set is
\[
 {\cal N}_3=\{(3,0,0),(3,1,0),(3,0,1),(3,1,1)\}.       \tag{37.7}
\]
With \(C=63/65\) and \(S=16/65\), their common first
transverse phase is
\[
 -\,(C-iS)^3
 =-\frac{201663}{274625}
   +i\frac{186416}{274625}.                            \tag{37.8}
\]
The second and third transverse phases are selected independently
from
\[
 (-1,0),\ (-63/65,-16/65)
 \quad\hbox{and}\quad
 (1,0),\ (63/65,16/65),                               \tag{37.9}
\]
according to \(n_3,n_4\).

\label{thm:newv37-four-charts}
For every \(n\in{\cal N}_3\), every active momentum \(k_1\), and

 d_{\infty,\mathbb T^3}
 \bigl((k_2,k_3,k_4),a_n\bigr)\le\frac18,              \tag{37.10}
\]

\]
where

\]
The common new-chart floor is \(\delta_{311}\).  The minus pencil

constant-two pencil gains \(49/1875\) on each signed tube.

\begin{proof}
For each of the four exact phase triples (37.8)--(37.9), regroup the
complete direct Wilson bank and independently construct a rational
Hermitian splitting.  Each centre block is exactly positive.
Apply the V28 six-vertex physical arc hull in all three local
variables.  All
\[
 4\cdot6^3=864                                         \tag{37.13}
\]
product-vertex blocks pass the exact rational rounded-basis
congruence and Gram-residual test.  Doubling their minimum block
floors gives (37.12).  Exact fraction comparison selects
\(\delta_{311}\).  Iterated matrix convexity, momentum inversion,
and the Hessian reserve prove the remaining claims.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Third-cube and quarter-face coverage}

Define
\[
\begin{split}
 {\cal K}^{(3)}_t
 &:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,s_3,s_4):
       2t\le s_2\le3t,\ 0\le s_3,s_4\le t\},\\
 {\cal P}_{1}
 &:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,s_3,s_4):
       0\le s_2\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_3,s_4\le t\}.
\end{split}                                            \tag{37.14}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact pointwise cover of the first quarter-face
plate]
\label{thm:newv37-plate-cover}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal P}_1\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\delta_{\rm plate}I_{45},                      \tag{37.15}
\]
where
\[
 \delta_{\rm plate}:=
\frac{53543524052354523326188125464407}
{20247552105550488576000000000000000000}>0.           \tag{37.16}
\]
The inverted plate obeys the same bound for the minus pencil, and
the original pencil has floor
\(\delta_{\rm plate}+49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
First consider \({\cal K}^{(3)}_t\).  Its four \(s_2=2t\)
vertices are the V36 power-two charts, and its four \(s_2=3t\)
vertices are Theorem~\ref{thm:newv37-four-charts}.  All eight have
radius \(r=1/8\).  In local cube coordinates, select the nearest
endpoint in each coordinate.  The distance to the selected vertex
is at most
\[
 \frac t2<\frac18                                      \tag{37.17}
\]
by Lemma~\ref{lem:newv37-angle}.  Hence the third cube is covered.
Together with V36, this covers
\[
 0\le s_2\le3t,\qquad0\le s_3,s_4\le t.
\]

It remains to cover \(3t\le s_2\le\pi/4\).  Select the power-three
centre with \(n_3,n_4\in\{0,1\}\) nearest to \(s_3/t,s_4/t\).
The last two coordinate distances are bounded by \(t/2<1/8\),
while Lemma~\ref{lem:newv37-angle} gives
\[
 0\le s_2-3t\le\frac{\pi}{4}-3t
 <\frac{71}{1792}<\frac18.                            \tag{37.18}
\]
Thus one of the four power-three tubes contains every point in the
residual strip, proving the pointwise cover of \({\cal P}_1\).

The new minimum \(\delta_{311}\) is strictly larger than the V36
slab floor.  Exact fraction comparison therefore leaves the uniform
plate floor equal to (37.16).
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[Two transverse quarter faces are now closed]
\label{ass:newv39}
The certified pointwise region is now
\[
 [0,\pi/4]^2\times[0,t].
\]
Thus two transverse coordinates reach their genuine quarter-cell
faces, including the two-face corner.  This is an exact
finite-dimensional Wilson-penc\-il result and uses no unproved
coordinate-permutation symmetry.

The third coordinate still has width only \(t\).  Even a completed
transverse quarter cell would remain only one local ingredient:
global torus propagation and all continuum mass-gap bridges would
still have to be proved.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V40 audits upstream programmes, then starts the third layer]

Absent a stronger imported reduction, independently certify the

 n_2,n_3\in\{0,1,2,3\},\qquad n_4=2,                  \tag{39.17}

and attempt to widen (39.8) to

\section{V40: companion audit and the third-coordinate power-two layer}
\label{sec:newv40-third-layer}

This is the required five-version checkpoint.  The companion files
were inspected before the next chart decision.  Their useful
principles are retained at the level at which they are actually
proved; neither companion programme supplies a Yang--Mills phase
chart.

\subsection{Mandatory companion audit}

\begin{audit}[Active companion checkpoints]
\label{aud:newv40-companions}
At the time of the V40 decision, the active Standard-Model file was
\[
\begin{gathered}
\texttt{Renewal\_SM\_Einstein\_Continuum\_Research\_Notes\_V38.tex},\\
391388\ {\rm bytes},\qquad11230\ {\rm lines},\\
\texttt{273CF5028368FE295D511C70F540E227416D73CCA57DCF838DB9EC503DC9CF0E}.
\end{gathered}                                           \tag{40.1}
\]
The active Navier--Stokes file was
\[
\begin{gathered}
\texttt{Renewal\_NS\_Stability\_Research\_Notes\_V26.tex},\\
278283\ {\rm bytes},\qquad5319\ {\rm lines},\\
\texttt{82C887F8C2C69E4F28FAD7C3DC8DE5B9099CB5A4BF431EBA1FFF3E91935978AD}.
\end{gathered}                                           \tag{40.2}
\]
SM V38 proves a \(C^1\) trace-ideal compiler and derives a
distributional Ward identity from an exact or vanishing covariance
defect.  It explicitly leaves the uniform physical trace-norm
majorant and covariance-defect limit unproved.  NS V26 computes
norms matched to rank-one Oseen-kernel inputs and shows that such an
input-class restriction can sharpen a second-derivative constant,
while not changing the global conclusion.
\end{audit}

\begin{theorem}[V40 nonimport and acquisition decision]
\label{thm:newv40-audit-decision}
Neither companion result implies a new Wilson-penc\-il chart,
coordinate permutation, or continuum Yang--Mills estimate.
Accordingly:
\begin{enumerate}
\item no companion theorem is imported into the finite-dimensional
      chart proof;
\item the SM lesson is retained as a future continuum gate: a
      scalar determinant or local symbol bound cannot replace a
      uniform operator-valued remainder and its first variation;
\item the NS lesson is retained as a possible future refinement:
      a smaller physical-subspace norm may be used only after the
      Yang--Mills constraint factorization defining that input class
      is proved.
\end{enumerate}
Thus the next in-scope move remains direct certification of the
\(n_4=2\) layer.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The hypotheses of the SM compiler concern trace-class heat
remainders, common carriers, and covariance defects; none is a
statement about the present rational \(45\times45\) Wilson pencil.
The NS estimate concerns contractions of Oseen-kernel derivatives
with rank-one tensors; no identification of that tensor class with
the gauge-fixed Wilson constraint range is present.  Consequently
neither theorem has a premise that applies to a transverse phase
chart.  The two retained lessons are implications conditional on
future Yang--Mills hypotheses, not imported conclusions.
\end{proof}

\]

\label{cth:newv40-isotropic-failure}
The V28 product polygon of radius \(r\) does not certify the

\[
 (127/128,511/4096,\,

\[

\begin{proof}
The verifier reconstructs each rational Hermitian vertex block and

using exact fractions.  A negative quadratic form excludes positive

floating arithmetic is used only to propose the integer witnesses.
\end{proof}

 t<\frac{61123}{245760}

\subsection{Pointwise cover of the widened third-coordinate prism}

Define
\[
 {\cal P}_{234}^{(2)}:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,s_3,s_4):
 0\le s_2,s_3\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_4\le2t\}.               \tag{40.14}
\]

\begin{lemma}[The three-special-vertex cube repair]
\label{lem:newv40-special-cover}
The nine cubes
\[
 [jt,(j+1)t]\times[\ell t,(\ell+1)t]\times[t,2t],
 \qquad j,\ell\in\{0,1,2\},                            \tag{40.15}
\]
are covered by their chart boxes when the lower \(111\) box has
radii \((\rho,\rho,\rho)\), the upper \(112\) and \(212\) boxes
have the radii in (40.7), and all other boxes have radii
\((r,r,r)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For a point in one of the cubes, choose a coordinatewise nearest
vertex and let \(d_2,d_3,d_4\le t/2<r\) be its three local
distances.  An ordinary nearest vertex covers the point.

If the nearest vertex is \(111\), it covers unless some
\(d_q>\rho\).  If \(q=2\) or \(3\), flip that coordinate: the new
lower vertex is ordinary, the flipped distance is
\(t-d_q<t-\rho<r\), and the other distances are below \(r\).  If
only \(d_4>\rho\), the \(112\) box covers because
\(d_2,d_3\le\rho\) and \(t-d_4<t-\rho<r\).

If the nearest vertex is \(112\), only coordinates \(2\) or \(3\)
can fail.  Flipping coordinate \(3\) reaches an ordinary upper
vertex.  Flipping coordinate \(2\) also reaches an ordinary upper
vertex except when it reaches \(212\).  In that exceptional case,
the \(212\) box covers if \(d_4\le\rho\); if \(d_4>\rho\), also flip
coordinate \(4\), reaching the ordinary lower vertex \(211\).
Every flipped distance is below \(t-\rho<r\).

If the nearest vertex is \(212\), only coordinate \(4\) can fail;
flipping it reaches ordinary \(211\), with distance below
\(t-\rho<r\).  These cases exhaust the special vertices and prove
the cover.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact two-step third-coordinate prism]
\label{thm:newv40-widened-prism}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal P}_{234}^{(2)}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{40}I_{45},                             \tag{40.16}
\]
where
\[
 \theta_{40}:=
\frac{38967606503323447966954527325371479}
{54398423320315073925120000000000000000000}>0.         \tag{40.17}
\]
The inverted prism has the same minus-pencil bound, and the
original pencil has floor \(\theta_{40}+49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
V39 covers \(0\le s_4\le t\).  Lemma
\ref{lem:newv40-special-cover} covers the rational core
\[
 0\le s_2,s_3\le3t,\qquad t\le s_4\le2t.               \tag{40.18}
\]
For the first-coordinate cap \(3t\le s_2\le\pi/4\), choose
\(n_2=3\), the nearest \(n_3\in\{0,1,2,3\}\) and the nearest
\(n_4\in\{1,2\}\).  All selected boxes are ordinary.  The latter
two distances are at most \(t/2<r\), while
\[
 0\le s_2-3t\le\frac{\pi}{4}-3t
 <\frac{71}{1792}<r.                                   \tag{40.19}
\]
For the second-coordinate cap \(3t\le s_3\le\pi/4\), set \(n_3=3\),
choose the nearest \(n_4\in\{1,2\}\), and choose the nearest
\(n_2\in\{0,1,2,3\}\), using \(n_2=3\) and (40.19) on its cap.
Again all selected boxes are ordinary, and the identical estimate
holds in coordinate \(3\).  This includes the cap corner and covers
the whole new strip.  Union with V39 proves (40.16).

The new exact minimum \(\eta_{212}\) is smaller than the V39 plate
floor.  Hence their exact minimum is (40.17).
\end{proof}

\section{V41: closure of one transverse quarter cell}
\label{sec:newv41-quarter-cell}

V40 covered the selected displacement prism through the second
power of the rational phase.  This iteration constructs the entire
third-power face independently, fills the remaining slab, and
closes the selected transverse quarter cell.  The word
\emph{selected} is essential: no unproved translation, reflection,
or coordinate-permutation transport is used.

 r=\frac18,\qquad t=2\arctan r,\qquad

\]

\[
 \left(\frac{63+16i}{65}\right)^3
 =\frac{201663}{274625}
   +i\frac{186416}{274625}.                            \tag{41.2}
\]

\subsection{The last slab and the quarter-cell cover}

Define
\[
 {\cal C}_{234}^{+}:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,s_3,s_4):
          0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le\pi/4\}.                 \tag{41.6}
\]

\begin{lemma}[Covering above the two inherited special boxes]
\label{lem:newv41-special-cover}
The nine cubes
\[
 [jt,(j+1)t]\times[\ell t,(\ell+1)t]\times[2t,3t],
 \qquad j,\ell\in\{0,1,2\},                            \tag{41.7}
\]
are covered by the chart boxes when the lower \(112\) box has
radii \((\rho,\rho,r)\), the lower \(212\) box has radii
\((r,r,\rho)\), where \(\rho=99/800\), and every other lower or
upper box has radii \((r,r,r)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Given a point in one of (41.7), choose a coordinatewise nearest
vertex and write its three distances as
\(d_2,d_3,d_4\le t/2<r\).  An ordinary nearest box covers.

If the nearest vertex is \(112\), only coordinates \(2\) or \(3\)
can violate its radii.  When \(d_3>\rho\), flip coordinate \(3\)
to the ordinary vertex \(122\).  The flipped distance is
\(t-d_3<t-\rho<r\), and the other two distances are below \(r\).
When \(d_2>\rho\), flip coordinate \(2\) to \(212\).  Its first
two radii cover because \(t-d_2<r\) and \(d_3<r\).  If also
\(d_4>\rho\), flip coordinate \(4\) as well, reaching the ordinary
upper vertex \(213\); here \(t-d_4<t-\rho<r\).  Thus this case is
covered even when both shortened radii of \(112\) fail.

If the nearest vertex is \(212\), only coordinate \(4\) can fail.
Flipping that coordinate reaches the ordinary upper vertex \(213\)
and gives \(t-d_4<t-\rho<r\).  Since
\[
 t<\frac{61123}{245760}<r+\rho,                        \tag{41.8}
\]
all asserted strict inequalities are exact.  These cases exhaust
the nonordinary nearest vertices.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact positivity on the selected quarter cell]
\label{thm:newv41-selected-quarter-cell}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal C}_{234}^{+}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{41}I_{45},                             \tag{41.9}
\]
where
\[
 \theta_{41}:=
 \frac{38967606503323447966954527325371479}
 {54398423320315073925120000000000000000000}>0.        \tag{41.10}
\]
On the inverted cell
\[
 -{\cal C}_{234}^{+}
 =\{b_\circ+(s_2,-s_3,-s_4):
       0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le\pi/4\}\quad(\bmod\,2\pi),   \tag{41.11}
\]
the minus pencil has the same floor.  The original pencil has
floor \(\theta_{41}+49/1875\) on the corresponding cells.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv40-widened-prism} covers
\(0\le s_4\le2t\).  Lemma~\ref{lem:newv41-special-cover} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv41-sixteen-charts} cover the rational core
\[
 0\le s_2,s_3\le3t,\qquad 2t\le s_4\le3t.              \tag{41.12}
\]
For a first-coordinate cap \(3t\le s_2\le\pi/4\), choose
\(n_2=3\) and choose \(n_3,n_4\) from the nearest rational-grid
vertices of the relevant slab.  All selected boxes on that cap
are ordinary.  The cap distance obeys the exact estimate
\[
 0\le s_2-3t\le\frac{\pi}{4}-3t
 <\frac{71}{1792}<r.                                   \tag{41.13}
\]
The identical construction with \(n_3=3\) covers the
second-coordinate cap, including the cap corner.

It remains to cover \(3t\le s_4\le\pi/4\).  Set \(n_4=3\) and
choose the nearest \(n_2,n_3\in\{0,1,2,3\}\), using index \(3\)
on either cap.  Every resulting third-power box is ordinary;
nearest-grid distances are below \(r\), and each cap distance is
controlled by (41.13).  This covers the whole selected cell.

The minimum of the new face, \(\eta_{113}\), is larger than the
inherited V40 floor \(\theta_{40}\); exact
cross-multiplication therefore gives (41.10).
Finally \(b_\circ=-b_\circ\) on the transverse torus, so (41.11)
and Lemma~\ref{lem:newv4-inversion} prove the minus statement.
\end{proof}

\end{center}

\begin{assessment}[What V41 closes and what it does not]
\label{ass:newv41}
V41 completes the plus-pencil atlas on precisely the selected
quarter cell (41.6), and inversion completes the minus pencil on
the paired cell (41.11).  This is a finite-dimensional,
pointwise-in-momentum theorem with an explicit rational floor.

It is not a full transverse-torus theorem.  V4 supplies exact
certificates at every quarter-turn anchor, but no theorem presently
transports the entire V41 cell atlas between those anchors or
between all sign octants.  In particular, V35--V36 already showed
that the tempting coordinate-permutation and re-tree congruences
are not exact for the direct pencil.  Treating them as a global
transport would therefore be circular.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V42 attacks the missing orientation upstream]
\label{dec:newv41-next}
First test, by exact coefficient comparison, whether any admissible
orientation or reflection intertwiner transports the plus pencil
at \(b_\circ\) from (41.6) to
\[
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,-s_3,-s_4):
       0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le\pi/4\}.                    \tag{41.17}
\]
If no exact congruence exists, do not weaken the theorem: construct
independent splittings for the seven new vertices of the first
oppositely oriented \(t\)-cube, prove its product-hull cover, and
then proceed layerwise.  No quarter-grid translation is to be
assumed without a separately verified pencil identity.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V41]
\label{fire:newv41-final}
V41 proves exact Wilson-symbol positivity on one selected
plus-oriented transverse quarter cell, with the paired
minus-oriented statement obtained only by momentum inversion.  It
does not prove all-octant or full-torus coverage.  No global
covariant inequality, cofinal response sign, compact graph-screen,
interacting continuum measure, reflection-positive limit,
exponential clustering, or Hamiltonian Yang--Mills mass gap has
been proved.  The full Yang--Mills mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V42: the first oppositely oriented cube}
\label{sec:newv42-opposite-cube}

V41 isolated the missing sign-orientation problem instead of
mistaking momentum inversion for a plus-to-plus transport.  We now
test the elementary transports exactly and, because none is an
identity, construct the first opposite cube from independent
splittings.

\subsection{Exact boundary of the transport test}

Put
\[
 {\cal K}_t^-:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,-s_3,-s_4):0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le t\}.    \tag{42.1}
\]
Its all-shifted vertex is the inverse of the V30 centre:
\[
 b_-:=b_\circ+(t,-t,-t),\qquad
 b_+:=b_\circ+(-t,t,t).                                \tag{42.2}
\]

\begin{proposition}[No elementary dihedral orientation identity]
\label{prop:newv42-transport-screen}
At the pair \(b_+,b_-\), none of the eight active-phase maps
\[
 z\longmapsto qz,\qquad z\longmapsto q\bar z,
 \qquad q\in\{1,i,-1,-i\},                             \tag{42.3}
\]
turns the plus pencil at \(b_+\) into either the raw or entrywise
complex-conjugate plus pencil at \(b_-\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Evaluate the two exact Gaussian-rational \(45\times45\) pencils at
\(z\in\{1,i,-1,-i\}\).  For every map in (42.3), subtract both the
raw target and the entrywise-conjugate target.  Every one of the
sixteen resulting identity tests has a nonzero exact residual.  For
the phase-preserving map at \(z=1\), representative zero-based
entries are
\[
 (P_+(b_+)-P_+(b_-))_{0,8}=-\frac{128}{585}i,\qquad
 (\overline{P_+(b_+)}-P_+(b_-))_{0,21}=\frac13 i.       \tag{42.4}
\]
The exact ledgers for the other seven maps are bound into the V42
payload.  An identity on the active circle would hold at these four
sample phases, so each recorded residual is a valid obstruction.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What the screen does not exclude]
The first three power traces for the phase-preserving raw pair
agree at the four sampled phases.  Therefore Proposition
\ref{prop:newv42-transport-screen} is deliberately not stated as
an obstruction to an unknown unitary similarity or arbitrary
nonunitary congruence.  No such transform has been constructed, and
none is used below.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Failure and repair of the opposite corner}

For \(\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}^3\), define
\[
 a_\varepsilon^-=
 b_\circ+(\varepsilon_2t,-\varepsilon_3t,
                    -\varepsilon_4t).                 \tag{42.5}
\]

\begin{countertheorem}[The uniform full-radius certificate fails]
\label{cth:newv42-isotropic-failure}
At \(a_{111}^-=b_-\), the radius-\(1/8\) V28 product polygon with
the independently rounded centre splitting is not a positive
block certificate.  At the product vertex
\[
 (127/128,-511/4096,\,
   127/128,511/4096,\,
   127/128,511/4096),                                  \tag{42.6}
\]
there is an integer vector \(v\) for which
\[
 v^*{\cal Q}_{111}v
 =-\frac{65145192372590211103605557}
 {1198080000000000}<0.                                 \tag{42.7}
\]
The SHA--256 of \(v\) is
\[
\texttt{87B7826567FED3DE1FB42729BC61B7542B36D53017E2906C3E2C567E77DCC50C}.
\]
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
Direct exact substitution of (42.6) and the recorded integer vector
into the rational \(90\times90\) certificate block reduces to
(42.7).  This refutes only that fixed-splitting, full-radius
certificate; it is not a negative eigenvalue of the physical
pencil and is not used to infer one.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Seven independent opposite-vertex charts]
\label{thm:newv42-seven-charts}
At the six nonzero vertices other than \(111\), use radius \(r=1/8\)
in every local variable.  At \(111\), use
\(\rho=99/800\) in every local variable.  Then for every active
momentum and every transverse momentum in the corresponding chart,
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\eta_\varepsilon^-I_{45},                      \tag{42.8}
\]
where
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
\varepsilon&\eta_\varepsilon^-\\ \hline
001&\dfrac{5213205895852976393050588437667}
{59904000310901760000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
010&\dfrac{8585055290038565158638755139467}
{119808000620964864000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
011&\dfrac{164167063005827451888134092673}
{3328000017209088000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
100&\dfrac{90279037682348802683684147471}
{2304000011990016000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
101&\dfrac{8608088172868257562554517617889}
{519168002704346112000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
110&\dfrac{998601710643906307488183553}
{93600000483069600000000000000000}\\[5pt]
111&\dfrac{29262972378521451980314767820515450889}
{2338689611962397304000000000000000000000000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{42.9}
\]
The exact minimum is \(\eta_{110}^->0\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At each centre (42.5), regroup the direct plus-pencil coefficient
bank and construct a separate rational Hermitian splitting.  The
six ordinary charts use the six-vertex V28 polygon and the repaired
corner uses the eight-vertex V30 polygon.  Exact rounded-basis
congruence and Gram-residual checks are positive at
\[
 6\cdot6^3+8^3=1808                                   \tag{42.10}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity fills the seven
product boxes.  Twice each exact block floor gives (42.9), and exact
cross-multiplication selects \(110\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Two signs on the paired first cubes]
\label{thm:newv42-paired-cubes}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal K}_t^-\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq
 \theta_{42}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{42}:=
 \frac{998601710643906307488183553}
 {93600000483069600000000000000000}>0.                 \tag{42.11}
\]
Together with Theorem~\ref{thm:newv34-cube-cover} and momentum
inversion, both signs are positive on both paired cubes
\({\cal K}_t\) and \({\cal K}_t^-\), with common floor
\[
 \min\{\eta_{\rm cube},\theta_{42}\}
 =\eta_{\rm cube}
 =\frac{3323190119284031031100923176317}
 {311500801619804160000000000000000000}.               \tag{42.12}
\]
The original pencil gains \(49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The V28 chart covers \(a_{000}^-=b_\circ\).  Choose a nearest
Boolean vertex for a point of \([0,t]^3\).  Its coordinate
distances are at most \(t/2<r\).  An ordinary selected vertex
covers.  If the selected vertex is \(111\) and all distances are at
most \(\rho\), the repaired chart covers.  Otherwise flip one
coordinate whose distance exceeds \(\rho\).  The new vertex is
ordinary, its flipped distance is
\[
 t-d<t-\rho<r
\]
because \(t<r+\rho=199/800\), and its other distances remain below
\(r\).  Hence the eight charts cover \({\cal K}_t^-\).
The V28 anchor floor is larger than \(\eta_{110}^-\), so (42.11)
follows.

V34 gives the plus statement on \({\cal K}_t\), while inversion
gives its minus statement on \({\cal K}_t^-\).  Inverting (42.11)
gives the missing minus statement on \({\cal K}_t\).  Exact
comparison shows \(\eta_{\rm cube}<\theta_{42}\), proving (42.12).
The Hessian reserve gives the final assertion.
\end{proof}

\label{rec:newv42-provenance}
The diagnostic source and scan-ledger SHA--256 values are

\texttt{804BDC9E98FE0D6B0063A1ABEB41711381A63207AF43B335B13DA70454DD8AD2}.
\end{gathered}                                         \tag{42.13}
\]
The exact verifier is
\begin{center}

\path{scripts/verify_ym_new_v42_opposite_first_cube.py}.
\end{center}
Its source and canonical-payload SHA--256 values are
\[
\begin{gathered}
\texttt{3A84FA003DDE8CBF18DED7B9561AF5999A8DEC0F00B3651B49E741DAD1BB5E23},\\
\texttt{213799F2ED871633FDBB3598AF9BE26AAFABAA5FB59AE0789BB616EFF0CF672F}.
\end{gathered}                                         \tag{42.14}
\]
The seven-chart and elementary-transport ledgers are
\[

\texttt{88C648B0502664C7E6CE1908BD0AD6B4625E97A471CBDE682AD92CB277A294D0},\\
\texttt{C7A7D6FCFDE4DEC11447741BC59413A6B19EEE5E3EEFD472CC69BFBE4F36D38E}.

\begin{assessment}[Independent construction was necessary]
\label{ass:newv42}
The elementary transport test failed, and the superficially
uniform replacement failed at one exact product vertex.  Going
upstream to a smaller rational arc hull repaired precisely that
corner without shrinking the covered physical cube.  The result is
the first open region on which both direct response signs are
certified at the same transverse momenta.

This remains a two-cube result.  It does not supply the unproved
transport needed to reflect the whole V41 quarter-cell atlas.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V43 begins the opposite second layer]
\label{dec:newv42-next}
Construct independently the four opposite-orientation centres
\[
 b_\circ+(2t,-n_3t,-n_4t),\qquad n_3,n_4\in\{0,1\}.    \tag{42.16}
\]
Screen the full radius-\(1/8\) product hull, preserve any exact
negative witness, and repair only the failed local axes.  Then use
nearest-vertex overlap to attempt a pointwise cover of
\([0,2t]\times[0,t]^2\) in the opposite displacement coordinates.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V42]
\label{fire:newv42-final}
V42 proves both direct signs on the union of two paired first
rational cubes.  It does not prove either sign on all transverse
orientations or on the full torus.  No global covariant inequality,
cofinal response sign, compact graph-screen, interacting continuum
measure, reflection-positive limit, exponential clustering, or
Hamiltonian Yang--Mills mass gap has been proved.  The full
Yang--Mills mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V43: the opposite two-step slab}
\label{sec:newv43-opposite-second-layer}

V42 established the first opposite cube without a symmetry
shortcut.  We now extend its first displacement coordinate by one
rational step and treat the inherited shortened corner explicitly.

\subsection{Four independent second-layer charts}

Let
\[
 {\cal N}_{2}^{-}:=\{(2,n_3,n_4):n_3,n_4\in\{0,1\}\},
\qquad
 a_n^-=b_\circ+(n_2t,-n_3t,-n_4t).                   \tag{43.1}
\]
The common first transverse phase is
\[
 -\left(\frac{63+16i}{65}\right)^2
 =-\frac{3713}{4225}-i\frac{2016}{4225}.              \tag{43.2}
\]

\begin{record}[The V43 screen is discovery only]
\label{rec:newv43-screen}
The diagnostic script
\begin{center}
\footnotesize
\path{scripts/diagnose_ym_new_v43_opposite_second_layer.py}
\end{center}
screened the \(216\) full-radius product vertices at each of the
four centres.  All numerical minima were positive, so it emitted no
exact negative witness.  This absence is not used as a proof; the
next theorem rechecks every vertex by exact rational arithmetic.
\end{record}

\begin{theorem}[Four exact opposite second-layer charts]
\label{thm:newv43-four-charts}
For each \(n\in{\cal N}_2^-\), every active momentum, and every
transverse momentum in the radius-\(1/8\) product box about \(a_n^-\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq\zeta_n I_{45},      \tag{43.3}
\]
where
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
n&\zeta_n\\ \hline
200&\dfrac{25802939808656007980597949804277}
{519168002693962752000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
201&\dfrac{13703395147175805160268233623947}
{519168002673196032000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
210&\dfrac{63896032235755880473850761675063}
{2530944013122944640000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
211&\dfrac{10712843411454189248552792157011}
{4049510420940018278400000000000000000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{43.4}
\]
The exact layer minimum is \(\zeta_{211}>0\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Regroup the direct plus-pencil bank independently at the four exact
phase triples (43.1), construct a separate rational Hermitian
splitting at each centre, and use the six-vertex V28 polygon in all
three local variables.  Exact rounded-basis congruence and
Gram-residual checks are positive at
\[
 4\cdot6^3=864                                         \tag{43.5}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity fills the four boxes.
Twice the exact block floors gives (43.4), and exact
cross-multiplication selects \(211\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{A repaired cover of the new cube}

Define
\[
 {\cal S}_{2,1,1}^{-}:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,-s_3,-s_4):
       0\le s_2\le2t,\ 0\le s_3,s_4\le t\}.            \tag{43.6}
\]

\begin{lemma}[The inherited small \(111\) box causes no hole]
\label{lem:newv43-special-cover}
The eight endpoint boxes cover
\([t,2t]\times[0,t]\times[0,t]\) when the lower \(111\) box has
radii \((\rho,\rho,\rho)\), \(\rho=99/800\), and every other lower
or upper box has radii \((r,r,r)\), \(r=1/8\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Choose a coordinatewise nearest endpoint.  All three distances are
at most \(t/2<r\), so an ordinary vertex covers.  If the nearest
vertex is \(111\), it covers when all distances are at most
\(\rho\).  Otherwise flip any coordinate with distance
\(d>\rho\).  The adjacent vertex is ordinary, its flipped distance
satisfies
\[
 t-d<t-\rho<r
\]
by \(t<r+\rho=199/800\), and the other two distances remain below
\(r\).  Hence no point is lost at the shortened corner.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Both signs on the paired two-step prisms]
\label{thm:newv43-paired-prisms}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal S}_{2,1,1}^{-}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{43}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{43}:=
 \frac{10712843411454189248552792157011}
 {4049510420940018278400000000000000000}>0.            \tag{43.7}
\]
Together with Theorem~\ref{thm:newv36-slab-cover} and momentum
inversion, both signs are positive on both paired two-step prisms.
Their sharp common recorded floor is
\[
 \gamma_{2,1,1}
 =\frac{53543524052354523326188125464407}
 {20247552105550488576000000000000000000},             \tag{43.8}
\]
and the original pencil gains \(49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
V42 covers the part of (43.6) with \(0\le s_2\le t\).
Lemma~\ref{lem:newv43-special-cover} covers the second cube using
the V42 lower boxes and the four exact upper boxes of Theorem
\ref{thm:newv43-four-charts}.  Exact comparison shows that the new
layer minimum \(\zeta_{211}\) is smaller than the V42 cube floor,
giving (43.7).

V36 supplies the plus statement on the oppositely signed prism and
the minus statement here.  Inverting (43.7) supplies the missing
minus statement there.  Exact comparison gives
\(\gamma_{2,1,1}<\theta_{43}\), proving the common floor (43.8).
The global Hessian reserve proves the original-pencil assertion.
\end{proof}

\section{V44: the opposite first quarter-face plate}
\label{sec:newv44-opposite-first-face}

V43 reached the second rational layer in the first opposite
displacement coordinate.  The third layer is now certified
independently and its radius-\(1/8\) boxes cover the remaining
quarter-face cap.

\subsection{Four exact terminal-layer charts}

Let
\[
 {\cal N}_{3}^{-}:=\{(3,n_3,n_4):n_3,n_4\in\{0,1\}\},
\qquad
 a_n^-=b_\circ+(n_2t,-n_3t,-n_4t).                   \tag{44.1}
\]
Their common first transverse phase is
\[
 -\left(\frac{63+16i}{65}\right)^3
 =-\frac{201663}{274625}-i\frac{186416}{274625}.       \tag{44.2}
\]

\begin{record}[The V44 screen is not a certificate]
\label{rec:newv44-screen}
The numerical diagnostic screened all \(4\cdot216\) full-radius
product vertices and found no negative candidate.  It was used only
to choose the exact run.  Every positive statement below comes from
the independent rational verifier.
\end{record}

\begin{theorem}[Four exact opposite terminal-layer charts]
\label{thm:newv44-four-charts}
For each \(n\in{\cal N}_3^-\), every active momentum, and every
transverse momentum in the radius-\(1/8\) product box about \(a_n^-\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq\xi_n I_{45},        \tag{44.3}
\]

\label{cth:newv47-isotropic-failure}
For the centre splittings constructed directly at opposite centres
\(112\) and \(212\), the radius-\(r\) V28 product polygon in all
three local variables is not positive semidefinite.  At the common
rational vertex
\[
 (127/128,-511/4096,\,
  127/128,511/4096,\,
  127/128,511/4096),                                   \tag{47.2}
\]
exact integer-vector evaluation gives, respectively,
\[
 -\frac{9827594581909209530873378117}
        {202475520000000000}<0,\qquad
 -\frac{7231041849217726194096207691}
        {5264363520000000000}<0.                       \tag{47.3}
\]
The integer-witness SHA--256 identifiers are
\[
\begin{gathered}
\texttt{15880D847C05B4E155220DC09CE50443C618DF855695E567E0AFBA8958EC0268},\\
\texttt{F669C26E5F164ABA8E8DE52261CA1C46D623BF28731B751865B79ADDD96D7A3F}.
\end{gathered}                                         \tag{47.4}
\]
Thus a uniform full-radius theorem for the opposite \(n_4=2\)
layer would be false.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
The diagnostic independently regroups the opposite direct
plus-pencil, constructs its rational Hermitian centre splitting,
reconstructs the two displayed vertex blocks, and evaluates each
displayed integer vector using exact fractions.  A negative
quadratic form excludes positive semidefiniteness.  Floating
arithmetic only proposed the integer vectors; it proves no sign in
(47.3).
\end{proof}

Replace only the false radii:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
\text{centre}&(r_2,r_3,r_4)\\ \hline
112&(\rho,\rho,r)\\
212&(r,r,\rho)\\
\text{other fourteen opposite \(n_4=2\) centres}&(r,r,r).
\end{array}                                             \tag{47.5}
\]
The exact overlap margin inherited as a scalar estimate is
\[
 t<\frac{61123}{245760}
   <\frac{199}{800}=r+\rho.                            \tag{47.6}
\]
No matrix chart is inherited in (47.5).

\begin{proof}
At each of the sixteen exact phase triples (47.7), regroup the
direct opposite plus-pencil bank and construct a separate rational
Hermitian splitting.  Fourteen charts use the six-vertex V28
radius-\(r\) polygon on all three axes.  At \(112\), use the V30
eight-vertex radius-\(\rho\) polygon on axes two and three and the
six-vertex radius-\(r\) polygon on axis four.  At \(212\), use the
radius-\(r\) polygon on axes two and three and the
radius-\(\rho\) polygon on axis four.

Exact rounded-basis congruence and exact Gram-residual checks are
positive at
\[
 14\cdot6^3+(8\cdot8\cdot6)+(6\cdot6\cdot8)
 =3696                                                 \tag{47.11}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity over the three
polygon factors fills every product box.  Twice the minimum exact
block floor gives (47.10), and exact cross-multiplication selects
\(\mu_{212}\).  This construction uses no orientation transport.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Pointwise completion of the opposite two-step prism}

Define
\[
 {\cal P}_{234}^{-,(2)}:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,-s_3,-s_4):
 0\le s_2,s_3\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_4\le2t\}.              \tag{47.12}
\]

\begin{lemma}[The opposite three-special-vertex repair]
\label{lem:newv47-special-cover}
The nine cubes
\[
 [jt,(j+1)t]\times[\ell t,(\ell+1)t]\times[t,2t],
 \qquad j,\ell\in\{0,1,2\},                            \tag{47.13}
\]
are covered by their opposite chart boxes when the lower \(111\)
box has radii \((\rho,\rho,\rho)\), the upper boxes \(112\) and
\(212\) have the radii in (47.5), and every other box has radii
\((r,r,r)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For a point of a cube choose a coordinatewise nearest vertex and
write its three distances as \(d_2,d_3,d_4\le t/2<r\).  An
ordinary nearest vertex covers.

If the nearest vertex is \(111\), it covers unless some
\(d_q>\rho\).  For \(q=2\) or \(3\), flip that coordinate to an
ordinary lower vertex.  Its new distance is
\(t-d_q<t-\rho<r\).  If only \(d_4>\rho\), the \(112\) box covers:
\(d_2,d_3\le\rho\) and its fourth distance is
\(t-d_4<t-\rho<r\).

If the nearest vertex is \(112\), only coordinate two or three can
fail.  Flipping coordinate three reaches an ordinary upper vertex.
Flipping coordinate two reaches an ordinary upper vertex except
at \(212\).  There \(212\) covers when \(d_4\le\rho\); if
\(d_4>\rho\), flip coordinate four as well to the ordinary lower
vertex \(211\).  Every flipped distance is below
\(t-\rho<r\).

If the nearest vertex is \(212\), only coordinate four can fail;
flipping it reaches the ordinary lower vertex \(211\), again with
distance below \(t-\rho<r\).  These are all shortened vertices, so
the nine cubes are covered.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Both direct signs on the paired two-step prisms]
\label{thm:newv47-paired-two-step-prisms}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal P}_{234}^{-,(2)}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{47}^{-}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{47}^{-}:=
 \frac{2339583897708929420698123184858707}
 {3263905399346196745420800000000000000000}>0.         \tag{47.14}
\]
Together with Theorem~\ref{thm:newv40-widened-prism} and momentum
inversion, both direct signs are positive on
\({\cal P}_{234}^{(2)}\) and \({\cal P}_{234}^{-,(2)}\), with
common floor
\[
 \theta_{\rm pair}^{(2)}:=
 \frac{38967606503323447966954527325371479}
 {54398423320315073925120000000000000000000}>0.        \tag{47.15}
\]
The corresponding original pencils gain \(49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
V46 covers \(0\le s_4\le t\).  Lemma
\ref{lem:newv47-special-cover} covers
\[
 0\le s_2,s_3\le3t,\qquad t\le s_4\le2t.              \tag{47.16}
\]
For \(3t\le s_2\le\pi/4\), set \(n_2=3\) and choose the
coordinatewise nearest \(n_3\in\{0,1,2,3\}\) and
\(n_4\in\{1,2\}\).  Every selected chart is ordinary; its latter
two distances are at most \(t/2<r\), while
\[
 0\le s_2-3t\le\frac{\pi}{4}-3t
 <\frac{71}{1792}<r.                                  \tag{47.17}
\]
The second-coordinate cap is identical: set \(n_3=3\), use the
nearest remaining grid indices, and use \(n_2=3\) at the cap
corner.  This proves the cover of (47.12).  The new layer floor is
smaller than the V46 plate floor, so their exact minimum is
(47.14).

Theorem~\ref{thm:newv40-widened-prism} gives the direct plus
statement on \({\cal P}_{234}^{(2)}\) and, after inversion, the
direct minus statement on \({\cal P}_{234}^{-,(2)}\).  Equation
(47.14) and inversion supply the two missing signs.  Exact
cross-multiplication gives
\(\theta_{\rm pair}^{(2)}<\theta_{47}^{-}\), proving (47.15).
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[Paired depth \(2t\), not paired quarter cells]
\label{ass:newv47}
The independently constructed opposite atlas now reaches the same
third-coordinate depth as the V40 atlas.  Thus both direct response
signs hold on the paired regions
\[
 [0,\pi/4]^2\times[0,2t]

in their respective displacement coordinates.  The remaining
third-coordinate slab \(2t\le s_4\le\pi/4\), including its rational

\begin{decision}[V48 attempts the opposite quarter cell]
\label{dec:newv47-next}
Construct independently the sixteen opposite \(n_4=3\) charts
\[
 b_\circ+(n_2t,-n_3t,-3t),
 \qquad n_2,n_3\in\{0,1,2,3\}.                         \tag{47.21}
\]
Screen the full-radius hull first, retain every exact negative
witness, and repair only the axes required by those witnesses.
Then attempt the third-coordinate rational slab and
\(\pi/4-3t\) cap.  If successful, combine it with the already
certified original quarter cell only through proved momentum
inversion, not an assumed orientation symmetry.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V47]
\label{fire:newv47-final}
V47 rejects two false isotropic opposite charts, replaces them by
exact anisotropic charts, and proves both direct response signs on
paired two-step third-coordinate prisms.  It does not prove paired
transverse quarter cells or the full torus.  No global covariant
inequality, physical heat-transfer limit, cofinal response sign,
compact graph-screen, interacting continuum measure,
reflection-positive limit, exponential clustering, or Hamiltonian
Yang--Mills mass gap has been proved.  The full Yang--Mills
mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V48: closure of the opposite transverse quarter cell}
\label{sec:newv48-opposite-quarter-cell}

V47 reached third-coordinate depth \(2t\) in the opposite
displacement orientation.  The remaining task for that cell is to
construct the \(n_4=3\) face, cover the slab above \(2t\), and fill
the terminal cap.  Every new splitting below is built from the
direct opposite pencil.

\subsection{Screen and exact power-three face}

Retain \(r=1/8\), \(\rho=99/800\), and set
\[
 {\cal N}_{4,3}^{-}:=
 \{(n_2,n_3,3):n_2,n_3\in\{0,1,2,3\}\},\qquad
 a_n^-=b_\circ+(n_2t,-n_3t,-n_4t).                   \tag{48.1}
\]
The common third transverse phase is
\[
 \left(\frac{63-16i}{65}\right)^3
 =\frac{201663}{274625}
  -i\frac{186416}{274625}.                            \tag{48.2}
\]

\begin{proof}
At all sixteen exact opposite phase triples, independently regroup
the direct plus-pencil bank and construct its rational Hermitian
centre splitting.  Use the V28 six-vertex tangent polygon of
radius \(r\) in every local variable.  Exact rounded-basis
congruence and exact Gram-residual estimates are positive at all
\[
 16\cdot6^3=3456                                       \tag{48.5}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity fills the product
boxes.  Twice each least exact block floor gives (48.4), and exact

\subsection{The last slab and paired two-sign cells}

Define the paired cells
\[
\begin{aligned}
 {\cal C}_{234}^{+}
 &:=\{b_\circ+(-s_2,s_3,s_4):
             0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le\pi/4\},\\
 {\cal C}_{234}^{-}
 &:=\{b_\circ+(s_2,-s_3,-s_4):
             0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le\pi/4\}.
\end{aligned}                                          \tag{48.6}
\]

\begin{lemma}[Cover above the two inherited shortened boxes]
\label{lem:newv48-special-cover}
The nine cubes
\[
 [jt,(j+1)t]\times[\ell t,(\ell+1)t]\times[2t,3t],
 \qquad j,\ell\in\{0,1,2\},                            \tag{48.7}
\]
are covered by the opposite chart boxes when the lower \(112\)
box has radii \((\rho,\rho,r)\), the lower \(212\) box has radii
\((r,r,\rho)\), and every other lower or upper box has radii
\((r,r,r)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Choose a coordinatewise nearest vertex and write the local
distances as \(d_2,d_3,d_4\le t/2<r\).  An ordinary nearest box
covers.  At lower vertex \(112\), only coordinate two or three can
fail.  If \(d_3>\rho\), flip coordinate three to the ordinary lower
vertex \(122\).  If \(d_2>\rho\), flip coordinate two to \(212\).
The new distance is \(t-d_2<t-\rho<r\).  This box covers unless
also \(d_4>\rho\); in that case flip coordinate four too, reaching
the ordinary upper vertex \(213\), with
\(t-d_4<t-\rho<r\).

At lower vertex \(212\), only coordinate four can fail.  Flipping
it reaches the ordinary upper vertex \(213\) with distance below
\(t-\rho<r\).  These strict inequalities follow from
\[
 t<\frac{61123}{245760}<r+\rho,                        \tag{48.8}
\]
and the listed cases exhaust all shortened boxes.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Both direct signs on the paired quarter cells]
\label{thm:newv48-paired-quarter-cells}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal C}_{234}^{-}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{48}^{-}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{48}^{-}:=
 \frac{2339583897708929420698123184858707}
 {3263905399346196745420800000000000000000}>0.         \tag{48.9}
\]
Together with Theorem~\ref{thm:newv41-selected-quarter-cell} and
momentum inversion, both direct response signs are positive on
both cells in (48.6), with common floor
\[
 \theta_{\rm cells}:=
 \frac{38967606503323447966954527325371479}
 {54398423320315073925120000000000000000000}>0.        \tag{48.10}
\]
The corresponding original pencils gain \(49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv47-paired-two-step-prisms} covers the
opposite cell through \(s_4=2t\).  Lemma
\ref{lem:newv48-special-cover} and Theorem
\ref{thm:newv48-sixteen-charts} cover
\[
 0\le s_2,s_3\le3t,\qquad2t\le s_4\le3t.              \tag{48.11}
\]
For \(3t\le s_2\le\pi/4\), set \(n_2=3\) and choose the
nearest \(n_3,n_4\) on the relevant rational grid.  All selected
cap boxes are ordinary, the two grid distances are at most
\(t/2<r\), and
\[
 0\le s_2-3t\le\frac{\pi}{4}-3t
 <\frac{71}{1792}<r.                                  \tag{48.12}
\]
Setting \(n_3=3\) gives the identical second-coordinate cap,
including their corner.

For \(3t\le s_4\le\pi/4\), set \(n_4=3\), choose the nearest
\(n_2,n_3\), and use index \(3\) on either of their caps.
Equation (48.12) controls every terminal distance.  Hence the whole
opposite cell is covered.  Exact comparison shows that the new
face minimum \(\nu_{113}\) exceeds the inherited V47 prism floor,
which proves (48.9).

V41 gives direct plus on \({\cal C}_{234}^{+}\) and direct minus
on \({\cal C}_{234}^{-}\).  Equation (48.9) gives direct plus on
\({\cal C}_{234}^{-}\), and inversion gives direct minus on
\({\cal C}_{234}^{+}\).  Finally, exact cross-multiplication shows
\(\theta_{\rm cells}<\theta_{48}^{-}\), proving (48.10).
\end{proof}

transverse octants or the full torus.  No global covariant
inequality, physical heat-transfer limit, cofinal response sign,
compact graph-screen, interacting continuum measure,
reflection-positive limit, exponential clustering, or Hamiltonian
Yang--Mills mass gap has been proved.  The full Yang--Mills
mass-gap goal remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V49: a nonduplicative mixed-sign seed cube}
\label{sec:newv49-mixed-seed-cube}

V48 closed one inversion pair of transverse quarter cells.  The
next orientation changes only the sign of the second displacement
coordinate relative to \({\cal C}_{234}^{+}\).  Before computing
new charts, the exact coverage ledger is de-duplicated.

\subsection{Ledger correction and a scoped transport obstruction}

Define
\[
 {\cal K}_{--+}:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,-s_3,s_4):
             0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le t\}.                 \tag{49.1}
\]

\begin{record}[Only three vertices are new]
\label{rec:newv49-dedup}
The four \(n_3=1\) triples listed in the V48 decision are
\[
 010,\quad110,\quad011,\quad111.                       \tag{49.2}
\]
The \(010\) chart is already the full-radius opposite chart
certified in Theorem~\ref{thm:newv42-seven-charts}.  The face
\(n_3=0\) is certified by the V34 atlas.  Consequently the only
new vertices of (49.1) are \(110,011,111\).  The V49 calculation
does not recompute \(010\).
\end{record}

\begin{proposition}[No tested elementary single-axis transport]
\label{prop:newv49-transport}
Compare the direct plus pencils at the all-shifted source and
target displacements
\[
 (-t,t,t)\quad\hbox{and}\quad(-t,-t,t).                \tag{49.3}
\]
None of the eight active-phase maps
\[
 z\longmapsto qz,\qquad z\longmapsto q\bar z,
 \qquad q\in\{1,i,-1,-i\},                             \tag{49.4}
\]
turns the source pencil into either the raw target pencil or its
entrywise complex conjugate.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Evaluate both exact Gaussian-rational \(45\times45\) pencils at
\(z\in\{1,i,-1,-i\}\).  For every map in (49.4), subtract the raw
target and the entrywise-conjugate target.  Every candidate has a
nonzero exact residual.  For the phase-preserving map at \(z=1\),
representative zero-based residual entries are
\[
 (P_{\rm src}-P_{\rm tar})_{1,5}
 =\frac{2048}{38025}-i\frac{896}{4225},\qquad
 (\overline{P_{\rm src}}-P_{\rm tar})_{0,8}
 =i\frac{128}{585}.                                    \tag{49.5}
\]
The complete residual ledger covers all eight maps and four active
roots.  This proves only the asserted failure of raw identity and
entrywise-conjugation transports.  It does not exclude an unknown
general similarity, unitary equivalence, or nonunitary congruence.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Three independent charts and the cube cover}

For \((n_2,n_3,n_4)\in\{110,011,111\}\), put
\[
 a_n^{--+}:=b_\circ+(-n_2t,-n_3t,n_4t).               \tag{49.6}
\]

\begin{record}[The three-chart screen is not a proof]
\label{rec:newv49-screen}

vertices.  It found positive numerical minima at all three centres
and emitted no exact negative witness.  Positivity below comes
only from the separate exact verifier.
\end{record}

\label{thm:newv49-three-charts}
For every \(n\in\{110,011,111\}\), every active momentum, and every
transverse momentum in the radius-\(1/8\) product box about
\(a_n^{--+}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq\xi_n I_{45},       \tag{49.7}
\]
where
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
n&\xi_n\\ \hline
011&\dfrac{2160967549340856416590229267557}
{31150080161855815680000000000000000}\\[5pt]
110&\dfrac{825390615147965040459205416282301}
{10243584053676380160000000000000000000}\\[5pt]
111&\dfrac{23935945159934713924432226614406503}
{399499778086187830272000000000000000000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{49.8}
\]
The exact new-chart minimum is \(\xi_{111}>0\).  Momentum inversion
gives the same minus-pencil bounds on the inverted boxes.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At the three exact physical phase triples (49.6), independently
regroup the direct plus-pencil bank and construct three rational
Hermitian centre splittings.  Apply the V28 six-vertex tangent
polygon of radius \(1/8\) on each local axis.  Exact rounded-basis
congruence and Gram-residual checks are positive at all
\[
 3\cdot6^3=648                                         \tag{49.9}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity fills the boxes,
twice the least block floors gives (49.8), and exact
cross-multiplication selects \(111\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact cover of the mixed-sign seed cube]
\label{thm:newv49-mixed-cube}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal K}_{--+}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{49}I_{45},\qquad

 \frac{3323190119284031031100923176317}

\begin{proof}
The four vertices with \(n_3=0\) are full-radius charts from the
V34 atlas.  Vertex \(010\) is a full-radius V42 chart, and the
remaining three full-radius charts are supplied by Theorem
\ref{thm:newv49-three-charts}.  Thus all eight Boolean vertices of
(49.1) have radius \(r=1/8\).

Choose a coordinatewise nearest Boolean vertex.  Every coordinate
distance is at most
\[
 \frac t2<\frac{61123}{491520}<\frac18.                \tag{49.11}
\]
Its chart therefore contains the point.  The inherited V34 cube
floor is smaller than both the V42 opposite-cube floor and the
new minimum \(\xi_{111}\); exact fraction comparison gives
(49.10).  Momentum inversion and the Hessian reserve give the last
two assertions.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[V50 companion-audit decision]
\label{thm:newv50-audit-decision}
No companion theorem is imported into the finite Wilson chart
proof of this iteration.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
SM V35 proves state-norm telescoping for finitely many density
owners without an independence assumption.  It is a useful
discipline for a later continuum assembly: sector changes must be
charged owner by owner.  However, the physical density
factorization and five non-Newton debits in that note remain open,
and state-norm telescoping gives no local \(45\times45\) Wilson
Loewner inequality.

NS V26 computes rank-one norms for Oseen-kernel derivatives.  Its
input class and operator are different from the whole reduced
Yang--Mills fibre.  Using that estimate here would replace the
required full-space hypothesis by a rank-one restriction.  Hence
neither companion result supplies a premise for the charts below.
\end{proof}

 {1557504008178453504000000000000000000},
