\section{V54: exact atlas intersections and a third-axis bottleneck}
\label{sec:newv54-third-axis-bottleneck}

The next mixed layer contains sixteen proposed centres.  This
iteration first proves which of them are already present in a
certified atlas.  It then screens only the genuinely new centres
and follows every failed screen with an exact rational witness.
Because four failures survive the smallest simple radius repair
compatible with overlap, the programme moves upstream to the
centre splitting rather than claiming a slab.

\subsection{Exact signed-phase intersection ledger}

Let
\[
 u:=\frac{63+16i}{65},\qquad |u|=1,
\]
and use the common anchor \((-1,-1,1)\).  The three phase-power
orientations relevant here have step signs
\[
 \sigma_{\rm orig}=(-,+,+),\qquad
 \sigma_{\rm opp}=(+,-,-),\qquad
 \sigma_{\rm mix}=(-,-,+).                            \tag{54.1}
\]
The proposed mixed upper face is
\[
 {\cal N}_{4,2}^{--+}
 :=\{(n_2,n_3,2):0\le n_2,n_3\le3\}.                 \tag{54.2}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact intersection classification]
\label{thm:newv54-intersections}
Among the sixteen rational phase triples in (54.2), the exact
intersections with the established original, opposite, and
lower mixed atlases are precisely
\[
 002,\quad102,\quad202,\quad302.                      \tag{54.3}
\]
Each is the identically labelled original-atlas centre from V40.
The other twelve centres are genuinely new:
\[
\begin{gathered}
012,022,032,\quad112,122,132,\\
212,222,232,\quad312,322,332.                         \tag{54.4}
\end{gathered}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Generate every phase as an ordered triple of pairs in
\(\mathbb Q^2\), multiply pairs with exact rational arithmetic, and
compare componentwise.  The exact ledger compares (54.2) with all
\(4^3\) original powers, all \(4^3\) opposite powers, and all
\(4\cdot4\cdot2\) already constructed mixed powers.  It returns
only (54.3).

There is also a direct finite explanation.  The original and mixed
signs agree on axes \(2\) and \(4\), while their axis-\(3\) powers
agree for the present range only when \(n_3=0\); this gives the
four rows in (54.3).  Equality with the opposite atlas would
require, on axis \(4\), \(u^2=u^{-2}\).  Equality with a lower mixed
layer would require \(u^2\in\{1,u\}\).  Exact rational evaluation
shows \(u^4\ne1\), \(u^2\ne1\), and \(u^2\ne u\).  Thus no other
row can occur.  This agrees with every componentwise ledger
comparison and proves (54.4).
\end{proof}

\subsection{The full-radius obstruction}

The twelve new centres were screened using the inherited
radius-\(1/8\) product hull and the centrewise rational Hermitian
splitting obtained from Riccati weight \(13/25\).  Eight rows,
\[
 012,022,032,112,122,132,212,312,                     \tag{54.5}
\]
had positive numerical minima.  That statement is discovery only
and is not an exact positivity claim.

\begin{theorem}[Four exact full-radius certificate obstructions]
\label{thm:newv54-full-obstructions}
For each \(n\in\{222,232,322,332\}\), the fixed-splitting
radius-\(1/8\) certificate block has a rational product vertex and
an integer vector \(v_n\) for which
\[
 v_n^*{\cal B}_n v_n=q_n<0,                           \tag{54.6}
\]
with
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
n&q_n\\ \hline
222&-\dfrac{4905498866267920814373349688137}
{225051540480000000000}\\[5pt]
232&-\dfrac{2326269872257182074287642894038439}
{35108040314880000000000}\\[5pt]
322&-\dfrac{67990377710563436750348307353}
{2851530240000000000}\\[5pt]
332&-\dfrac{20679916925262658541975879524157}
{325074447360000000000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{54.7}
\]
Consequently this fixed splitting cannot certify any of those four
full-radius product boxes.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At each displayed exact polygon vertex, form the \(90\times90\)
Hermitian certificate block from the regrouped rational
coefficient bank and the recorded rational splitting.  The
diagnostic first uses a floating eigenvector only to propose an
integer vector.  It then evaluates the quadratic form in exact
rational arithmetic.  The four values are (54.7), so each block is
not positive semidefinite.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[What the obstruction does not prove]
\label{fire:newv54-certificate-only}
The block \({\cal B}_n\) is a sufficient active-circle
factorization built from a selected splitting.  Its failure does
not prove that the underlying \(45\times45\) Wilson pencil is
negative at a physical momentum.  It proves only that this
splitting and this product hull do not establish the desired
Loewner bound.
\end{firewall}

\subsection{Failure of the overlap-compatible one-axis repair}

The V40 repair radius \(\rho=99/800\) is nearly the smallest
third-axis radius that can overlap a full-radius lower face:
\[
 t<\frac{61123}{245760}
   <\frac18+\frac{99}{800}.                            \tag{54.8}
\]
Keep radius \(1/8\) on the first two local axes and replace only
the third radius by \(\rho\).

\begin{theorem}[The simple anisotropic repair remains obstructed]
\label{thm:newv54-anisotropic-obstructions}
For the same four centres and fixed centre splittings, the
anisotropic certificate again has exact integer-vector witnesses
with quadratic values
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
n&q_n^{\rm aniso}\\ \hline
222&-\dfrac{31134504860683397105582110782185281}
{1674383461171200000000000}\\[5pt]
232&-\dfrac{2281614226260956392200398806199693099}
{36278308325376000000000000}\\[5pt]
322&-\dfrac{26766204939828673843958284281437}
{1325961561600000000000}\\[5pt]
332&-\dfrac{260934292331536719409213071119489969}
{4353396999045120000000000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{54.9}
\]
In particular, merely shortening the third local axis to
\(99/800\) does not repair the certificate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The anisotropic product hull has \(6\cdot6\cdot8=288\) rational
vertices per centre.  At the recorded vertex in each row, exact
evaluation of the integer-vector quadratic form gives (54.9).
Every value is strictly negative.
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[A useful obstruction, not a lost gap]
\label{ass:newv54}
V54 saves four exact V40 charts and prevents twelve redundant
computations.  More importantly, it localizes the first obstruction
in the mixed third-coordinate expansion.  The failure persists at
an overlap-compatible shortened third radius, so repeated shrinking
would be both ineffective and geometrically circular.  The next
move must alter the splitting or the subdivision.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V55 audits companions and repairs upstream]
\label{dec:newv54-next}
V55 is a five-version checkpoint.  First inspect the newest active
SM and NS notes and import no statement whose hypotheses do not
match the direct Wilson pencil.  Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item exactly certify the eight unobstructed full-radius candidates
in (54.5);
\item at \(222,232,322,332\), vary the convex weight between the
two Riccati endpoint splittings centre by centre, keeping every
accepted conclusion rational and exact;
\item if no fixed splitting covers a required box, distinguish
failure of the \(90\times90\) sufficient factorization from
negativity of the underlying \(45\times45\) active-momentum pencil,
and introduce an active-arc or transverse subdivision only with an
explicit overlap proof.
\end{enumerate}
No third-coordinate slab is to be claimed until all upper-face
vertices have compatible exact charts.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V54]
\label{fire:newv54-final}
V54 proves an exact atlas-intersection classification and exact
failures of two specified certificate hulls.  It proves no new
positive mixed chart and no third-coordinate enlargement.  V53's
direct plus-pencil plate remains the current mixed positive region,
with a direct minus statement only on its inverted partner.  No
mixed-sign quarter cell, all-octant or full-torus positivity,
global covariant inequality, interacting continuum measure,
reflection-positive limit, exponential clustering, or Hamiltonian
Yang--Mills mass gap has been proved.  The full mass-gap goal
remains open.
\end{firewall}

\section{V55: companion audit and isolation of four third-axis defects}
\label{sec:newv55-audit-eight-charts}

This is the required five-version checkpoint.  The current
companion notes are frozen and inspected before any new Wilson
claim.  The eight V54 candidates without a screen obstruction are
then certified exactly.  Finally, a bounded centre-specific
Riccati-weight search shows that a single fixed splitting does not
repair the other four full boxes.

\subsection{Mandatory companion audit}

\label{aud:newv55-companions}
The files inspected before the chart computation were

 &15500&517791\\

 &5319&278283.

\]

\end{audit}

\begin{theorem}[V55 companion-audit decision]
\label{thm:newv55-audit}
No theorem from either companion note is imported into the finite
Wilson chart proof.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
SM V51 conditionally identifies an oriented augmented
energy-response amplitude, a Gram-to-Hilbert--Schmidt compiler, and
a two-Hilbert--Schmidt-factor trace-class metric response.  Its
physical response orientation and Sobolev Hilbert--Schmidt tail
remain open.  Even if supplied, those operator-ideal conclusions
would not imply positivity of a local \(90\times90\) active-circle
certificate block.

NS V26 computes rank-one Oseen-kernel derivative norms.  Its input
class and operator differ from the full \(45\)-dimensional reduced
Wilson fibre.  Importing it would replace the required whole-space
Loewner hypothesis by a rank-one estimate.  Hence neither
companion result supplies a premise here.
\end{proof}

 {\cal U}_{4,2}^{--+}
 :=\{012,022,032,112,122,132,212,312\}.               \tag{55.3}

\begin{proof}
At every exact physical phase triple, independently regroup the
direct plus-pencil bank and construct its rational Hermitian
splitting.  Apply the V28 radius-\(1/8\) six-vertex polygon on all
three local axes.  Exact rounded-basis congruence and Gram-residual
checks are positive at all
\[
 8\cdot6^3=1728                                       \tag{55.6}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity fills the product
boxes, twice the least block floor yields (55.5), and exact
cross-multiplication selects \(132\).  Momentum inversion and the
Hessian reserve give the last two assertions.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Bounded centre-specific splitting search}

For each blocked centre \(222,232,322,332\), compute the two
Riccati endpoint splittings once.  On the rational grid
\[
 w=j/100,\qquad0\le j\le100,                          \tag{55.7}
\]
rank convex combinations by the lesser of their centre-block
minimum and their V54 obstruction-vertex minimum.  Rationalize and
test the best five weights on the entire 216-vertex hull.

\begin{theorem}[Twenty exact fixed-splitting obstructions]
\label{thm:newv55-weight-obstructions}
None of the five selected weights at any blocked centre gives a
positive full-radius certificate.  The tested weights were
\[
\begin{array}{c|l}
222&47/100,\ 12/25,\ 23/50,\ 49/100,\ 9/20\\
232&49/100,\ 12/25,\ 1/2,\ 47/100,\ 51/100\\
322&12/25,\ 47/100,\ 49/100,\ 23/50,\ 1/2\\
332&51/100,\ 1/2,\ 13/25,\ 49/100,\ 53/100.
\end{array}                                             \tag{55.8}
\]
For every listed centre-weight pair the exact rational certificate
block has an integer-vector quadratic witness \(q<0\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For each rationalized splitting, scan all 216 rational product
vertices.  A floating eigenvector only proposes an integer vector
at the least numerical vertex.  Exact rational evaluation gives a
strictly negative quadratic form in every case.  For example, at
the first listed weight in each row the exact values are
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
222&-\dfrac{242008457861902025180340248129}
{168788655360000000000}\\[5pt]
232&-\dfrac{1931062750834448254313408003970361}
{35108040314880000000000}\\[5pt]

{8777010078720000000000}\\[5pt]
332&-\dfrac{2162854621192187591794627991266403}
{35108040314880000000000}.
\end{array}                                             \tag{55.9}
\]
The record ledger contains the other sixteen exact values and all
integer-vector hashes.  Thus every tested block is indefinite.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[Interpretation of the weight search]
\label{fire:newv55-weight}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv55-weight-obstructions} rejects the twenty
specified fixed splittings.  It does not prove that no Hermitian
splitting exists, and it does not prove physical Wilson-pencil
negativity.  In particular, \(322\) improves at its original
obstruction vertex for \(w=12/25\), but a different exact product
vertex becomes negative.  This is evidence for subdivision rather
than evidence against the mass gap.
\end{firewall}

\begin{decision}[V56 inserts a rational third-axis bridge]

Let

 v:=\exp(2i\arctan(1/16))=\frac{255+32i}{257},

 h<\frac18,\qquad
 t-h=2\arctan(8/129)<\frac{16}{129}<\frac18.          \tag{55.14}

Hence the intermediate and terminal radius-\(1/16\) intervals
overlap, while the full-radius \(n_4=1\) lower chart overlaps the
intermediate interval.  If all eight new bridge charts certify

mixed third-coordinate slab.

\section{V56: a rational bridge across the four third-axis defects}
\label{sec:newv56-rational-bridge}

V55 isolated four upper-face boxes for which wide fixed-splitting
certificates fail.  This iteration subdivides only the third
coordinate by the exact rational phase
\[
 v=\frac{255+32i}{257}=\exp(2i\arctan(1/16)),
 \qquad h:=2\arctan(1/16).                            \tag{56.1}
\]
No approximation of a physical phase is used.

\begin{record}[The V56 screen is discovery only]
\label{rec:newv56-screen}
At each pair
\[
 (n_2,n_3)\in\{(2,2),(2,3),(3,2),(3,3)\},
\]
the diagnostic screened an intermediate third phase \(u\,v\) and
the terminal phase \(u^2\), using local radii
\[
 (1/8,1/8,1/16).                                     \tag{56.2}
\]
All eight numerical minima were positive.  This only selected the
exact run.
\end{record}

\begin{theorem}[Eight exact rational bridge charts]
\label{thm:newv56-bridge-charts}
Every active momentum and every transverse momentum in any of the
eight product boxes just described satisfies
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\beta_{56}I_{45},\qquad
 \beta_{56}:=
 \frac{124858034621259483368922559804923169}
 {6924662819772807942144000000000000000000}>0.         \tag{56.3}
\]
The inverted boxes have the corresponding direct minus-pencil
bound, and the original pencils gain \(49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At the four intermediate and four terminal exact rational phase
triples, independently regroup the direct plus-pencil bank and
construct a rational Hermitian splitting.  Apply the V28 rational
polygons with radii (56.2).  Exact rounded-basis congruence and
Gram-residual checks are positive at all
\[
 8\cdot6^3=1728                                       \tag{56.4}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity fills the boxes.
Twice the smallest exact block floor is (56.3).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Pointwise closure of the two-step third-coordinate slab}

Define
\[
 {\cal P}_{2,2,2}^{--+}:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,-s_3,s_4):
 0\le s_2,s_3\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_4\le2t\}.             \tag{56.5}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact mixed two-step third-coordinate slab]
\label{thm:newv56-mixed-slab}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal P}_{2,2,2}^{--+}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{56}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{56}:=
 \frac{7044366574786104953261215974778471}
 {1184481798121401901056000000000000000000}>0.         \tag{56.6}
\]
The inverted slab has the same direct minus-pencil floor.  The
original pencils gain \(49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
V53 covers \(0\le s_4\le t\).  Fix a point with
\(t\le s_4\le2t\), and choose nearest power centres
\(n_2,n_3\in\{0,1,2,3\}\), using \(3\) in either terminal cap.
The first two local distances are bounded by \(t/2<1/8\), or by
\(\pi/4-3t<71/1792<1/8\).

If \((n_2,n_3)\) is not one of the four blocked pairs, the
full-radius \(n_4=1\) lower chart and the V40/V55 full-radius
\(n_4=2\) upper chart cover the third interval by nearest
endpoints.

At a blocked pair, use the full-radius lower chart, the
radius-\(1/16\) intermediate chart at \(t+h\), and the
radius-\(1/16\) terminal chart at \(2t\).  Their third-coordinate
intervals overlap because
\[
 h=2\arctan(1/16)<\frac18<\frac18+\frac1{16},         \tag{56.7}
\]
and the tangent subtraction formula gives
\[
 t-h=2\arctan(8/129)
 <\frac{16}{129}<\frac18=\frac1{16}+\frac1{16}.       \tag{56.8}
\]
Thus every third-coordinate fibre is covered.  Taking the product
with the already selected first two coordinates proves pointwise
coverage of (56.5).

Exact comparison shows that the four shared V40 floors, the V55
eight-chart floor, and \(\beta_{56}\) all exceed the V53 plate
floor.  Hence the latter remains the union floor (56.6).
\end{proof}

\section{V57: closure of the mixed direct-plus quarter cell}
\label{sec:newv57-mixed-quarter-cell}

The terminal \(n_4=3\) face is first de-duplicated exactly.  Its
full-radius screen then exposes five certificate defects.  The V56
rational bridge is applied only at those five pairs, after which a
single exact run certifies all genuinely new ordinary and bridge
charts.

\begin{theorem}[Exact terminal-face intersections]
\label{thm:newv57-intersections}
Among the sixteen mixed phase triples
\[
 b_\circ+(-n_2t,-n_3t,3t),\qquad0\le n_2,n_3\le3,    \tag{57.1}
\]
the exact intersections with established atlases are precisely
\[
 003,\quad103,\quad203,\quad303,                      \tag{57.2}
\]
each from the V41 original atlas.  The remaining twelve centres are
genuinely new.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The exact ledger compares ordered triples of rational unit phases
against every original and opposite power through exponent \(3\)
and every inherited mixed power through \(n_4=2\).  Only (57.2)
matches componentwise.  Equivalently, original and mixed signs
agree on axes \(2\) and \(4\), so equality requires \(n_3=0\);
opposite and lower mixed layers are excluded by the exact
non-equalities among \(u^j\), \(0\le j\le3\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{The completed mixed quarter cell}

Define
\[
 {\cal C}^{--+}:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,-s_3,s_4):
 0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le\pi/4\}.                         \tag{57.8}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Exact direct-plus mixed quarter cell]
\label{thm:newv57-quarter-cell}
For every active momentum and every transverse momentum in
\({\cal C}^{--+}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{57}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{57}:=\alpha_{57}>0.                          \tag{57.9}
\]
The inverted cell has the same direct minus-pencil floor.  The
corresponding original pencils gain \(49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
V56 covers \(0\le s_4\le2t\).  Choose nearest \(n_2,n_3\) power
centres, using \(3\) in either first- or second-coordinate cap.
Their local distances are below \(1/8\).

Outside the five pairs (57.4), the lower \(n_4=2\) and terminal
\(n_4=3\) charts are full-radius and cover by nearest endpoints.
At a pair in (57.4), use the certified lower chart, the intermediate
chart at \(2t+h\), and the small terminal chart at \(3t\).
The lower chart has third radius \(1/16\) at the four high pairs
and radius \(1/8\) at \(13\).  In every case the interval chain
overlaps because
\[
 h<\frac18=\frac1{16}+\frac1{16},\qquad
 t-h<\frac{16}{129}<\frac18.                         \tag{57.10}
\]
The terminal cap is also contained in every small terminal box:
\[
 \frac{\pi}{4}-3t<\frac{71}{1792}<\frac1{16}.         \tag{57.11}
\]
This proves pointwise coverage of (57.8).  Exact comparison with
the V56 slab floor, the four shared V41 floors, and
\(\gamma_{57}\) selects \(\alpha_{57}\), proving (57.9).
\end{proof}

\section{V58: the fourth-orientation seed cube}
\label{sec:newv58-fourth-seed}

The inverse orientation needed to supply the missing minus pencil
on the V57 mixed cell has displacement signs \((++-)\).  This
iteration constructs its first direct-plus cube without using an
orientation transport.

\begin{theorem}[Exact Boolean intersection ledger]
\label{thm:newv58-intersections}
Among the eight Boolean centres
\[
 b_\circ+(n_2t,n_3t,-n_4t),\qquad n_2,n_3,n_4\in\{0,1\}, \tag{58.1}
\]
the inherited centres are exactly
\[
 000,\quad001,\quad010,\quad100,\quad101,             \tag{58.2}
\]
and the genuinely new centres are exactly
\[
 011,\quad110,\quad111.                               \tag{58.3}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Compare every ordered rational phase triple in (58.1)
componentwise with all certified powers through exponent \(3\) in
the original \((-++)\), opposite \((+--)\), and mixed \((--+)\)
atlases.  Centre \(000\) occurs in all three atlases; \(001,100,101\)
occur in the opposite atlas; and \(010\) occurs in the original
atlas.  The exact ledger finds no match for (58.3).  This finite
rational comparison proves the classification.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}
At each exact new phase triple, independently regroup the direct
plus-pencil bank and construct a rational Hermitian splitting.
Apply the V28 radius-\(1/8\) six-vertex polygon on all three local
axes.  Exact rounded-basis congruence and Gram-residual checks are

gives (58.5).

\section{V59: a two-step slab in the fourth orientation}
\label{sec:newv59-fourth-two-step}

We now widen the direct-plus \((++-)\) atlas in its first
coordinate.  The exact phase comparison is performed before any
new matrix certificate is constructed, so an old physical centre
is never counted as new evidence.

\begin{theorem}[Exact second-layer intersection ledger]
\label{thm:newv59-intersections}
For the four candidate centres
\[
 a_{2n_3n_4}^{++-}
 :=b_\circ+(2t,n_3t,-n_4t),
 \qquad n_3,n_4\in\{0,1\},                            \tag{59.1}
\]
the centres \(200\) and \(201\) agree componentwise with the
opposite-atlas centres having the same power triples, whereas
\(210\) and \(211\) agree with no certified centre of the original,
opposite, or mixed atlases through exponent \(3\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Write \(u=(63+16i)/65\).  Each candidate is represented by an
ordered triple of Gaussian-rational unit phases, obtained by
multiplying the three anchor phases by
\((u^{n_2},u^{n_3},\overline u^{\,n_4})\).  Compare numerator and
denominator of the real and imaginary parts in each ordered
coordinate with all \(3\cdot4^3\) previously certified power
triples.  The exact finite ledger returns precisely
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
\text{candidate}&\text{exact prior match}\\ \hline
200&(\text{opposite},200)\\
201&(\text{opposite},201)\\
210&\varnothing\\
211&\varnothing .
\end{array}
\]
Equality here is equality in \(\mathbb Q(i)^3\), not a floating
comparison.  This proves the assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{record}[The V59 screen is discovery only]
\label{rec:newv59-screen}

\[

\]

\end{record}

\label{thm:newv59-two-charts}
For \(n\in\{210,211\}\), every active momentum, and every transverse
momentum in the radius-\(1/8\) product box about
\(a_n^{++-}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq\varepsilon_n I_{45}, \tag{59.3}
\]
where

n&\varepsilon_n\\ \hline

\dfrac{24288583202717510537544398281991537}
{399499778066212841472000000000000000000}\\[6pt]
211&
\dfrac{97300060471600226885006806894500437}
{2596748557471931446272000000000000000000}.
\end{array}                                           \tag{59.4}
\]
Both fractions are strictly positive.  The inverted boxes have
the corresponding direct minus-pencil bounds, and each original
pencil gains the previously proved reserve \(49/1875\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At each exact phase triple, regroup the Wilson direct plus-pencil
coefficient bank independently and construct its own rational
Hermitian central splitting.  On every local coordinate use the
radius-\(1/8\) V28 rational six-vertex polygon.  For each of the
\(2\cdot6^3=432\) product vertices, exact rational arithmetic
checks the rounded-eigenbasis congruence and the Gram-residual
lower bound and obtains a positive block floor.  Thus the two
\(90\times90\) certificate blocks are positive at every product
vertex.  Separate matrix convexity fills each product hull, and
the V28 disk inclusion fills the stated boxes.  Doubling the
minimum block floors gives (59.4).  No numerical eigenvalue is
used in this implication.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Fourth-orientation two-step slab]
\label{thm:newv59-two-step-slab}
On
\[
 {\cal K}^{++-}_{2,1,1}:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,s_3,-s_4):
       0\le s_2\le2t,\ 0\le s_3,s_4\le t\},           \tag{59.5}
\]
the direct plus pencil obeys the uniform exact bound
\[

\]

minus-pencil floor on the inverse slab.

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv58-seed-cube} covers the old cube
\([0,t]^3\) in the signed coordinates of (59.5).  It remains to
cover
\[
 [t,2t]\times[0,t]\times[0,t].                        \tag{59.7}
\]
Its lower face consists of the four full-radius V58 centres

are exactly the old opposite-atlas charts identified in
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv59-intersections}, with respective floors
\[
\frac{25802939808656007980597949804277}
 {519168002693962752000000000000000000},
\qquad
\frac{13703395147175805160268233623947}
 {519168002673196032000000000000000000};              \tag{59.8}
\]
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv59-two-charts} supplies \(210,211\).

Given a point of (59.7), choose a nearest endpoint in each
coordinate.  Each of its three distances is at most
\[
 t/2<61123/491520<1/8,                                \tag{59.9}
\]
so the corresponding one of the eight full-radius charts covers
the point.  Exact comparison of the V58 seed floor, the two
fractions (59.8), and (59.4) selects the V58 seed floor, which is
(59.6).  Joining the old and new cubes proves (59.5).
\end{proof}

\section{V60: companion checkpoint and the fourth first plate}
\label{sec:newv60-fourth-first-plate}

This is the required five-version checkpoint.  We first test the
newest companion notes for an actual implication into the present
finite-dimensional Wilson Loewner problem.  We then close the
terminal first-coordinate face of the fourth orientation.

\begin{audit}[Mandatory SM/NS checkpoint]
\label{audit:newv60-companions}
The frozen companion records are
\begin{center}
\small
\path{Renewal_SM_Einstein_Continuum_Research_Notes_V75.tex}\\
\(23322\) lines, \(777425\) bytes,
\[
\texttt{C04AB6D2F61A4D6EE21A52B577D199CF
15244A732BE50CD29D941C4D632DC1F6};

\end{center}
No theorem is imported.  SM V75 proves, in a flat periodic block
model, that neutral pairing of internal face sources removes an
infrared pole and gives a volume-uniform collective spectral bound.
That is a useful discipline for a later continuum assembly, but it
does not imply positivity of any present \(45\)-dimensional Wilson
pencil or any local \(90\)-dimensional certificate block.  NS V26
computes rank-one norms of first and second derivatives of the Oseen
kernel; those estimates likewise imply no Wilson Loewner bound.
Thus neither companion note changes the present acquisition order.
\end{audit}

\begin{theorem}[Completed fourth-orientation first quarter-face]
\label{thm:newv60-first-plate}
On
\[
 {\cal P}^{++-}_1:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,s_3,-s_4):
       0\le s_2\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_3,s_4\le t\},        \tag{60.7}

\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)

V59 covers \(0\le s_2\le2t\).  On the third cube
\([2t,3t]\times[0,t]^2\), the lower face is the full-radius V59
face \(200,201,210,211\).  Its upper face consists of the inherited

and the two charts in Theorem~\ref{thm:newv60-two-charts}.

For \(3t\le s_2\le\pi/4\), keep \(n_2=3\) and choose the nearest

while

 0\le s_2-3t\le\pi/4-3t<71/1792<1/8.                \tag{60.10}

\begin{corollary}[Both direct signs on the paired first plates]
\label{cor:newv60-paired-plates}
Let
\[
 {\cal P}^{--+}_1:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,-s_3,s_4):
       0\le s_2\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_3,s_4\le t\}.        \tag{60.11}
\]

 {427729538219916291840000000000000000000}>0.         \tag{60.12}
\]

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv57-quarter-cell} gives the direct plus pencil
with floor \(\alpha_{57}\) on the complete mixed cell and hence on
\({\cal P}^{--+}_1\).  Momentum inversion gives the direct minus
pencil with the same floor on \({\cal P}^{++-}_1\).
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv60-first-plate} supplies direct plus on the
latter, and its inversion supplies direct minus on the former.
Exact comparison gives \(\alpha_{57}<\theta_{60}\).  Adding back
the already established Hessian reserve yields the original-pencil
statement.
\end{proof}

\section{V61: the fourth second-coordinate two-step slab}
\label{sec:newv61-fourth-second-two-step}

The fourth atlas now widens in its second coordinate.  Because one
candidate phase is already present in the original atlas, the exact
intersection ledger again precedes the certificate run.

\begin{theorem}[Exact \(n_3=2\) intersection ledger]
\label{thm:newv61-intersections}
Among
\[
 a_{n_2\,2\,n_4}^{++-}
 :=b_\circ+(n_2t,2t,-n_4t),
 \qquad n_2\in\{0,1,2,3\},\quad n_4\in\{0,1\},        \tag{61.1}
\]
the centre \(020\) is exactly the original-atlas centre \(020\).
The other seven power triples
\[
 021,\ 120,\ 121,\ 220,\ 221,\ 320,\ 321             \tag{61.2}
\]
match no certified original, opposite, or mixed centre through
exponent \(3\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Compute all eight ordered phase triples in \(\mathbb Q(i)^3\) and
compare their reduced real and imaginary parts componentwise with
the \(3\cdot4^3\) old atlas records.  The exact comparison produces
the single match
\[
 (020;\text{ fourth})=(020;\text{ original})
\]
and an empty match list for every triple in (61.2).  This finite
exhaustive rational ledger proves the assertion.
\end{proof}

where

\]
The exact new-layer minimum is \(\lambda_{221}>0\).

\begin{proof}
At each of the seven exact physical centres, independently regroup
the Wilson direct plus-pencil bank and independently construct a
rational Hermitian splitting.  Apply the V28 rational six-vertex
radius-\(1/8\) polygon in the three local variables.  Exact
rounded-basis congruence and Gram-residual arithmetic is positive
at every one of
\[
 7\cdot6^3=1512                                       \tag{61.5}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity fills each product
hull and the V28 inclusion fills each radius-\(1/8\) box.  Twice
the exact minimum block floors gives (61.4), and exact
cross-multiplication selects \(221\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Fourth-atlas second-coordinate two-step slab]
\label{thm:newv61-second-slab}
On
\[
 {\cal P}^{++-}_2:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,s_3,-s_4):
 0\le s_2\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_3\le2t,\ 0\le s_4\le t\}, \tag{61.6}
\]
the direct plus pencil obeys
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{61}I_{45},\qquad

 \frac{3323190119284031031100923176317}
 {311500801619804160000000000000000000}>0.            \tag{61.7}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
V60 covers the old plate \(0\le s_3\le t\).  For the new rational
core
\[
 0\le s_2\le3t,\qquad t\le s_3\le2t,\qquad
 0\le s_4\le t,                                       \tag{61.8}
\]
split the first coordinate into its three step cubes.  Every lower
\(n_3=1\) vertex is a full-radius V58--V60 chart.  The upper face
has the exactly inherited original chart \(020\), with floor
\[
 \frac{65735741302062922977663008547209}
 {778752004086111744000000000000000000},              \tag{61.9}
\]
and the seven full-radius charts of
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv61-seven-charts}.  Coordinatewise nearest
endpoints are within \(t/2<1/8\), so the sixteen upper and lower
face charts cover (61.8).

For the cap \(3t\le s_2\le\pi/4\), keep \(n_2=3\) and choose the
nearest \(n_3\in\{1,2\}\) and \(n_4\in\{0,1\}\).  The latter
distances are at most \(t/2\), and the first is at most
\(\pi/4-3t<71/1792<1/8\).  Hence the eight terminal charts cover
the cap.  Exact comparison of (61.9), (61.4), and the V60 plate
floor selects the latter, proving (61.7).
\end{proof}

\]
with common floor \(\alpha_{57}\) from (60.12).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}

fourth slab and, after inversion, direct minus on the mixed slab.
Since \(\alpha_{57}<\theta_{61}\), the stated common floor follows.
\end{proof}

\section{V62: closure of the fourth second quarter face}
\label{sec:newv62-fourth-second-face}

We close the terminal \(n_3=3\) face and the remaining angular cap
in the second coordinate.  As before, phase identity is decided
exactly before new certificates are constructed.

\begin{theorem}[Exact \(n_3=3\) intersection ledger]
\label{thm:newv62-intersections}
Among
\[
 a_{n_2\,3\,n_4}^{++-}
 :=b_\circ+(n_2t,3t,-n_4t),
 \qquad n_2\in\{0,1,2,3\},\quad n_4\in\{0,1\},        \tag{62.1}
\]
the centre \(030\) is exactly the original-atlas centre \(030\).
The remaining seven centres
\[
 031,\ 130,\ 131,\ 230,\ 231,\ 330,\ 331             \tag{62.2}
\]
have no componentwise-identical phase triple in the certified
original, opposite, or mixed atlases through exponent \(3\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Enumerate the eight ordered Gaussian-rational phase triples and the
\(3\cdot4^3\) old atlas triples.  Equality is tested after reduction
of both real and imaginary components in all three ordered
coordinates.  The exact intersection is the singleton
\((030;\mathrm{original})\); the seven match lists in (62.2) are
empty.  Exhaustion of these finite lists proves the result.
\end{proof}

\label{rec:newv62-screen}

The smallest floating value,
\(2.978100949286\cdot10^{-6}\), occurred at \(231\).  It is not
used as evidence in the exact theorem.
\end{record}

\begin{theorem}[Seven exact terminal second-coordinate charts]
\label{thm:newv62-seven-charts}
For every \(n\) in (62.2), every active momentum, and every
transverse momentum in the radius-\(1/8\) product box about

\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq\mu_{62}I_{45},    \tag{62.3}

 \frac{160628625813916257938454500944947947}
 {27006184996708858201497600000000000000000}>0.       \tag{62.4}
\]
The minimum is attained by the recorded chart \(231\).  The
inverted boxes have the corresponding direct minus-pencil bound.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At each of the seven exact physical centres independently regroup
the direct Wilson bank and construct its own rational Hermitian
splitting.  Apply the V28 six-vertex radius-\(1/8\) rational polygon
on all three local axes.  Exact rounded-basis congruence and exact
Gram-residual certification pass at all
\[
 7\cdot6^3=1512                                       \tag{62.5}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity fills the product
hulls and the V28 disk inclusion fills the boxes.  Twice each exact
minimum block floor is positive; direct cross-multiplication of the
seven recorded values selects the \(231\) value (62.4).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Completed fourth second quarter face]
\label{thm:newv62-second-face}
On
\[
 {\cal Q}^{++-}_{12}:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,s_3,-s_4):
       0\le s_2,s_3\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_4\le t\},        \tag{62.6}
\]
the direct plus pencil obeys
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{62}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{62}:=\mu_{62}>0.                             \tag{62.7}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
V61 covers \(0\le s_3\le2t\).  For the rational third layer
\[
 0\le s_2\le3t,\quad 2t\le s_3\le3t,\quad
 0\le s_4\le t,                                       \tag{62.8}
\]
the lower \(n_3=2\) face consists of the full-radius V61 charts.
The upper face consists of the exactly inherited original chart
\(030\), whose floor is
\[
 \frac{101002011062490397772958551593163}
 {1349836807015101849600000000000000000},             \tag{62.9}
\]
and the seven charts in Theorem~\ref{thm:newv62-seven-charts}.
On each step cube a coordinatewise nearest endpoint is within
\(t/2<1/8\), proving coverage of (62.8).

For \(3t\le s_2\le\pi/4\) with \(2t\le s_3\le3t\), keep \(n_2=3\);
for \(3t\le s_3\le\pi/4\), keep \(n_3=3\).  Choose every remaining
rational endpoint nearest to the point.  Each step distance is at
most \(t/2\), and each terminal distance is at most
\[
 \pi/4-3t<71/1792<1/8.                               \tag{62.10}
\]
This covers both terminal strips, including their corner.  Exact
comparison gives
\(\mu_{62}<\theta_{61}\) and \(\mu_{62}\) below (62.9), so (62.7)
is the common floor.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Both signs on the paired second quarter faces]

\section{V63: the fourth third-coordinate obstruction ledger}
\label{sec:newv63-fourth-third-obstruction}

The final coordinate begins differently from the first two: the
standard full-radius central splitting fails at four of the required
centres.  This iteration records exact failure witnesses and
certifies every unobstructed new chart, without claiming a slab.

\begin{theorem}[Exact \(n_4=2\) intersection ledger]
\label{thm:newv63-intersections}
Among the sixteen candidates
\[
 a_{n_2n_3\,2}^{++-}
 :=b_\circ+(n_2t,n_3t,-2t),
 \qquad n_2,n_3\in\{0,1,2,3\},                        \tag{63.1}
\]
the four centres
\[
 002,\quad102,\quad202,\quad302                      \tag{63.2}
\]
are exactly the opposite-atlas centres with the same power triples.
The other twelve candidates match no certified original, opposite,
or mixed phase triple through exponent \(3\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Enumerate the ordered triples in \(\mathbb Q(i)^3\) for (63.1) and
compare reduced real and imaginary parts componentwise with all
\(3\cdot4^3\) old atlas triples.  The exact intersection consists
only of the four pairs in (63.2).  Every other match list is empty.
This finite exhaustive equality test proves the statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{countertheorem}[Four exact failures of the fixed full-radius splitting]
\label{ctr:newv63-four-failures}
For the independently proposed central splittings at
\[
 222,\quad232,\quad322,\quad332,                      \tag{63.3}
\]
the radius-\(1/8\) product hull contains a vertex at which the
corresponding \(90\times90\) certificate block is not positive
semidefinite.  Exact integer test vectors give respectively the
negative quadratic values
\[
\begin{aligned}
q_{222}&=-\frac{29432992764628819424593402550269}
 {1350309242880000000000},\\
q_{232}&=-\frac{89471919527475575613480212774117}
 {1350309242880000000000},\\
q_{322}&=-\frac{90653838276369363774168152239}
 {3802040320000000000},\\
q_{332}&=-\frac{744477008771443233144968095696387}
 {11702680104960000000000}.
\end{aligned}                                         \tag{63.4}
\]
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
For each centre, evaluate its exact grouped coefficient bank and
rational Hermitian splitting at the recorded product vertex
\[
 (511/513,-16/171,\ 127/128,-511/4096,\
   127/128,511/4096),                                 \tag{63.5}
\]
except that the first pair for \(322\) is
\((127/128,-511/4096)\).  Contracting the resulting rational
Hermitian block with the corresponding recorded nonzero integer
vector yields (63.4).  Each value is strictly negative, so that
particular certificate block cannot be positive semidefinite on
the full product hull.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[Meaning of the four witnesses]
\label{fire:newv63-witness-meaning}
Countertheorem~\ref{ctr:newv63-four-failures} rejects only the
specified sufficient \(90\times90\) splitting and full-radius hull.
It neither proves that the physical \(45\times45\) Wilson pencil is
negative nor rules out another splitting, a smaller anisotropic
chart, or an overlapping bridge cover.
\end{firewall}

 8\cdot6^3=1728                                       \tag{63.8}

\begin{assessment}[A localized certificate obstruction]
\label{ass:newv63}
Four inherited opposite-atlas charts and eight newly certified
charts provide twelve of the sixteen upper-face vertices.  The
four missing vertices form the block
\(\{2,3\}\times\{2,3\}\times\{2\}\).  Because the lower
\(n_4=1\) face is complete but these four upper vertices lack
compatible charts, V63 makes no third-coordinate slab claim.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V64 goes upstream to rational bridge charts]
\label{dec:newv63-next}
Do not repeat the failed full-radius splitting or the centre-weight
search already exhausted in the analogous mixed atlas.  Introduce
the exact rational phase
\[
 v=\frac{255+32i}{257}
   =\exp\!\bigl(2i\arctan(1/16)\bigr),\qquad
 h=2\arctan(1/16),                                   \tag{63.12}
\]
and test bridge centres on the third axis for the four blocked
pairs \((n_2,n_3)=(2,2),(2,3),(3,2),(3,3)\), using radii
\((1/8,1/8,1/16)\).  Require exact phase construction, independent
splittings, and exact product-vertex certification before using
the overlap inequalities
\[
 h<1/8,\qquad t-h<16/129<1/8                         \tag{63.13}
\]
in a cover.
\end{decision}

\section{V64: rational repair of the fourth power-two third-coordinate layer}
\label{sec:newv64-fourth-rational-bridge}

V63 localized the obstruction to four upper vertices.  We now use
an exact conjugate half-step on the negative third axis, rather than
altering the already failed full-radius splitting.

\begin{definition}[Conjugate rational bridge]
\label{def:newv64-bridge}
Put
\[
 v_-:=\frac{255-32i}{257}
 =\exp(-ih),\qquad h:=2\arctan(1/16),                 \tag{64.1}
\]
and let
\[
 {\cal B}:=\{(2,2),(2,3),(3,2),(3,3)\}.              \tag{64.2}
\]
For \((j,\ell)\in{\cal B}\), retain the exact fourth-atlas phases
in the first two coordinates.  On the third axis use one
intermediate phase obtained by multiplying the \(n_4=1\) phase by
\(v_-\), and retain the exact \(n_4=2\) terminal phase.  Both boxes
have radii
\[
 (r,r,r_b)=(1/8,1/8,1/16).                           \tag{64.3}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{record}[The V64 screen is discovery only]
\label{rec:newv64-screen}
All eight \(216\)-vertex anisotropic screens—four intermediate and
four small terminal boxes—were positive.  The smallest floating
value, at the small terminal chart \(232\), was
\[
 9.019632562493\cdot10^{-6}.
\]
The proof below is an independent exact run.
\end{record}

\begin{theorem}[Eight exact conjugate bridge charts]
\label{thm:newv64-eight-bridges}
For every active momentum, the direct plus pencil is positive on
all eight boxes of Definition~\ref{def:newv64-bridge}, with common
exact floor
\[
 \beta_{64}:=
 \frac{3246191153368060548562902829742213729}
 {180041233321474697023488000000000000000000}>0.      \tag{64.4}
\]
The inverted boxes have the corresponding direct minus-pencil
bound.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The phases in (64.1) are exact rational unit phases.  At each of
the eight exact centres independently regroup the Wilson
direct-plus bank and construct a rational Hermitian splitting.
Use the V28 six-vertex polygon of radius \(1/8\) on the first two
local axes and its six-vertex radius-\(1/16\) version on the third.
Exact rounded-basis congruence and exact Gram-residual checks are
positive at all
\[
 8\cdot6^3=1728                                       \tag{64.5}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity fills the product
hulls.  Twice the exact block floors and exact fraction comparison
give (64.4), attained by the small \(232\) chart.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The bridge intervals join \(t\) to \(2t\)]
\label{lem:newv64-overlap}
On the signed third-coordinate axis, the radius-\(1/8\) lower box
at \(t\), the radius-\(1/16\) bridge box at \(t+h\), and the
radius-\(1/16\) terminal box at \(2t\) have a connected union
containing \([t,2t]\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The first two intervals overlap because
\[
 h=2\arctan(1/16)<1/8<1/8+1/16.                     \tag{64.6}
\]
The tangent subtraction formula gives
\[
 t-h=2\arctan(8/129)<16/129<1/8=1/16+1/16,          \tag{64.7}
\]
so the second and third intervals overlap.  Since the first
contains \(t\) and the last contains \(2t\), their connected union
contains the entire segment.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Fourth third-coordinate two-step slab]
\label{thm:newv64-third-two-step}
On
\[
 {\cal Q}^{++-}_{2}:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,s_3,-s_4):
 0\le s_2,s_3\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_4\le2t\},             \tag{64.8}
\]
the direct plus pencil obeys
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{64}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{64}:=
 \frac{160628625813916257938454500944947947}
 {27006184996708858201497600000000000000000}>0.       \tag{64.9}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
V62 covers \(0\le s_4\le t\).  Consider first
\(0\le s_2,s_3\le3t\) and \(t\le s_4\le2t\).  Choose nearest
rational endpoints in the first two coordinates; their distances
are at most \(t/2<1/8\).

If the selected upper \(n_4=2\) vertex is not in the block
\({\cal B}\), it is either one of the four exactly inherited
opposite-atlas charts (63.2) or one of the eight V63 full-radius
charts.  The lower and upper radius-\(1/8\) intervals cover the
third-coordinate step.  If the selected pair belongs to
\({\cal B}\), its lower \(n_4=1\) chart is full-radius and
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv64-eight-bridges} supplies the intermediate
and small terminal charts; Lemma~\ref{lem:newv64-overlap} covers
the third-coordinate segment.  All these charts have radius
\(1/8\) in the first two axes, so the selected chart also covers
the transverse distances.

For either \(s_2\) or \(s_3\) in the terminal cap
\([3t,\pi/4]\), keep the corresponding endpoint equal to \(3t\).
Its distance is below \(71/1792<1/8\); the same preceding argument
then covers the terminal strips and their corner.  Thus (64.8) is
covered pointwise.

The common floors entering the cover are the V62 face floor
\(\theta_{62}\), the V63 new-chart floor \(\nu_{63}\), the four
shared opposite-atlas chart floors, and \(\beta_{64}\).  Exact
comparison selects \(\theta_{62}\), which is (64.9).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Both signs on the paired third-coordinate slabs]
\label{cor:newv64-paired-slabs}
Both direct signs are positive on \({\cal Q}^{++-}_{2}\) and its
mixed inverse
\[
 {\cal Q}^{--+}_{2}:=
 \{b_\circ+(-s_2,-s_3,s_4):
 0\le s_2,s_3\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_4\le2t\},             \tag{64.10}
\]
with common floor \(\alpha_{57}\) from (60.12).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
V57 gives direct plus on the complete mixed quarter cell and hence
on \({\cal Q}^{--+}_{2}\); inversion gives direct minus on the
complete fourth cell and hence on \({\cal Q}^{++-}_{2}\).
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv64-third-two-step} supplies direct plus on the
fourth slab and, after inversion, direct minus on the mixed slab.
Exact comparison gives \(\alpha_{57}<\theta_{64}\).
\end{proof}

\section{V65: companion checkpoint and completion of the fourth cell}
\label{sec:newv65-fourth-cell}

This iteration first performs the compulsory companion audit.  It
then treats the terminal third-coordinate layer by separating seven
ordinary full-radius charts from five exactly witnessed failures and
repairing the latter with conjugate rational bridges.

\begin{audit}[Mandatory V65 companion checkpoint]
\label{audit:newv65-companions}

\small
\path{Renewal_SM_Einstein_Continuum_Research_Notes_V88.tex}\\

No theorem is imported.  SM V88 proves that a uniform moment in a
stronger negative Sobolev norm implies tightness in a weaker norm,
and supplies a polynomial mode-growth criterion.  It does not
derive the required moments, construct the continuum measure, or
imply any finite Wilson Loewner chart.  NS V26's rank-one Oseen
derivative norms likewise imply no Wilson bound.  The local
terminal-layer acquisition order therefore remains unchanged.
\end{audit}

\begin{theorem}[Exact terminal intersection ledger]
\label{thm:newv65-intersections}
Among
\[
 a_{n_2n_3\,3}^{++-}
 :=b_\circ+(n_2t,n_3t,-3t),
 \qquad n_2,n_3\in\{0,1,2,3\},                        \tag{65.2}

\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Compare all ordered triples in \(\mathbb Q(i)^3\) by reduced real
and imaginary components.  Exhaustion against the
\(3\cdot4^3\) prior atlas records returns exactly the four matches
stated above and empty match lists for the remaining twelve.
\end{proof}

\begin{countertheorem}[Five terminal fixed-splitting failures]
\label{ctr:newv65-five-failures}

\[
 (511/513,-16/171,\ 127/128,-511/4096,\

\]
the independently proposed certificate splittings at

q_{133}&=-\frac{1008794116846649748198810210168889}
 {228202262046720000000000},\\

 {456404524093440000000000}.

\]

\begin{proof}
At each centre reconstruct the exact rational certificate block at
(65.3) and contract with the recorded nonzero integer vector.
The five reduced values are (65.4), hence each block has a negative

certificate, not the physical \(45\times45\) Wilson pencil.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Seventeen exact terminal repair charts]
\label{thm:newv65-seventeen-charts}
The seven ordinary full-radius terminal charts
\[
 013,\ 023,\ 033,\ 113,\ 123,\ 213,\ 313             \tag{65.5}
\]
have common direct plus-pencil floor
\[
 \omega_{65}:=
 \frac{32285333114551279609414733991019043}
 {5560483996547524691712000000000000000000}>0.        \tag{65.6}
\]
For each blocked pair
\[
 (1,3),(2,2),(2,3),(3,2),(3,3),                     \tag{65.7}
\]
the conjugate half-step bridge at \(2t+h\) and the small terminal
chart at \(3t\) use the common radii
\[
 (1/8,1/8,1/16).
\]
These ten charts have common floor
\[
 \varpi_{65}:=
 \frac{107450224987098180813145630543247111291}
 {7021608099537513183916032000000000000000000}>0.    \tag{65.8}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At every one of the seventeen exact physical centres independently
regroup the Wilson direct-plus bank and construct a rational
Hermitian splitting.  Use the radius-\(1/8\) six-vertex polygon on
all axes for the seven ordinary charts.  For the ten repair charts,
use radius \(1/8\) in the first two axes and radius \(1/16\) in the
third.  Exact rounded-basis congruence and exact Gram-residual
certification pass at all
\[
 17\cdot6^3=3672                                      \tag{65.9}
\]
product vertices.  Separate matrix convexity fills the product
hulls.  Exact comparison of twice the block floors gives (65.6)
and (65.8), attained at ordinary centre \(313\) and small terminal
centre \(233\), respectively.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact fourth direct-plus quarter cell]
\label{thm:newv65-fourth-cell}
On
\[
 {\cal C}^{++-}:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,s_3,-s_4):
       0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le\pi/4\},                   \tag{65.10}
\]
the direct plus pencil satisfies
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{65}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{65}:=\omega_{65}>0.                         \tag{65.11}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
V64 covers \(0\le s_4\le2t\).  For the rational third layer,
choose nearest endpoints in the first two coordinates.  At an
unblocked pair, the \(n_4=2\) lower and \(n_4=3\) upper charts are
full-radius, so nearest endpoints cover the step.

At a pair in (65.7), use the lower chart at \(2t\), the bridge at
\(2t+h\), and the small terminal chart at \(3t\).  For the four
pairs already blocked at \(n_4=2\), the lower radius is \(1/16\);
for \((1,3)\) it is \(1/8\).  In either case the first overlap holds
because
\[
 h<1/8=1/16+1/16,                                    \tag{65.12}
\]
and the second because
\[
 t-h=2\arctan(8/129)<16/129<1/8=1/16+1/16.          \tag{65.13}
\]
Thus every difficult third-axis interval from \(2t\) to \(3t\) is
covered.  All repair boxes retain radius \(1/8\) transversely.

The first two coordinate caps are covered by keeping the relevant
endpoint at \(3t\), since \(\pi/4-3t<71/1792<1/8\).  For the
terminal third-coordinate cap, keep \(n_4=3\).  The ordinary boxes
have radius \(1/8\), and the five small boxes suffice because
\[
 \pi/4-3t<71/1792<1/16.                              \tag{65.14}
\]
This includes all intersections of the three caps and proves
pointwise coverage of (65.10).

The cover floors are the V64 slab floor, the four exactly inherited
opposite-atlas terminal floors, \(\omega_{65}\), and
\(\varpi_{65}\).  Exact fraction comparison selects
\(\omega_{65}\), proving (65.11).
\end{proof}

\section{V66: the fifth-orientation seed cube}
\label{sec:newv66-fifth-seed}

The four completed sign cells leave two inversion pairs.  We begin
the direct-plus representative \((+++)\), whose inverse is
\((---)\).  The first task is deliberately local: identify the old
physical centres in its Boolean seed cube exactly, construct only
the missing charts, and prove the cube cover without invoking any
coordinate-permutation transport.

\begin{theorem}[Exact fifth-orientation intersection ledger]
\label{thm:newv66-intersections}
Among the eight Boolean centres
\[
 a^{+++}_{n_2n_3n_4}
 :=b_\circ+(n_2t,n_3t,n_4t),
 \qquad n_2,n_3,n_4\in\{0,1\},                       \tag{66.1}
\]
the inherited centres are exactly
\[
 000,\quad001,\quad010,\quad011,\quad100,\quad110,   \tag{66.2}
\]
and the genuinely new centres are exactly
\[
 101,\quad111.                                       \tag{66.3}
\]
More precisely, the exact prior matches are
\[
\begin{array}{c|l}
000&(\mathrm{original},000),(\mathrm{opposite},000),
     (\mathrm{mixed},000),(\mathrm{fourth},000)\\
001&(\mathrm{original},001),(\mathrm{mixed},001)\\
010&(\mathrm{original},010),(\mathrm{fourth},010)\\
011&(\mathrm{original},011)\\
100&(\mathrm{opposite},100),(\mathrm{fourth},100)\\
110&(\mathrm{fourth},110).
\end{array}                                           \tag{66.4}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Represent every centre by its ordered triple in
\(\mathbb Q(i)^3\).  Compare the reduced real and imaginary
components with all \(4\cdot4^3\) certified centres of the original,
opposite, mixed, and fourth atlases through exponent \(3\).  The
finite comparison returns precisely (66.4), and returns empty match
lists for \(101\) and \(111\).  Thus (66.2)--(66.3) are equalities
of exact phase triples, not numerical identifications.
\end{proof}

\begin{record}[The V66 screen is discovery only]
\label{rec:newv66-screen}
A floating-point full-radius product screen was positive at both
new centres.  It selected the splittings tested below but is not
used in any positivity implication.
\end{record}

\begin{theorem}[Two exact fifth-orientation charts]
\label{thm:newv66-two-charts}
For \(n\in\{101,111\}\), every active momentum, and every transverse
momentum in the radius-\(1/8\) product box about
\(a_n^{+++}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)\succeq\delta_n I_{45},   \tag{66.5}

\section{V70: companion checkpoint and the fifth second quarter-face}
\label{sec:newv70-fifth-second-face}

This is the mandatory five-version checkpoint.  We first test the
newest companion results for an actual implication into the present
finite-dimensional Wilson problem.  We then close the terminal
second-coordinate layer.

\label{audit:newv70-companions}

\]

SM V5 proves a finite angular-atlas stability theorem: coercivity
away from a compact zero cone, together with uniform lower bounds
on finitely many covering conic charts, implies a global lower
bound.  That theorem assumes the local chart bounds and verified
coverage; it does not construct a Wilson chart, relate its shell
variables to the present phase torus, or imply positivity of a
\(45\times45\) pencil.  It reinforces the present coverage
discipline but supplies no mathematical input.  NS V26's rank-one
Oseen-kernel derivative norms likewise imply no Wilson Loewner
bound.  No companion theorem is imported, and the local acquisition
order remains unchanged.
\end{audit}

\begin{theorem}[Four exact fifth rational-repair charts]
\label{thm:newv72-four-charts}

\]
throughout the corresponding product boxes, uniformly in active

\begin{proof}
Each centre is an exact triple of rational unit phases.  Independently
regroup its Wilson direct-plus coefficient bank and construct a
rational Hermitian splitting.  Use the radius-\(1/8\) six-vertex
polygon on the first two local axes and the radius-\(1/16\)
six-vertex polygon on the third.  Exact rounded-basis congruence and
Gram-residual certification pass at
\[
 4\cdot6^3=864.                                       \tag{72.4}
\]
Separate matrix convexity and the rational disk inclusions fill all

(72.3), attained at the small \(232\) chart.

\begin{theorem}[Fifth third-coordinate two-step slab]
\label{thm:newv72-two-step-slab}
On
\[
 {\cal Q}^{+++}_{12,2}:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,s_3,s_4):
 0\le s_2,s_3\le\pi/4,\ 0\le s_4\le2t\},             \tag{72.5}
\]
the direct plus pencil obeys
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{72}I_{45},\qquad

 \frac{23784808996629854813771747874389}
 {241558005344776833024000000000000000000}>0.         \tag{72.6}
\]

\begin{proof}
V70 covers \(0\le s_4\le t\).  Consider first the rational core
\[
 0\le s_2,s_3\le3t,\qquad t\le s_4\le2t.             \tag{72.7}
\]
For every unblocked upper vertex, the lower \(n_4=1\) chart and the
upper \(n_4=2\) chart both have radius \(1/8\), so nearest endpoints
cover the third-axis step.

At the blocked transverse pairs \((2,2),(2,3)\), the lower chart at
\(t\) has third radius \(1/8\), the bridge at \(t+h\) has radius
\(1/16\), and the terminal chart at \(2t\) has radius \(1/16\).
Their one-dimensional intervals overlap because
\[
 h=2\arctan(1/16)<1/8<1/8+1/16                     \tag{72.8}
\]
and
\[
 t-h=2\arctan(8/129)<16/129<1/8=1/16+1/16.          \tag{72.9}
\]

Choosing nearest transverse endpoints therefore covers every
rational cube in (72.7).

For the first- and second-coordinate caps, keep the capped grid
index equal to \(3\), choose the other transverse endpoint nearest
to the point, and use
\[
 \pi/4-3t<71/1792<1/8.                               \tag{72.10}
\]
The blocked pairs have first index \(2\), so a first-coordinate cap
never selects them; the second cap may select \((2,3)\), whose
repair boxes retain transverse radius \(1/8\).  Thus the cap strips
and their corner are covered.  Exact comparison among the V70
floor, four inherited original-chart floors, the ten V71 floors,
and (72.3) selects the V71 value (72.6).
\end{proof}

full-torus positivity, construct an interacting continuum measure,
establish reflection positivity or clustering, or prove a
Hamiltonian Yang--Mills mass gap.  The full mass-gap goal remains
open.
\end{firewall}

 a_{n_2n_3\,3}^{+++}

\section{V74: terminal repair and the fifth quarter cell}
\label{sec:newv74-fifth-cell}

The four V73 failures concern only the fixed full-radius splitting.
We now place a positive rational half-step between the lower and
terminal centres, shrink only the third angular radius, and certify
the resulting eight charts independently.  This repairs the four
columns without asserting an unproved orientation symmetry.

\subsection{The positive half-step repair}

Put
\[
 h:=2\arctan(1/16),\qquad
 \zeta_h:=\frac{255+32i}{257}=e^{ih}.                    \tag{74.1}
\]
For \(n_2n_3\in\{22,23,32,33\}\), let
\[
\begin{aligned}
 d_{n_2n_3}
   &:=b_\circ+(n_2t,n_3t,2t+h),\\
 e_{n_2n_3}
   &:=b_\circ+(n_2t,n_3t,3t).
\end{aligned}                                          \tag{74.2}
\]
The phase triples at these centres are Gaussian-rational because
\(\zeta_h\), the phase of \(t\), and the coordinates of \(b_\circ\)
are Gaussian-rational.

\begin{theorem}[Eight exact terminal-repair charts]
\label{thm:newv74-repair}
On the product boxes of angular radii
\[
 (1/8,1/8,1/16)                                        \tag{74.3}
\]
about the eight centres in (74.2), the direct plus pencil satisfies
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\beta_{74}I_{45},\qquad
 \beta_{74}:=
 \frac{259509357093630463169249262978427919}
 {13849325638894697582592000000000000000000}>0.        \tag{74.4}
\]
All \(8\cdot6^3=1728\) product vertices pass in exact rational
arithmetic.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At each centre, the direct Wilson coefficient bank is regrouped
afresh and its rational Hermitian splitting is constructed
independently.  On the first two axes use the radius-\(1/8\)
six-vertex rational polygon; on the last axis use the radius-\(1/16\)
polygon with inner parameters \(1/64,1/32\).  For every one of the
\(6^3\) product vertices, rounded-basis congruence reduces the
candidate block and a rational Gram-residual debit supplies a
strict lower bound.

The smallest doubled block floor among the 1728 exact checks is
(74.4), attained at the small chart \(e_{23}\).  Separate affine
dependence on the three phase variables and iterated matrix
convexity fill the polygonal product hulls.  The previously proved
disk inclusions then fill the physical angular boxes.  Thus the
finite vertex calculation proves the stated pointwise matrix
inequality, rather than merely sampling the boxes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exact terminal interval cover]
\label{lem:newv74-terminal-cover}
For each of the four repaired pairs, the lower chart at \(2t\), the
bridge chart at \(2t+h\), and the small terminal chart at \(3t\)
form a connected cover from \(2t\) to \(\pi/4\) in the third
coordinate.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The elementary inequality \(\arctan x<x\), for \(x>0\), gives
\[
 h<1/8.                                                 \tag{74.5}
\]
The tangent subtraction formula, with both angles in
\((0,\pi/2)\), gives
\[
\begin{split}
 t-h
 &=2\arctan\!\left(
     \frac{1/8-1/16}{1+(1/8)(1/16)}\right)\\
 &=2\arctan(8/129)<16/129<1/8.
\end{split}                                            \tag{74.6}
\]
For the pairs \(22,23\), the V72 lower charts and the two new charts
all have third radius \(1/16\); hence (74.5)--(74.6) are strictly
below the sums of adjacent radii.  For \(32,33\), the lower chart
has radius \(1/8\), so its first overlap is larger still.

Finally, \(\arctan x>x-x^3/3\) at \(x=1/8\), together with
\(\pi<22/7\), yields
\[
 0<\frac{\pi}{4}-3t
 <\frac{11}{14}-6\left(\frac18-\frac1{1536}\right)
 =\frac{71}{1792}<\frac1{16}.                          \tag{74.7}
\]
Thus each terminal chart reaches the cap at \(\pi/4\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Closure of the cell}

Define the fifth direct-plus quarter cell
\[
 {\cal C}^{+++}_5:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,s_3,s_4):
          0\le s_2,s_3,s_4\le\pi/4\}.                  \tag{74.8}
\]

\begin{theorem}[The fifth direct-plus quarter cell]
\label{thm:newv74-fifth-cell}
Throughout \({\cal C}^{+++}_5\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{74}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{74}:=
 \frac{23784808996629854813771747874389}
 {241558005344776833024000000000000000000}>0.          \tag{74.9}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv72-two-step-slab} covers
\(0\le s_4\le2t\).  It remains to cover
\(2t\le s_4\le\pi/4\).

For each of \(s_2,s_3\), choose a nearest centre among
\(0,t,2t,3t\).  Inside \([0,3t]\) the distance is at most
\(t/2<1/8\); in the terminal cap it is at most the quantity in
(74.7).  Thus the full transverse radii in the first two
coordinates always cover the selected point.

For the twelve unblocked terminal columns, the full-radius charts
at \(2t\) and \(3t\) overlap because \(t<1/4=1/8+1/8\), and the
terminal chart covers the cap.  Four of the \(3t\) charts are the
original-atlas charts \(003,013,023,033\); the remaining eight are
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv73-eight-charts}.  The four blocked columns are
covered by Lemma~\ref{lem:newv74-terminal-cover} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv74-repair}.  Hence every point of (74.8) lies
in a certified chart.

The exact floors entering this finite cover are the V72 slab floor,
the four inherited original-chart floors, the V73 eight-chart
floor, and \(\beta_{74}\).  Exact cross-multiplication selects the
V72 floor, which is precisely (74.9).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The inverted sixth cell]
\label{cor:newv74-inverted-cell}
On the inverted cell \(-{\cal C}^{+++}_5\), the direct minus pencil
has the same lower bound:
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,-}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{74}I_{45},
 \qquad k\in-{\cal C}^{+++}_5.                          \tag{74.10}
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:newv4-inversion},
\(P_{\mathrm W,-}(-k)=\overline{P_{\mathrm W,+}(k)}\), to
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv74-fifth-cell}.  Complex conjugation preserves
the Hermitian order and the exact lower bound.
\end{proof}

global comparison invokes a certification grid assigned to its
future V27.  It is therefore neither a self-contained import nor a
Yang--Mills continuum input.  The audit changes no V74 hypothesis.
\end{proof}

 a^{---}_{n_2n_3n_4}

\section{V76: two-step first-coordinate depth in the sixth atlas}
\label{sec:newv76-sixth-first-two-step}

We enlarge the independently constructed \((---)\) direct-plus
atlas in one coordinate.  Exact phase de-duplication is performed
before either new splitting is built.

\begin{theorem}[Exact sixth \(n_2=2\) face ledger]
\label{thm:newv76-intersections}
On the face
\[
 a^{---}_{2n_3n_4}=b_\circ-(2t,n_3t,n_4t),
 \qquad n_3,n_4\in\{0,1\},                              \tag{76.1}
\]
the centres \(200\) and \(210\) are inherited, respectively, from
the original/mixed atlases and the mixed atlas.  The centres \(201\)
and \(211\) match no previously certified phase triple.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Compare the reduced real and imaginary parts of the four ordered
Gaussian-rational phase triples with the five completed
\(4^3\)-centre atlases and the V75 sixth seed.  Equality gives
\[
\begin{array}{c|l}
200&(\mathrm{original},200),(\mathrm{mixed},200)\\
210&(\mathrm{mixed},210),\\
201&\varnothing\\
211&\varnothing.
\end{array}                                             \tag{76.2}
\]
Thus only the last two splittings require construction.
\end{proof}

 \frac{680125411954756513591820485362317}
 {17755545691831681843200000000000000000}>0.           \tag{76.3}
\]

centre and construct two rational Hermitian splittings.  At every
one of the \(2\cdot6^3=432\) product vertices, exact
rounded-congruence and Gram-residual estimates yield a strictly
positive block floor.  The two doubled least floors are
\[
 \frac{6038096019210378870967063458937}
 {129792000670505472000000000000000000},
 \qquad
 \nu_{76},                                             \tag{76.4}
\]
so their exact minimum is (76.3).  Separate matrix convexity and the
radius-\(1/8\) disk inclusions fill both physical boxes.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}
SM V29 proves a conditional Laplace estimate for a declared convex

identified with the RPESM gravitational action and does not close
the infinite-volume construction.  It supplies neither a Wilson
Loewner chart nor a verified Yang--Mills continuum hypothesis.
NS V26 supplies rank-one Oseen-kernel derivative norms, which imply
neither of those ingredients.  Hence the audit changes no local
Wilson or later continuum premise in this programme.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exact negative-direction overlap]
\label{lem:newv81-overlap}

 t-h=2\arctan(8/129)<16/129<1/8=1/16+1/16.            \tag{81.6}

\section{V83: terminal repair and the paired fifth/sixth cells}
\label{sec:newv83-sixth-cell}

Set
\[
 t=2\arctan(1/8),\qquad h=2\arctan(1/16),\qquad
 \overline{\zeta_h}=\frac{255-32i}{257}.               \tag{83.1}
\]
For each blocked pair \(22,23,32,33\), multiply the third phase at
the \(n_4=2\) centre by \(\overline{\zeta_h}\) to obtain a bridge
at depth \(2t+h\), and retain the exact \(n_4=3\) centre with third
radius \(1/16\).

\begin{theorem}[Eight exact terminal-repair charts]
\label{thm:newv83-repair}
The four bridge charts and four small terminal charts, all with
angular radii
\[
 (1/8,1/8,1/16),                                      \tag{83.2}
\]
have direct plus-pencil common floor
\[
\beta_{83}:=
\frac{519041537870655445232375518854077269}
     {27698651280476164325376000000000000000000}>0.    \tag{83.3}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
All phase coordinates in (83.1) are rational points of the unit
circle.  At each of the eight phase triples, independently regroup
the direct Wilson coefficient bank and construct a rational
Hermitian splitting.  Use the radius-\(1/8\) rational polygon in
the first two variables and the radius-\(1/16\) polygon in the
third.  Exact rounded-basis congruence and Gram-residual estimates
are positive at all
\[
 8\cdot6^3=1728                                        \tag{83.4}
\]
product vertices.  Twice the smallest exact block floor is (83.3).
Separate matrix convexity and the three rational disk inclusions
fill the eight anisotropic physical boxes.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exact terminal chains and cap]
\label{lem:newv83-terminal-chains}
Every blocked third-axis column is covered continuously from depth
\(2t\) to \(\pi/4\) by its lower chart, bridge chart, and small
terminal chart.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The pairs \(22,23\) have lower third radius \(1/16\) from V81,
whereas \(32,33\) have lower third radius \(1/8\).  Since
\(\arctan x<x\) for \(x>0\),
\[
 h<1/8=1/16+1/16.                                    \tag{83.5}
\]
Thus every lower chart overlaps its bridge; the full lower radii
at \(32,33\) only enlarge that overlap.  The tangent subtraction
formula gives
\[
\begin{split}
 t-h
 &=2\arctan\!\left(
 \frac{1/8-1/16}{1+(1/8)(1/16)}\right)\\
 &=2\arctan(8/129)<16/129<1/8
   =1/16+1/16.
\end{split}                                            \tag{83.6}
\]
Hence every bridge overlaps its small terminal chart.
Finally, \(\arctan x>x-x^3/3\) at \(x=1/8\) and \(\pi<22/7\)
imply
\[
 0<\frac{\pi}{4}-3t
 <\frac{11}{14}-6\left(\frac18-\frac1{1536}\right)
 =\frac{71}{1792}<\frac1{16}.                         \tag{83.7}
\]
Every small terminal chart therefore reaches the quarter-turn cap.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The sixth direct-plus quarter cell]
\label{thm:newv83-sixth-cell}
On
\[
 {\cal C}^{---}_6:=
 \{b_\circ-(s_2,s_3,s_4):
      0\leq s_2,s_3,s_4\leq\pi/4\},                   \tag{83.8}
\]
the direct plus pencil obeys
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,+}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{83}I_{45},\qquad
 \theta_{83}:=
 \frac{23784808996629854813771747874389}
 {241558005344776833024000000000000000000}>0.         \tag{83.9}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv81-two-step-slab} covers
\(0\leq s_4\leq2t\).  For each of \(s_2,s_3\), choose a nearest
centre among \(0,t,2t,3t\).  In \([0,3t]\) its distance is at most
\(t/2<1/8\), while the terminal cap is controlled by (83.7), so
the full first and second radii always cover.

Of the sixteen \(n_4=3\) columns, \(003,013,023,033\) are the exact
shared-face charts of V48 and the eight centres in (82.4) are
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv82-eight-charts}.  Their full lower and upper
third radii cover the interval from \(2t\) to \(3t\), and their
upper radius covers the terminal cap.  The remaining four columns
are covered by Lemma~\ref{lem:newv83-terminal-chains} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv83-repair}.  Thus every point of (83.8) lies
in a certified physical chart.

The exact cover floors are the V81 slab floor, the four inherited
V48 face floors, the V82 eight-chart floor, and \(\beta_{83}\).
Exact cross-multiplication selects the V81 floor, which is (83.9).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Both signs on the fifth/sixth inversion pair]
\label{cor:newv83-paired-cells}

and on the V74 cell
\[
 {\cal C}^{+++}_5=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,s_3,s_4):
      0\leq s_2,s_3,s_4\leq\pi/4\},                   \tag{83.10}
\]
with common floor \(\theta_{83}\).

\begin{proof}

Theorem~\ref{thm:newv83-sixth-cell} supplies direct plus on

direct minus on \({\cal C}^{+++}_5\).  Hermitian order is preserved
by complex conjugation, and exact comparison leaves the common
floor (83.9).
\end{proof}

\section{V84: the seventh-orientation Boolean seed cube}
\label{sec:newv84-seventh-seed}

The sole untreated inversion pair has displacement signs
\((+-+)\) and \((-+-)\).  Begin with the direct plus atlas for
\[
 {\cal Q}^{+-+}_1:=
 \{b_\circ+(s_2,-s_3,s_4):0\leq s_2,s_3,s_4\leq t\},
 \qquad t=2\arctan(1/8).                               \tag{84.1}
\]
No coordinate-permutation or sign-change intertwiner is assumed.

\begin{theorem}[Exact Boolean-cube intersection ledger]
\label{thm:newv84-intersection}
Of the eight Boolean power centres, exactly
\[
 000,\ 001,\ 010,\ 011,\ 100,\ 101,\ 110              \tag{84.2}
\]
coincide with centres in the six completed sign atlases.  The centre
\(111\) is genuinely new.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Form each phase triple in \(\mathbb Q(i)^3\) with step signs
\((1,-1,1)\).  Compare it by exact numerator--denominator equality
with all \(6\cdot4^3\) power triples of the completed atlases.  The

\begin{theorem}[V85 companion decision]
\label{thm:newv85-audit-decision}
No theorem is imported from either companion checkpoint.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
SM V44 proves a graph-uniform Bessel edge moment and conditional
fluctuation smearing for its declared positive conformal model.  It
also isolates an uncontrolled conditional mean and explicitly leaves
joint continuum tightness, gauge scaling, and reflection positivity
open.  Those results neither identify the SM action with the present
Wilson/RPESM action nor supply a finite Wilson Loewner chart or a YM
continuum limit.

NS V26 treats pointwise rank-one norms of Oseen-kernel derivatives.
It contains no Wilson coefficient bank, reflection-positive gauge
measure, or continuum YM estimate.  Thus neither checkpoint discharges
an input of the present finite-atlas step or the later continuum
programme.
\end{proof}

\section{V95: companion audit and the eighth second quarter face}

\subsection{Mandatory five-version companion audit}

\begin{audit}[The active SM and NS checkpoints]
\label{aud:newv95-companions}
The newest active Standard-Model companion inspected was
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\path{Renewal_SM_Einstein_Continuum_Research_Notes_V78.tex},
\end{center}
with \(646030\) bytes, \(19771\) physical lines, and SHA--256
\begin{center}\scriptsize
\texttt{1D6496AFAF78EC273700CB0666B024ED6572834DF2062E065BA7244FA46AD93F}.
\end{center}
Its latest theorem is the exact conditional-response cancellation
for a quartic Higgs scalar law.  Its firewall leaves the required
global covariance norm, environmental susceptibility, gauge
cofinality, and continuum measure open.

The newest active Navier--Stokes companion inspected was
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\path{Renewal_NS_Stability_Research_Notes_V26.tex},
\end{center}
with \(278283\) bytes, \(5319\) physical lines, and SHA--256
\begin{center}\scriptsize
\texttt{82C887F8C2C69E4F28FAD7C3DC8DE5B9099CB5A4BF431EBA1FFF3E91935978AD}.
\end{center}
Its latest results compute pointwise rank-one norms of first and
second Oseen-kernel derivatives; its full stability and regularity
theorem remains open.
\end{audit}

\begin{theorem}[V95 no-import decision]
\label{thm:newv95-no-import}
Neither companion checkpoint supplies a theorem that closes a live
hypothesis of the eighth direct-plus Wilson-symbol atlas.  No
companion result is imported at V95.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
SM V78 concerns a conditional quartic scalar law and reformulates,
but does not prove, its global environment covariance estimate.  It
contains neither the direct Wilson pencil nor a full-radius chart
certificate or a transverse-torus cover.  NS V26 concerns Oseen
kernels for a parabolic fluid equation and has none of those
objects.  Conversely, neither companion proves gauge cofinality, a
reflection-positive continuum measure, or a Hamiltonian spectral
gap that could bypass the present finite-cutoff atlas.  Thus no
conclusion from either note matches the antecedent of a pending
Yang--Mills implication.
\end{proof}

\([0,t]\).  The companion audit supplies no shortcut.  The remaining
finite-cutoff atlas work is the third-coordinate extension; even its
completion would not prove the continuum or Hamiltonian steps.
\end{assessment}

\[

For every active momentum, every \(k_\perp\in{\cal T}_{\perp}\),
and each response sign \(\sigma\in\{+,-\}\),
\[
 P_{\mathrm W,\sigma}^{(4/3)}(k)
 \succeq\theta_{97}I_{45}.                           \tag{97.13}
\]

\begin{proof}
The seven previously closed direct-plus atlases cover seven sign

inversion permutes the eight sign cells, and complex conjugation
preserves Hermitian order and the exact floor, proving the
\(\sigma=-\) statement.
\end{proof}
