\refstepcounter{section}
\section*{V98: the first missing cofinal bridge}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{V98: the first missing cofinal bridge}
\label{sec:newv98-cofinal-bridge}

The finite atlas is complete, so another momentum patch would be
duplicative.  This version instead separates three quantities that
must not be conflated: the dimensionless Wilson-symbol floor
\(\theta_{97}\), a lattice-unit clustering exponent, and a physical
mass.  The separation identifies the first unproved implication in
the remaining dependency chain.

\subsection{Conversion from lattice units to physical units}

\begin{definition}[A cofinal clustering datum]
\label{def:newv98-cofinal-datum}
Let \(j\) range over a directed family of regulators with lattice
spacing \(a_j\downarrow0\) and volumes tending to \(\mathbb R^4\).
A cofinal clustering datum consists of positive numbers
\(C_j,\gamma_j\) such that, for a common class of local
gauge-invariant observables and every allowed integer time
separation \(n\),
\[
 \bigl|\langle A\,\tau_nB\rangle_j
       -\langle A\rangle_j\langle B\rangle_j\bigr|
 \leq C_j\|A\|\|B\|e^{-\gamma_j n}.                \tag{98.1}
\]
The exponent \(\gamma_j\) is dimensionless.  The corresponding
physical rate is
\[
 m_j:=\gamma_j/a_j.                                 \tag{98.2}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact scaling of a cofinal clustering exponent]
\label{thm:newv98-scaling}
Assume (98.1), \(\sup_j C_j<\infty\),
\(\sup_j\gamma_j<\infty\), and
\[
 m_*:=\liminf_j\frac{\gamma_j}{a_j}>0.              \tag{98.3}
\]
For every \(0<m<m_*\), the cofinal connected two-point functions,
evaluated at a fixed positive physical separation \(r\), obey for
all sufficiently advanced \(j\)
\[
 \bigl|\langle A\,\tau_{n_j}B\rangle_j
       -\langle A\rangle_j\langle B\rangle_j\bigr|
 \leq C\,\|A\|\|B\|e^{-mr},\qquad
 n_j:=\lfloor r/a_j\rfloor,                         \tag{98.4}
\]
with \(C\) independent of \(j\) and \(r\).  By contrast, if only
\(\gamma_j/a_j\to0\) is known, (98.1) alone yields no positive
cutoff-independent physical decay rate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Since \(n_j\geq r/a_j-1\),
\[
 e^{-\gamma_jn_j}
 \leq e^{\gamma_j}
      \exp\!\left(-\frac{\gamma_j}{a_j}r\right).
                                                               \tag{98.5}
\]
For \(m<m_*\), (98.3) gives
\(\gamma_j/a_j\geq m\) eventually.  Thus (98.1) and (98.5) imply
(98.4) with
\[
 C:=\bigl(\sup_jC_j\bigr)
       \exp\bigl(\sup_j\gamma_j\bigr).              \tag{98.6}
\]
If \(\gamma_j/a_j\to0\), the exponent delivered by the same
calculation tends to zero.  Hence that estimate, without additional
information, cannot furnish any fixed \(m>0\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What the scaling theorem does not assert]
\label{rem:newv98-scaling}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv98-scaling} is a conversion theorem.  It does
not construct the states in (98.1), prove their tightness, or
identify their limit.  Even (98.4) becomes a continuum statement
only after convergence of the relevant Schwinger functions has been
proved.
\end{remark}

\subsection{An exact logical countermodel}

\begin{countertheorem}[A fixed symbol floor does not determine a physical gap]
\label{ctr:newv98-symbol-not-gap}
There is no implication from a regulator-independent inequality of
the form
\[
 P_{j,\sigma}(k)\succeq\theta I_{45},
 \qquad \theta>0,                                    \tag{98.7}
\]
by itself to a positive lower bound on the physical transfer
Hamiltonian gap.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
For every \(a\in(0,1]\), take
\[
 P_{a,\sigma}(k):=\theta I_{45}
 \quad\text{for every }k,\sigma,                     \tag{98.8}
\]
so (98.7) holds uniformly on any torus.  Independently let
\(\mathcal H_a=\mathbb C^2\), \(\Omega=(1,0)\), and let the
dimensionless transfer Hamiltonian and transfer operator be
\[
 \widehat H_a=
 \begin{pmatrix}0&0\\0&a^2\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad T_a=e^{-\widehat H_a}.                       \tag{98.9}
\]
With
\[
 A=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\1&0\end{pmatrix},
\]
one has \(\langle\Omega,A\Omega\rangle=0\) and, exactly,
\[
 \langle\Omega,A T_a^n A\Omega\rangle=e^{-a^2n}.
                                                               \tag{98.10}
\]
The physical Hamiltonian is
\[
 H_a^{\rm phys}=a^{-1}\widehat H_a
 =\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\0&a\end{pmatrix},             \tag{98.11}
\]
whose gap is \(a\to0\).  Thus the same uniform symbol statement
(98.7) coexists with transfer gaps having no positive physical
lower limit.  The construction is a logical countermodel, not a
Yang--Mills counterexample: it proves that an additional theorem
linking the particular symbol to the particular transfer system is
indispensable.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Necessary content of the missing bridge]
\label{cor:newv98-required-bridge}
A successful continuation from V97 must do more than preserve
\(\theta_{97}\).  On one common regulator family it must derive a
volume-uniform probabilistic or transfer estimate whose
lattice-unit exponent \(\gamma_j\) satisfies
\[
 \liminf_j\gamma_j/a_j>0,                            \tag{98.12}
\]
or prove an equivalent scale-compatible spectral estimate.  It
must also provide the tightness and positivity needed to pass that
estimate to the limiting state.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The sufficiency of (98.12) for converting an already established
clustering estimate is Theorem~\ref{thm:newv98-scaling}.  The
failure of the Wilson-symbol floor alone to supply (98.12) is
Countertheorem~\ref{ctr:newv98-symbol-not-gap}.  Passage to the
limit is logically separate by
Remark~\ref{rem:newv98-scaling}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The dependency ledger after the atlas}

\begin{theorem}[The first unproved implication]
\label{thm:newv98-first-gap}
In the proof chain recorded in
Assessment~\ref{ass:newv1-dependency}, the first unproved implication
after V97 is
\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
 &\text{fixed-fibre full-torus Wilson-symbol positivity}\\
 &\not\Longrightarrow
 \text{a volume-uniform, scale-compatible}\\
 &\hspace{18mm}\text{contraction or clustering estimate}\\
 &\hspace{18mm}\text{on a cofinal regulator family}.
\end{split}
\tag{98.13}
\end{equation}
Consequently continuum measure construction,
OS reconstruction, and the Hamiltonian spectral
argument are downstream gates, not the immediate next premise.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv97-full-torus} quantifies over the finite
momentum fibre and the two response signs.  Its conclusion contains
no lattice-spacing index \(a_j\), volume index, running coupling,
common gauge-invariant probability measure, clustering exponent, or
transfer operator.  Records~\ref{rec:newv1-constructive} and
\ref{rec:newv1-fibre} provide many finite-regulator identities, but
Assessment~\ref{ass:newv1-dependency} explicitly leaves their
simultaneous realization on one volume-uniform contracting
regulator open.  No intervening theorem supplies a map
\[
 \theta_{97}\longmapsto\gamma_j
 \quad\text{with}\quad
 \liminf_j\gamma_j/a_j>0.                            \tag{98.14}
\]
Countertheorem~\ref{ctr:newv98-symbol-not-gap} shows that this
missing map cannot follow from the type of inequality proved in V97
alone.  Therefore the first missing implication is exactly
(98.13).  Each later gate requires a cofinal state or estimate as an
antecedent, so invoking it first would assume the missing premise.
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[Upstream correction after the atlas]
\label{ass:newv98}
V98 does not weaken the finite-cutoff theorem.  It closes a logical
ambiguity: the rational floor \(\theta_{97}\) is neither a
lattice-unit clustering exponent nor a physical mass.  The live
bottleneck is now the common-regulator cofinal bridge, specifically
a scale-compatible volume-uniform contraction from which (98.1) and
(98.12), or an equivalent transfer estimate, can be derived.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V99 regulator ledger]
\label{dec:newv98-next}
Return to the archived local-vacuum, good/bad polymer, stable-slice,
marginal-flow, toron, and determinant constructions.  Place their
hypotheses on one explicit regulator tuple
\[
 (a,L,\beta(a),\text{gauge fixing},\text{sector data}),          \tag{98.15}
\]
and make a compatibility ledger listing every constant with its
\(a\)- and \(L\)-dependence.  Identify the earliest row that cannot
be populated simultaneously.  If it is the owned-activity or
stable-slice contraction margin, derive its exact scale demand
before attempting any continuum compactness or reconstruction
argument.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V98]
\label{fire:newv98-final}
V98 proves the physical-unit scaling lemma, an exact logical
countermodel to direct symbol-to-gap inference, and the location of
the first missing cofinal bridge.  It does not construct the common
regulator family, prove a uniform RG contraction, build a continuum
measure, establish reflection positivity or clustering in the
limit, reconstruct the Hamiltonian, or prove the Yang--Mills mass
gap.
\end{firewall}

\refstepcounter{section}
\section*{V99: common-regulator compatibility and the tree-constant scale demand}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{V99: common-regulator compatibility and the tree-constant scale demand}
\label{sec:newv99-compatibility}

V98 deliberately stated the missing cofinal bridge at a high level.
The older archives contain more of that bridge than the compressed
statement displayed.  This version therefore performs a
source-by-source compatibility audit before attempting a new
estimate.  It distinguishes a theorem proved on one declared local
regulator from the stronger assertion that all such theorems hold
simultaneously, with uniform constants, along one cofinal regulator
family.

\subsection{Exact archive sources and the common tuple}

\begin{record}[Sources used in the compatibility audit]

\begin{definition}[Common-regulator realization]
\label{def:newv99-common-regulator}
A common-regulator realization is a directed family
\[
 {\mathfrak R}_j=
 (a_j,L_j,\beta_j,b,\mathfrak g_j,\mathfrak s_j,
  Q_j,C_j,\mathcal N_j),                             \tag{99.2}
\]
where \(a_j\downarrow0\), the physical volumes exhaust
\(\mathbb R^4\), \(\beta_j=\beta(a_j)\) is the bare inverse
coupling, \(b>1\) is the block factor, \(\mathfrak g_j\) denotes the
gauge/chart/filling choices, \(\mathfrak s_j\) the sector data,
\(Q_j\) the retained map, \(C_j\) the reduced covariance, and
\(\mathcal N_j\) the collection of polymer and source norms.
It \emph{populates} an archived row only when every hypothesis of
that row holds on this same tuple and every constant advertised as
uniform is independent of \(L_j\) and of all undeclared regulator
choices.  Dependence on \(a_j\) must be displayed either directly
or through \(\beta_j\).
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Compatibility ledger after V98]
\label{prop:newv99-ledger}
The verified archive record has the following ordered status.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=3.5em]
\item The full reduced transverse-torus Wilson-symbol floor for
      both response signs is closed at fixed cutoff by
      Theorem~\ref{thm:newv97-full-torus}.
\item The local flat Gaussian retained/detail split, conditional
      centering, and principal calibrated stable multiplier
      \(b^{-2}\) are closed in \texttt{V100\_f7}.
\item The exact soft/far-bad split and its local primitive
      estimates are closed in \texttt{V65\_f8}; in particular its
      far-bad quantity is
      \[
       q_b(\beta)=C_-^{-45}\left(\frac{\beta}{120}\right)^{180}
       \exp\{-\beta^{1/5}/120\}.                    \tag{99.3}
      \]
\item The abstract positive-Gaussian forest identity and labelled
      tree summation are available, but the concrete
      Yang--Mills fixed-tree inequality
      \[
       \|{\cal A}_T(F_1,\ldots,F_n)\|_{\rm weak}
       \le z_\beta^n K_T(\beta,a,L)^{\,n-1}          \tag{99.4}
      \]
      for the Wilson remainder, soft selector, current, and three
      score marks is open.
\item Consequently the numerical four-mark branch and the complete
      support-resolved nonlinear margin
      \(\eta_j<1-b^{-2}\) remain conditional.
\item Independently, the regulator-compatible running marginal
      path \(\beta(a)\), including its accumulated scale ledger,
      remains open.
\item A common volume-uniform RG trajectory, its limiting
      reflection-positive state, uniform physical clustering, and
      the Hamiltonian gap are downstream and open.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Item (i) is exactly the scope and firewall of V97.  The
calibrated-detail theorem and GCDC ledger at the end of
\texttt{V100\_f7} prove the local forms of GCDC
\textup{(G1)--(G5),(G7)} and explicitly leave
\textup{(G6),(G8),(G9)} open.  Equations (64.12), (64.16), and the
V65 assessment in \texttt{V65\_f8} prove item (iii).  That same
assessment and its claim firewall state that (99.4), which is
equation (65.4) there, has not been proved for the concrete vertex
bank.  Its theorem makes the later tree sum conditional on
\(q_T=e z_\beta K_T<1\), giving (iv)--(v).  GCDC (G9) and both
archives' final firewalls give (vi)--(vii).  None of these source
statements identifies all of its local choices with a single tuple
(99.2), so the audit does not silently assert such an
identification.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Refinement of the V98 bottleneck]
\label{cor:newv99-refinement}
Within the latest archived good/bad route, the earliest explicitly
unpaid analytic row is (99.4), not the finite Wilson-symbol atlas.
Proving (99.4) uniformly would close only that branch's local
four-mark gate; it would not by itself populate the independent
running-path row or imply (98.12).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The first assertion is the order of items (iii)--(v) in
Proposition~\ref{prop:newv99-ledger}.  The hypotheses and conclusion
of (99.4) contain no lattice clustering exponent \(\gamma_j\), no
relation between \(\beta_j\) and \(a_j\), and no accumulated
marginal flow.  Hence they cannot imply either GCDC (G9) or the
positive lower limit in (98.12) without another theorem.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The exact scale demand on the fixed-tree constant}

Set
\[
 c_*:=7680\pi^3,\qquad
 z_\beta\le C_{\rm an}\{e^{c_*\beta^{-1/5}}-1\}.     \tag{99.5}
\]
This is the explicit V65 local-vertex bound.  The following result
allows the tree constant to depend on the bare scale instead of
assuming, as the archived corollary did, that it is fixed.

\begin{theorem}[Subcritical tree-growth criterion]
\label{thm:newv99-tree-growth}
Assume that (99.4) holds on one common-regulator family, with
\(C_{\rm an},C_{\rm own},C_K<\infty\) independent of \(a,L\), and
\[
 K_T(\beta,a,L)\le C_K\beta^p.                      \tag{99.6}
\]
Write
\[
 A:=eC_{\rm an}c_*,\qquad
 \delta:=\frac15-p.                                 \tag{99.7}
\]
If \(p<1/5\), then for
\[
 \beta\ge\beta_T:=
 \max\left\{1,c_*^5,
       (2eA C_K)^{1/\delta}\right\}                 \tag{99.8}
\]
one has
\[
 q_T:=e z_\beta K_T\le\frac12                       \tag{99.9}
\]
and the V65 owned good activity satisfies the explicit bound
\[
 u_g(\beta)\le
 C_{\rm own}\{A+2e^2A^2C_K\}\,\beta^{-1/5}.         \tag{99.10}
\]
If \(p=1/5\), the same conclusion, with \(1/2\) replaced by
\[
 Q:=eAC_K<1,                                        \tag{99.11}
\]
holds after replacing the coefficient in (99.10) by
\[
 C_{\rm own}\left\{
 A+\frac{e^2A^2C_K}{1-Q}\right\}.                   \tag{99.12}
\]
Thus the concrete estimate must lose strictly less than
\(\beta^{1/5}\), or meet the displayed critical constant, for this
majorant to close uniformly by the present argument.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For \(\beta\ge c_*^5\), put
\(x=c_*\beta^{-1/5}\le1\).  The integral identity
\[
 e^x-1=\int_0^x e^t\,dt
\]
gives \(x\le e^x-1\le ex\).  Hence (99.5) implies
\[
 z_\beta\le A\beta^{-1/5}.                          \tag{99.13}
\]
Combining (99.6) and (99.13),
\[
 q_T\le eAC_K\beta^{p-1/5}
      =eAC_K\beta^{-\delta}.                        \tag{99.14}
\]
When \(\delta>0\), the final term in the maximum (99.8) makes
(99.14) at most \(1/2\).  The V65 tree sum is then defined, and its
equation (65.8) gives
\begin{equation*}
\tag{99.15}
\begin{split}
 u_g(\beta)
 &\le C_{\rm own}\left\{
 z_\beta+\frac{e^2z_\beta^2K_T}{1-q_T}\right\}\\
 &\le C_{\rm own}\left\{
 A\beta^{-1/5}
 +2e^2A^2C_K\beta^{p-2/5}\right\}.
\end{split}
\end{equation*}
Since \(p<1/5\) and \(\beta\ge1\),
\(\beta^{p-2/5}\le\beta^{-1/5}\), proving (99.10).

When \(p=1/5\), (99.14) is bounded by \(Q\).  Under (99.11), use
\((1-q_T)^{-1}\le(1-Q)^{-1}\) in the first line of
(99.15).  Now \(\beta^{p-2/5}=\beta^{-1/5}\), which proves
(99.12).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Conditional closure of the archived numerical gate]
\label{cor:newv99-numerical-gate}
Under either branch of
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv99-tree-growth}, for every fixed
\(\mu_*>0\) there is a finite \(\beta_0\) such that
\[
 6401e^{1+\mu_*}\{q_b(\beta)+u_g(\beta)\}\le1
 \quad(\beta\ge\beta_0).                            \tag{99.16}
\]
The threshold is independent of \(a,L\) provided all constants in
the theorem are.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The activity bound (99.10) or (99.12) tends to zero.  Also
\[
 \log q_b(\beta)=
 -45\log C_-+180\log(\beta/120)-\beta^{1/5}/120,     \tag{99.17}
\]
and \(\log\beta=o(\beta^{1/5})\); therefore
\(q_b(\beta)\to0\).  The expression on the left of (99.16) tends
to zero.  The definition of convergence supplies \(\beta_0\).
Independence from \(a,L\) follows because the majorants contain
them only through constants assumed uniform and through \(\beta\).
\end{proof}

\begin{countertheorem}[Hidden tree growth]
\label{ctr:newv99-hidden-growth}
The statement \(z_\beta=O(\beta^{-1/5})\) alone does not imply
\(q_T<1\) on a cofinal family if the dependence of \(K_T\) on
\(\beta,a,L\) is suppressed.
\end{countertheorem}

\begin{proof}
This is an exact numerical nonimplication.  Take
\[
 z_\beta=\frac12C_{\rm an}c_*\beta^{-1/5},
 \qquad K_T=\beta^p.                                \tag{99.18}
\]
Because \(e^x-1\ge x\), (99.5) holds.  But
\[
 q_T=\frac e2C_{\rm an}c_*\beta^{p-1/5}.            \tag{99.19}
\]
For \(p>1/5\) this diverges, and for \(p=1/5\) it need not be less
than one.  The assignment is not asserted to be the Yang--Mills
constant; it proves that a claim based only on the displayed
vertex smallness is logically incomplete unless the tree-constant
dependence is exposed.
\end{proof}

\subsection{What this closes and what remains orthogonal}

\begin{proposition}[Local weak coupling is not yet physical scaling]
\label{prop:newv99-orthogonal}
Even a uniform proof of (99.16) along some
\(\beta_j\to\infty\) would not establish a positive physical mass.
It would supply a source-analytic local good/bad branch at the
declared regulators.  To reach Theorem~\ref{thm:newv98-scaling},
one must still prove on the same family:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)},leftmargin=3.2em]
\item the localization and complete nonlinear correction needed
      for the genuine stable margin;
\item a regulator-compatible marginal trajectory
      \(\beta_j=\beta(a_j)\);
\item a volume-uniform multiscale contraction or transfer estimate
      producing \(\gamma_j\); and
\item \(\liminf_j\gamma_j/a_j>0\), followed by convergence and
      positivity of the limiting state.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The conclusion (99.16) is a dimensionless small-activity
inequality.  Neither side contains \(a_j\) or a clustering exponent.
Corollary~\ref{cor:newv99-refinement} shows that the marginal path is
an independent row, while Theorem~\ref{thm:newv98-scaling} requires
the ratio \(\gamma_j/a_j\).  Therefore (a)--(d) are additional
premises and cannot be obtained by relabelling the activity
constant as a mass.
\end{proof}

\begin{assessment}[Progress after V99]
\label{ass:newv99}
V99 replaces the coarse post-atlas bottleneck by a sourced
compatibility ledger.  It identifies the concrete all-tree
strong-to-weak estimate (99.4) as the first unpaid row in the
latest good/bad branch and proves the exact scale demand under which
that row would close the archived four-mark gate: subcritical loss
\(K_T=O(\beta^p)\) with \(p<1/5\), or the displayed critical
constant at \(p=1/5\).  The proof also makes volume and lattice
uniformity nonoptional.  No common-regulator realization has yet
been constructed.
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[V100 fixed-tree target and archive boundary]
\label{dec:newv99-next}
V100 should first perform the compulsory Standard-Model--Einstein
and Navier--Stokes audit.  It should then define, without an
abstract placeholder, the strong and weak analytic polymer norms
for the four concrete Yang--Mills owner types in (99.4).  The target
is a global tree-convolution estimate which spends the
strong-to-weak radius once and proves an \(a,L\)-uniform bound with
\(p<1/5\), or exposes the first vertex or covariance edge that
prevents it.  On completion V100 must be frozen as
\texttt{V100\_f10} and the next compact active lineage restarted at
contextual V1, in accordance with the archive rule.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary after V99]
\label{fire:newv99-final}
V99 proves a compatibility ledger and a conditional asymptotic
criterion for the archived tree majorant.  It does not prove the
concrete fixed-tree estimate (99.4), the full nonlinear stable
margin, the nonperturbative running coupling, a cofinal RG
trajectory, regulator removal, Osterwalder--Schrader reconstruction,
uniform physical clustering, or the Yang--Mills mass gap.
\end{firewall}

\refstepcounter{section}
\section*{V100: archive correction and the global normalized covariance margin}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{V100: archive correction and the global normalized covariance margin}
\label{sec:newv100-correction}

The V99 audit stopped at the V65 endpoint of the eighth archive and
therefore missed a later correction contained near the beginning of
\texttt{V100\_f9}.  That omission changes the next acquisition.
The degree-free fixed-tree estimate (99.4) is not an open target:
it was superseded by a proved degree-faithful theorem.  This V100
first corrects the record, then extracts a new all-torus normalized
covariance margin from the V97 atlas.

\subsection{Compulsory V100 companion audit}

\begin{audit}[V100 companion provenance]
\label{audit:newv100-companions}
The active companion sources inspected read-only at this checkpoint
were
\[
\begin{array}{c|r|r}
\text{source}&\text{bytes}&\text{lines}\\ \hline
\text{SM--Einstein V96}&777314&23541\\
\text{Navier--Stokes V26}&278283&5319
\end{array}                                                   \tag{100.1}
\]
with respective SHA--256 hashes
\begin{center}\scriptsize
\texttt{91A272E2A240AE6B5D339A08017418FCFE5A705F47AC74D3444270C4FBDE4669},\\
\texttt{82C887F8C2C69E4F28FAD7C3DC8DE5B9099CB5A4BF431EBA1FFF3E91935978AD}.
\end{center}

SM V96 corrects a central-sector form estimate which had been used
outside its support.  Its transferable lesson is exact: a
configuration-space Dirichlet floor cannot be substituted for a
global resolvent floor.  It also proves a volume-independent
response-source estimate by duality in one global weighted norm,
instead of summing pointwise spatial envelopes.  The SM operator,
weights, and constants are not Yang--Mills data and are not
imported.

NS V26 computes rank-one Oseen-kernel derivative norms.  It has no
compact gauge fibre, Haar quotient, heat-kernel forest, or marginal
determinant anomaly.  No NS constant or theorem enters the present
calculation.
\end{audit}

\begin{decision}[Audit consequence]
\label{dec:newv100-audit}
The next Yang--Mills response estimate must keep the complete
periodic Ward-recombined scalar inside one global trace or dual norm.
A local chart floor may be used only on its proved support.  This
agrees with the archived compact edge card and gives no permission
to compare its Dirichlet or chart-local inverse with the full
compact inverse.
\end{decision}

\subsection{Correction of the V99 forest ledger}

\begin{proposition}[The degree-free target in V99 is superseded]
\label{prop:newv100-v99-erratum}
Items (iv)--(v) of Proposition~\ref{prop:newv99-ledger},
Corollary~\ref{cor:newv99-refinement}, and
Decision~\ref{dec:newv99-next} are superseded as statements of the
current acquisition order.

The later \texttt{V100\_f9} archive proves, for a labelled tree
\(T\) on \(n\) local vertices,
\[
 \|{\cal A}_T(F_1,\ldots,F_n)\|_{\mu,r}
 \le z^nK_\mu^{\,n-1}\prod_{i=1}^n d_T(i)!,
 \qquad
 K_\mu=\frac{h_*G_\mu}{(R-r)^2},                    \tag{100.2}
\]
and the exact identity
\[
 \sum_{T\ {\rm tree\ on}\ [n]}
 \prod_{i=1}^n d_T(i)!
 =(n-2)!\binom{3n-3}{n-2}.                         \tag{100.3}
\]
Consequently its connected sum converges when
\[
                         8zK_\mu<1.                \tag{100.4}
\]
For the invariant compact heat-kernel forest it further proves
\[
 K_{\rm HK}(\mu;R,r)
 =\frac{8h_*G_\mu}{(R-r)^2},                        \tag{100.5}
\]
uniformly in the heat-kernel scale and in the volume.  Compact
V5--V8 of the same archive then close the one-scale Morse
good/bad all-colour kernel gate at sufficiently large \(\beta\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The one-time Taylor--Wiener radius lemma in compact V1 gives
\[
 \|\partial^\nu F\|_r
 \le |\nu|!(R-r)^{-|\nu|}\|F\|_R.                  \tag{100.6}
\]
At vertex \(i\), precisely \(d_T(i)\) edge derivatives land, so
the factorial in (100.2) is unavoidable.  Rooted leaf removal
spends \(h_*G_\mu\) once per edge and the radius once at each
endpoint, proving (100.2).  Prüfer words give (100.3), and the
bound
\(\binom{3n-3}{n-2}\le2^{3n-3}\) gives (100.4).

Compact V3 converts left to right invariant derivatives before
commuting them through the heat semigroup.  The two adjoint
conversions cost at most \(8\), giving (100.5), with no repeated
radius expenditure or time-ordering factorial.  The exact Morse
chart, cutoff-free gluing, nonduplicating owner product, and
all-colour schedule are the subsequent V5--V8 theorems.  These are
all earlier parts of the exact source fingerprinted in
Record~\ref{rec:newv99-sources}; none was present at the V65
endpoint on which V99 based its target.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Logical status of the V99 conditional theorem]
\label{rem:newv100-v99-logic}
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv99-tree-growth} is a valid implication from
its stronger degree-free assumption.  It is not a valid description
of the actual Yang--Mills forest card, because an ordinary analytic
norm produces the factorials in (100.2).  Equations
(99.9)--(99.16) are therefore not used below.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Correct common-regulator scale loss for the forest}

\begin{theorem}[Degree-faithful scale-loss criterion]
\label{thm:newv100-degree-scale}
Suppose the compact heat-kernel vertex bank on a common regulator
family satisfies
\[
 z_\beta\le C_{\rm an}
 \{e^{c_*\beta^{-1/5}}-1\},\qquad c_*=7680\pi^3,      \tag{100.7}
\]
and
\[
 K_{\rm HK}(\beta,a,L)\le C_K\beta^p                 \tag{100.8}
\]
with \(C_{\rm an},C_K\) independent of \(a,L\).  Put
\[
 A=eC_{\rm an}c_*,\qquad \delta=\frac15-p.            \tag{100.9}
\]
If \(p<1/5\), then
\[
 \beta\ge
 \max\{1,c_*^5,(16AC_K)^{1/\delta}\}                 \tag{100.10}
\]
implies
\[
 8z_\beta K_{\rm HK}\le\frac12                       \tag{100.11}
\]
and
\[
 u_{\rm good}(\beta)
 \le C_{\rm own}
 \{A+8A^2C_K\}\beta^{-1/5}.                           \tag{100.12}
\]
If \(p=1/5\), the corresponding sufficient critical condition is
\[
 Q:=8AC_K<1,                                          \tag{100.13}
\]
and (100.12) holds with coefficient
\[
 C_{\rm own}\left\{
 A+\frac{4A^2C_K}{1-Q}\right\}.                       \tag{100.14}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For \(\beta\ge c_*^5\),
\[
 e^{c_*\beta^{-1/5}}-1
 \le ec_*\beta^{-1/5},
\]
so \(z_\beta\le A\beta^{-1/5}\).  Hence
\[
 8z_\beta K_{\rm HK}
 \le8AC_K\beta^{p-1/5}.                              \tag{100.15}
\]
When \(p<1/5\), (100.10) makes the right side at most
\(1/2\).  Insert this and (100.8) into the proved
degree-faithful connected bound
\[
 u_{\rm good}\le C_{\rm own}
 \left\{z_\beta+
 \frac{4z_\beta^2K_{\rm HK}}
      {1-8z_\beta K_{\rm HK}}\right\}.                \tag{100.16}
\]
It gives
\[
 u_{\rm good}\le C_{\rm own}
 \{A\beta^{-1/5}
   +8A^2C_K\beta^{p-2/5}\}.
\]
Because \(p<1/5\) and \(\beta\ge1\), the second power is at most
\(\beta^{-1/5}\), proving (100.12).

For \(p=1/5\), (100.15) is bounded by \(Q\).  Use
\((1-8z_\beta K_{\rm HK})^{-1}\le(1-Q)^{-1}\) in
(100.16); both terms then have order \(\beta^{-1/5}\), with the
coefficient (100.14).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[What remains of the common-regulator forest row]
\label{cor:newv100-forest-row}
The one-scale forest combinatorics and analytic-radius problem are
closed by (100.2)--(100.5).  Along a varying regulator family, the
remaining forest compatibility check is the explicit inequality
\[
 \frac{8h_*G_\mu(\beta,a,L)}{(R-r)^2}
 =O(\beta^p),\qquad p<\frac15,                       \tag{100.17}
\]
or the critical alternative (100.13).  Under fixed
\(h_*,G_\mu,R-r>0\), this row has \(p=0\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Substitute (100.5) into
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv100-degree-scale}.  The last assertion is
immediate.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A new consequence of the completed V97 atlas}

Use the exact finite Laurent bank
\[
 H_{\rm W}(k)=
 \sum_{\alpha\in{\cal S}_{\rm W}}
 G_{{\rm W},\alpha}e^{i\alpha\cdot k}
\]
and define its finite coefficient ceiling
\[
 \overline M_{\rm W}:=
 \sum_{\alpha\in{\cal S}_{\rm W}}
 \|G_{{\rm W},\alpha}\|_{\rm F}.                     \tag{100.18}
\]
Then \(H_{\rm W}(k)\preceq\overline M_{\rm W}I\) on the
torus.  Retain the exact V97 floor
\[
 \theta_{97}=
 \frac{23784808996629854813771747874389}
 {241558005344776833024000000000000000000}.          \tag{100.19}
\]

\begin{theorem}[Global normalized Wilson covariance-derivative margin]
\label{thm:newv100-normalized-margin}
For every active momentum, every transverse momentum in
\({\cal T}_\perp\), and both signs,
\[
 2H_{\rm W}\pm{\boldsymbol\nabla}_1H_{\rm W}
 \succeq
 \left(\frac{49}{1875}+\theta_{97}\right)I_{45}.
                                                               \tag{100.20}
\]
Moreover, with
\[
 T_1(k):=
 H_{\rm W}(k)^{-1/2}
 ({\boldsymbol\nabla}_1H_{\rm W})(k)
 H_{\rm W}(k)^{-1/2},
\]
one has the all-torus strict bound
\[
 \boxed{
 \|T_1(k)\|_{\rm op}
 \le\kappa_{\rm W}:=
 \frac43-\frac{\theta_{97}}{\overline M_{\rm W}}
 <\frac43.}                                           \tag{100.21}
\]
If \(G_{\rm W}=H_{\rm W}^{-1}\), then
\[
 \left\|
 H_{\rm W}^{1/2}
 ({\boldsymbol\nabla}_1G_{\rm W})
 H_{\rm W}^{1/2}
 \right\|_{\rm op}
 \le\kappa_{\rm W}.                                  \tag{100.22}
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The global Hessian floor in (1.2) and
Theorem~\ref{thm:newv97-full-torus} give
\[
\begin{split}
 2H_{\rm W}\pm{\boldsymbol\nabla}_1H_{\rm W}
 &=
 \frac23H_{\rm W}
 +\left(\frac43H_{\rm W}
 \pm{\boldsymbol\nabla}_1H_{\rm W}\right)\\
 &\succeq
 \left(\frac23\frac{49}{1250}+\theta_{97}\right)I,
\end{split}
\]
which is (100.20).

The triangle inequality in the exact Laurent expansion gives
\(\|H_{\rm W}(k)\|_{\rm op}\le\overline M_{\rm W}\), so
\[
 H_{\rm W}(k)^{-1}\succeq
 \overline M_{\rm W}^{-1}I.                         \tag{100.23}
\]
Congruence of the two V97 inequalities by
\(H_{\rm W}^{-1/2}\) yields
\[
 \frac43I\pm T_1
 \succeq\theta_{97}H_{\rm W}^{-1}
 \succeq\frac{\theta_{97}}{\overline M_{\rm W}}I.     \tag{100.24}
\]
Since \(T_1\) is Hermitian, every eigenvalue lies in
\[
 \left[-\frac43+
 \frac{\theta_{97}}{\overline M_{\rm W}},
 \frac43-\frac{\theta_{97}}{\overline M_{\rm W}}\right],
\]
proving (100.21).  Positivity of
\(\theta_{97}\) gives strictness.

Finally apply the covariant derivation to
\(H_{\rm W}G_{\rm W}=I\):
\[
 {\boldsymbol\nabla}_1G_{\rm W}
 =-G_{\rm W}({\boldsymbol\nabla}_1H_{\rm W})G_{\rm W}.
                                                               \tag{100.25}
\]
Congruence by \(H_{\rm W}^{1/2}\) turns its right side into
\(-T_1\), proving (100.22).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the new margin]
\label{rem:newv100-margin-scope}
Equation (100.22) is the first normalized first-covariance-
derivative ceiling obtained from the completed open-set atlas,
rather than from a finite grid or a coarse absolute Laurent stock.
It concerns the declared fixed-cutoff Wilson fibre and the active
coordinate.  It is not a bound for a full regulator-dependent
Schwinger function.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Corrected dependency order at the archive boundary}

\begin{proposition}[The next unpaid marginal row]
\label{prop:newv100-next-row}
After the f9 correction and Theorem
\ref{thm:newv100-normalized-margin}, the earliest named unpaid row
on the archived marginal route is not a fixed-tree estimate.  It is
the actual compact edge card: the full periodic Ward-recombined
response scalar must be shown to satisfy one of
\[
\begin{gathered}
 e_j^{\rm edge}\longrightarrow0,\\
 \frac1N\sum_{j<N}e_j^{\rm edge}\longrightarrow0,\\
 e_j^{\rm edge}=d_{j+1}-d_j+\varepsilon_j,\quad
 d_N=o(N),\quad
 \frac1N\sum_{j<N}\varepsilon_j\longrightarrow0.
\end{gathered}                                             \tag{100.26}
\]
That result, together with a sublinear propagated reset/sector
boundary, is what would populate SCIC and ADMC and hence the
asymptotically free marginal trajectory.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Compact V1--V8 of \texttt{V100\_f9} prove the degree-faithful
forest, Morse gluing, unique owner product, and one-scale all-colour
gate.  Compact V10 proves the one-step anisotropy contraction, V11
the sectorwise zero-theta transport, and V12--V14 reduce the
marginal recursion to ADMC.  Compact V15 reduces the Gaussian part
of ADMC to SCIC.  Compact V16 proves the low-window implication and
lists exactly the three edge alternatives in (100.26).  The later
finite-fibre programme constructs the actual response oracle; V97
and Theorem~\ref{thm:newv100-normalized-margin} now provide the
missing full-torus first normalized covariance derivative for its
Wilson Hessian.  No archived theorem evaluates the complete
Ward-recombined edge mean or proves its Cesaro or coboundary form.
Therefore (100.26), not (99.4), is the next named marginal row.
\end{proof}

\begin{firewall}[The first derivative does not evaluate the anomaly]
\label{fire:newv100-no-anomaly}
The complete compact response contains the tadpole, bubble, Haar,
router, stationary-centre, toron, and overlap terms before the two
external derivatives are taken.  Bound (100.22) controls one
normalized covariance derivative.  It neither constructs the
complete scalar integrand nor proves that its edge mean vanishes,
is Cesaro-small, or is a scale coboundary.  Calling (100.22) the
Yang--Mills beta coefficient would discard precisely the trace
cancellations and boundary ownership that SCIC requires.
\end{firewall}

\begin{assessment}[Progress at V100]
\label{ass:newv100}
V100 performs the compulsory companion audit and repairs a genuine
archive-reading error in V99.  The ordinary analytic fixed-tree
card is not degree-free; its correct degree-weighted version and
compact heat-kernel implementation were already proved in f9.
V100 retains the useful scale question by proving the corrected
\(8zK_{\rm HK}\) criterion.  It then propagates the completed V97
atlas to the strict all-torus normalized covariance-derivative
margin (100.21)--(100.22) and relocates the live marginal
bottleneck to the complete compact edge scalar (100.26).
\end{assessment}

\begin{decision}[Post-archive continuation]
\label{dec:newv100-next}
Freeze this complete lineage as \texttt{V100\_f10} and begin a
contextual V1.  The new compact note should place the f9 forest
correction and the V100 covariance margin in its front ledger, then
attack (100.26) at the level of the complete periodic trace scalar.
Following the SM V96 audit lesson, it should use one global
normalized trace or dual norm and must not infer a full inverse from
a chart-local or Dirichlet floor.  If a sign/absolute-value estimate
loses the Ward cancellation, go upstream to the exact typed trace
before estimating.
\end{decision}

\begin{firewall}[Claim boundary at the tenth archive]
\label{fire:newv100-final}
V100 does not evaluate the compact edge anomaly, prove SCIC or
ADMC, construct the full common-regulator RG orbit, prove sector
tightness, remove the regulators, construct a reflection-positive
continuum measure, establish uniform physical clustering, reconstruct
the Hamiltonian, or prove the Yang--Mills mass gap.
\end{firewall}
